# koan-kit

This file is koan-kit's guide followed by its API reference, as one plain document for a reader that takes a single file. The same text is published at https://koan.rocks/docs/koan-kit/ and https://koan.rocks/docs/koan-kit-api/.

# koan-kit

koan-kit is the design system the kōan GUI app is built from: a theme and a set of components for [GPUI](https://www.gpui.rs), the UI framework from the Zed editor, taken from kōan's fork of it. It runs on macOS, Linux and iOS. It knows nothing of music or of kōan's engine, so an app of any kind can be built on it.

This page introduces the kit and shows its intended use. The [API reference](../reference/koan-kit-api.md) lists every public item with its documentation, generated from the source. The guide and the reference are also published together as one plain-text file, [koan.rocks/koan-kit.txt](https://koan.rocks/koan-kit.txt), for a reader such as a language model that takes a single document.

## What it provides

GPUI gives a page `div()`s, and each one accepts any padding, size and colour. A page assembled from those choices drifts from every other page. koan-kit puts containers back between the page and GPUI, so the page states what it shows and the kit decides the rest:

- **Containers that own their layout.** `Page` and `PageHeader`, `Toolbar`, `Form` with its sections and rows, `TableRow` and the lists built from it, `Collection`, `SplitView`, `Inspector` and sheets each own their margins, gaps, row heights and type. A page cannot set them, so pages built from the kit look the same.
- **Controls with their states.** Buttons, toggles, segmented controls, sliders, steppers, text and filter fields, menus, pickers and badges, each with hover, press, focus, disabled and selected states, keyboard access and an accessible name.
- **Tokens resolved per window.** `Look::of(window, cx)` gives the palette, type roles, spacing scale and motion for the window's appearance, text size and accent. Components take semantic parameters (a `Tone`, a `TypeRole`, a `ButtonKind`) and never a colour or a size in points.
- **Motion drawn on the GPU.** Transitions, glides, the wash behind the window and the playing indicators are keyframes a shader interpolates at present time, so a moving element costs no layout or paint on the CPU (`Animated`, `Glide`, `Veil`, `Rise`, `SlideUp`).
- **The system's settings.** Reduce Motion, Increase Contrast, Reduce Transparency and the system text size are read where the platform provides them and applied by every component.
- **Touch.** On a touch screen every control keeps a 44-point target, a long press opens the context menu, and a window narrower than 660 points takes the compact layout (`Look::touch`, `Look::compact`).

The kit is compositional, in the manner of atomic design. Atoms are the indivisible parts (`Text`, `KoanIcon`, `Button`, `Divider`, the spacing steps); molecules put a few atoms together for one job (`TwoLines`, a title over a subtitle; `ControlGroup`, touch-sized controls on one rhythm; a `TableRow`); and a few generic organisms lay molecules out (bars, sheets, the page). Something specific to one app, such as kōan's mini player, is the app's composition of these parts, not a component of the kit.

(Picture: The storybook's buttons page: each kind, and the destructive, disabled and toolbar states.)

## Depending on it

koan-kit is not yet published to crates.io. Depend on it from the kōan repository, and on GPUI at the revision the kit uses, which is pinned in kōan's root `Cargo.toml`. The `gpui` dependency is needed only because GPUI's derive macros name it; everything a page uses comes through `koan_kit`.

```toml
[dependencies]
koan-kit = { git = "https://github.com/radiosilence/koan" }
gpui = { package = "gpui-koan", git = "https://github.com/radiosilence/zed", rev = "<the rev in kōan's Cargo.toml>" }
```

A kit change is tested against kōan's own pages and its storybook before it lands, and the reference is regenerated with it, so the reference at a commit describes the kit at that commit.

## A first window

The example below is `crates/koan-kit/examples/first_window.rs`, and `cargo run -p koan-kit --example first_window` opens it. It shows the shape of every koan-kit app: `theme::init` and the icon set at startup, a window opened through the kit (`SecondaryWindow` here, `MainWindow` for an app's main window), and a view whose `render` composes containers and controls.

<!-- example: crates/koan-kit/examples/first_window.rs -->
```rust
//! A first window on koan-kit: a page whose header and form come from the
//! kit, a toggle and a choice the view keeps, and a button that puts them
//! back. Nothing in it sets a size, a colour or a gap: the page, the form
//! and the controls own those. The guide's first example
//! (`docs/guide/koan-kit.md`) is this file.
//!
//! cargo run -p koan-kit --example first_window

use koan_kit::app::*;
use koan_kit::components::{
    Button, ButtonKind, Form, FormRow, FormSection, Page, PageHeader, SecondaryWindow, Segmented,
    Toggle,
};
use koan_kit::theme::{self, Look};

/// The view: what the page shows, and nothing about how it looks.
struct Playback {
    shuffle: bool,
    quality: usize,
}

impl Render for Playback {
    fn render(&mut self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>) -> impl IntoElement {
        // The tokens resolved for this window: appearance, text size, accent.
        let look = Look::of(window, cx);
        let form = Form::new("playback")
            .section(
                FormSection::titled("playback")
                    .row(
                        Toggle::new("shuffle", "shuffle", self.shuffle).on_change(cx.listener(
                            |this, on: &bool, _, cx| {
                                this.shuffle = *on;
                                cx.notify();
                            },
                        )),
                    )
                    .row(
                        FormRow::new("quality").control(
                            Segmented::new("quality", ["original", "high", "low"], self.quality)
                                .on_change(cx.listener(|this, chosen: &usize, _, cx| {
                                    this.quality = *chosen;
                                    cx.notify();
                                })),
                        ),
                    )
                    .footer("Low quality is used only on a metered connection."),
            )
            .section(
                FormSection::new().row(
                    Button::new("reset", ButtonKind::Bordered)
                        .label("reset")
                        .on_click(cx.listener(|this, _, _, cx| {
                            this.shuffle = false;
                            this.quality = 0;
                            cx.notify();
                        })),
                ),
            );
        Page::new(PageHeader::new("playback").detail("a first window")).body(form.build(&look))
    }
}

fn main() {
    koan_kit::app::platform::application()
        // The icon set, which the controls' glyphs are drawn from.
        .with_assets(theme::icons::Assets)
        .run(|cx: &mut App| {
            // The theme, the system's display settings and the kit's keys.
            theme::init(cx);
            let opened = SecondaryWindow::new("Playback", "rocks.koan.first-window")
                .size(640.0, 480.0)
                .open(cx, |_, cx| {
                    cx.new(|_| Playback {
                        shuffle: false,
                        quality: 0,
                    })
                });
            if let Err(e) = opened {
                eprintln!("the window did not open: {e}");
                cx.quit();
            }
        });
}
```

Controls report through handlers, and `cx.listener` turns a view method into one: the view changes its own state and calls `cx.notify()`, and the next render shows the change. Words a control draws are the caller's. App words are passed lowercase, as the kit draws them (`theme::label` does the lowering), and data such as a record's title keeps its case through the `data` constructors (`PageHeader::data`, `FormRow::data`).

(Picture: The storybook's fields and forms page.)

## Pages decide what, the kit decides how

A page chooses its content, its order, which verbs a header or menu offers, which columns a table shows from the set the table defines, and what an empty page says. It does not choose where things sit or how they look:

- no `px(…)`, colours, font sizes or line heights;
- no margins, gaps or padding between sections, rows or header parts;
- no row heights, sleeve sizes or column widths of its own;
- no header, toolbar button, table row, menu panel or empty state built from `div()`s.

`koan_kit::app` re-exports the parts of GPUI a page needs to be a view (entities, contexts, windows, tasks, actions, events, focus, `div`) and leaves out the ones that choose a look, so going around the kit is a compile error rather than a habit. `koan_kit::raw` is GPUI whole, for the rare case nothing in the kit serves; each use there should carry a comment saying why. When a page needs something no container offers, the container gains the capability, and every page gets it.

## Choosing a container

| A page that shows | Is built from |
|---|---|
| A title, a detail line, verbs, and a body | `Page` with a `PageHeader` |
| Settings, or anything labelled and edited | `Form`, `FormSection`, `FormRow` |
| Rows of things with figures in columns | `TableRow` in a `ScrollArea`, `table_list` or `RowList` |
| A dense editor's table of values | `DenseTable` |
| Tiles or pills in rows that wrap | `Collection`, `TileGrid` |
| A sidebar or a detail panel beside the page | `SplitView`, `Inspector` |
| Nothing yet | `EmptyState` |
| A question before a write | `sheet::present` and its question host |
| A window's top bar | `Toolbar`, `ToolbarButton`, `ToolbarGroup` |
| A bar over a touch window's tabs | `AccessoryBar` holding a picture, `TwoLines` and a `ControlGroup` |
| A list of choices under a control | `Menu`, `KoanContextMenu`, `opens_panel` |
| A step-by-step flow | `Steps`, `ChoiceCards`, `CheckItem` |

(Picture: The storybook's tables and lists page.)

(Picture: The storybook's toolbars and headers page.)

## Tokens and the look

Every component reads `Look::of(window, cx)` in its `render`; a page reads it only to hand it to a container that asks for it, as `Form::build` does. The look carries:

- the palette, which text and glyphs take as a `Tone` (`Ink`, `Strong`, `Muted`, `Accent`, `Bad`) rather than as a colour;
- the type scale as `TypeRole`s, all in Geist Mono, sized by the person's text size (`Scale`);
- the spacing scale `Space`, through `Look::space`;
- `Motion`, the durations and the quick-out curve every transition uses, which Reduce Motion collapses;
- the accent, from `AccentFeed`, which an app sets from any colour (kōan sets it from the playing record's sleeve) and which reaches every component without redrawing the views that do not draw it.

Text takes its role through `KoanStyled::text_role`, and glyphs come from `Icon` and `KoanIcon`, drawn from the kit's icon set at the stroke the role asks for.

## Motion

Motion in the kit is written as keyframes and drawn by the GPU: a component writes where a value goes, when and along which curve, and the shader interpolates it at each present. Nothing in a page runs a timer to animate.

- `Veil`, `Rise`, `SlideUp`, `Fading`, `ZoomIn` and `Flying` bring sheets, panels and pictures in and out.
- `Glide` moves a value on its own between renders, such as a seek bar's playhead or a download's bar.
- `Animated` and `AnimatedQuad` are the base the others are built on, for a component of an app's own.
- `settle_length` says how long a transition takes, for an app that waits for one to finish.

## Touch and narrow windows

The same components run on iOS. `Look::touch` says a finger is the pointer, and controls keep a 44-point target (`Look::target`). `Look::compact` says the window is a touch window narrower than `COMPACT_WIDTH`, 660 points; a page asks it, each frame, to choose a layout, and never asks which device it is on. `KoanInsets` keeps content clear of the status bar, the home indicator and the keyboard.

## The storybook

`cargo run -p koan-kit` opens the storybook: every component and container on a page of its own, in each of its states, with switches for the appearance, the accent, the text size, Reduce Motion, touch and "show icons". `cargo run -p koan-kit --example storybook -- DIR` draws each page offscreen, dark and light side by side, into PNGs; the pictures on this page come from it.

## The reference

The [API reference](../reference/koan-kit-api.md) is generated from the kit's source by `just kit-reference`, and CI fails when it is out of date, so it always matches the code at the same commit. It lists every public item by the module an app names it from, with its doc comment, its signature and, for a type, its methods and the traits it implements.

# koan-kit API reference

<!-- Generated by `just kit-reference` from koan-kit's sources; edit the doc comments, not this file. -->

Every item an app can name through koan-kit's public paths, with its doc comment and signature, grouped by the module it is named from and then by the file it is written in. The [guide](../guide/koan-kit.md) says how the parts fit together. `koan_kit::raw`, GPUI re-exported whole, is left out: an app reaches for it only where nothing here serves, and says why.

## `koan_kit::app`

The app model a koan-kit app is written in, and the composition its pages
do: entities and their contexts, views and elements, events, actions,
focus, globals and tasks, and `div` with the traits that give it
children, layout and listeners; and the platform's services the app
uses (the clipboard, dropped files, the file picker), and the size a
cached view keeps between frames (`StyleRefinement`). Pages build from
these and koan-kit's components.

What a page sizes, colours or draws by hand (`px`, colours, fonts,
canvases, lists, overlays, animation) is not here: it goes through the
components and tokens, or through `raw` with a reason. Sleeves decoded
for the components to draw are in `image`.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `Action`, `AnyElement`, `AnyEntity`, `AnyView`, `AnyWeakEntity`, `AnyWindowHandle`, `App`, `AppContext`, `AsyncApp`, `AsyncWindowContext`, `BackGestureEvent`, `BackGesturePhase`, `Bounds`, `ClickEvent`, `ClipboardItem`, `Context`, `Div`, `DragMoveEvent`, `ElementId`, `Entity`, `EntityId`, `EventEmitter`, `ExternalPaths`, `FocusHandle`, `Focusable`, `Global`, `HapticFeedback`, `IntoElement`, `KeyBinding`, `KeyContext`, `KeyDownEvent`, `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, `MouseButton`, `MouseDownEvent`, `MouseMoveEvent`, `MouseUpEvent`, `NavigationDirection`, `ParentElement`, `PathPromptOptions`, `Pixels`, `Point`, `QuitMode`, `ReadGlobal`, `Render`, `RenderOnce`, `ScrollEvent`, `ScrollHandle`, `ScrollSource`, `SharedString`, `Size`, `Stateful`, `StyleRefinement`, `Subscription`, `Task`, `UpdateGlobal`, `WeakEntity`, `Window`, `WindowBounds`, `WindowHandle`, `WindowId`, `WindowVisibility`, `actions`, `div`, `relative`.

Re-exported from `gpui::prelude`: everything.


## `koan_kit::app::a11y`

What a page tells a screen reader where no component says it for the
page: a landmark's role (the sidebar as navigation, the stage as main), a
tile's role and the action it answers, a region announced as it changes.
Set beside the element's id with `role`, `aria_label` and `aria_live`.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `AccessibleAction`, `Role`.

Re-exported from `gpui::accesskit`: `Live`.


## `koan_kit::app::image`

A sleeve as the renderer holds it: decoded off the main thread by the
app's cover caches and handed to `Cover`, `TileSleeve` and the wash; its
average colour, worked out from its pixels; the renderer's id for it.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `Image`, `ImageFormat`, `ImageId`, `RenderImage`, `Rgba`.


## `koan_kit::app::input`

The text input koan-kit's fields draw (`TextField`, `FilterField`): a
page owns its state and listens to its events, and a field that leads a
list takes the arrows and Escape from it.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `TextContentType`.


## `koan_kit::app::platform`

What the app asks of the platform beyond its windows' content: starting
up, the menu bar and the Dock's menu, which system this is, the keymap
as the shortcuts sheet and the tests read it, how a trackpad's scroll is
phased, and on a phone the app leaving the screen and coming back.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `AppLifecyclePhase`, `KeyBindingContextPredicate`, `Keymap`, `Menu`, `MenuItem`, `OperatingSystem`, `OsAction`, `SystemMenuType`, `TouchPhase`.

Re-exported from `gpui_platform`: `application`.


## `koan_kit::app::test`

What a test of a page or the app drives it with.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `TestAppContext`, `VisualTestContext`, `test`.


## `koan_kit::components`

The kōan theme's components, drawn from its tokens: flat, square, and in
Geist Mono. docs/design/koan-theme.md#components describes each; the
comments here say only where GPUI asks for something the spec does not.

Containers (`Page`, `Toolbar`, `Form`, `Menu`, the table's rows) own the
spacing and metrics, and pages declare only content. The design, what
each piece owns and the map from the Swift roles to these, is
docs/design/koan-kit.md.

Every component is a `RenderOnce` element that reads the window's `Look`
as it renders. Interactive ones take an id, under which they keep what
the pointer is doing and the state of their transitions.

### components::accessory_bar

A bar over the foot of a touch window, above its tabs: something going
on that the person can reach from any page.

#### `AccessoryBar`

*struct* · A bar over a touch window's tabs, on the page's ground, laying out what
it is given in a row on the page's steps, centred down it, with an
optional progress line along its top edge, `AccessoryBar::height` tall.
A tap on the bar, or a finger pushing it up, calls `on_open`, which
opens what the bar stands for.

It holds no parts of its own: an app composes it, typically a picture,
`TwoLines` and a `ControlGroup`, whose presses stay the controls'.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct AccessoryBar { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn height(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The bar's height, as iOS draws a phone's bar over the tabs: 56
  points.

- `pub fn picture(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The side of a picture leading the bar, such as a cover: 30 points.

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  An empty bar, its gestures kept under `id`.

- `pub fn progress(self, share: f32, glide: Option<&Glide>) -> Self`

  A line along the top, `share` of the way across (0 to 1), moved on
  the GPU between reports by `glide` when one is given.

- `pub fn on_open(self, on_open: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a tap on the bar, or a finger pushing it up, does. Without it
  the bar takes neither.

Implements `ParentElement`, `RenderOnce`.

### components::animated

Animation the GPU draws: an element whose pixels come from a fragment
program, moved by keyframes the app writes from any thread, with no GPUI
frame drawn while it moves.

A page declares one with `AnimatedQuad::new(&PROGRAM, &animated)` and
keeps the `Animated` handle, writing into it what moves the drawing when
the data changes: the levels of the playing bars, a download's progress,
the anchor of a seek bar's playhead. A plain value is a uniform (`set`); a
moving one is a track (`to`, `segment`, `hold`): from, to, start, length
and curve, which the program reads at present time (`koan_track`), so the
page and the views around it are not drawn again while it moves.

What the kit takes care of:
- **The frame loop.** A change wakes the window to present; a track
  presents at the display's rate while it moves and once more as it
  lands, then the window parks. `run` keeps the clock going for a drawing
  that moves with `time` itself (the prism).
- **Parking.** A window with nothing changing or moving parks, as an idle
  window does. A hidden or occluded window is not woken, and a quad off
  screen is not in the scene, so it costs nothing.
- **Reduce Motion.** A `decorative` quad's tracks stand at their ends
  while the person has asked for less motion; its values still show as
  they change.
- **Long uptime.** Track times are seconds from an origin that moves to
  now every hour, so f32 keeps its precision.

Programs are MSL and WGSL source of one `shade` function (GPUI's
`ShaderProgram`), given the pixel's position and the quad's size in
device pixels, `time`, the uniforms and tracks, and an optional image,
and returning the pixel's straight-alpha colour. Where the renderer draws
no shaders (`SHADED` is false), a page draws its fallback itself.

#### `Ease`

*enum* · How a track moves between its ends (#1859).

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum Ease {
    Linear,
    /// Core Animation's `easeInEaseOut`, `cubic-bezier(0.42, 0, 0.58, 1)`.
    InOut,
    /// The theme's quick-out, `cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.9, 0.3, 1)`.
    QuickOut,
    /// A CSS cubic Bézier, `cubic-bezier(x1, y1, x2, y2)`.
    Bezier(f32, f32, f32, f32),
    /// A damped spring of unit mass; a retarget carries its velocity on, and
    /// its length is how long it takes to settle.
    Spring { stiffness: f32, damping: f32 },
}
```

#### `SHADED`

*constant* · Whether this platform's renderer draws shader quads: Metal (macOS and
iOS, the one renderer), and wgpu where it has storage buffers, as every
desktop Linux driver does.

```rust
pub const SHADED: bool = cfg!(
    any(target_os = "macos", target_os = "ios", target_os = "linux")
);
```

#### `Animated`

*struct* · What moves an `AnimatedQuad`: its uniforms, written from any thread, and
how it runs. Clones share it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct Animated(_);
```

- `pub fn downgrade(&self) -> WeakAnimated`

  A handle that does not keep the uniforms alive, for a closure or a
  thread that should stop writing once the element is gone.

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  Uniforms with their clock started now.

- `pub fn set(&self, index: usize, value: f32)`

  Uniform `index`; a change is presented at the next refresh.

- `pub fn now() -> Instant`

  The clock tracks are reckoned in: when a frame made now reaches the
  screen. What a retarget samples a moving value at.

- `pub fn at(&self, k: usize, at: Instant) -> f32`

  Where track `k` stands at `at`, as the program draws it.

- `pub fn moving(&self) -> bool`

  Whether a track is moving now.

- `pub fn settled_in(&self) -> Option<Duration>`

  How long until every track stands still: `None` while one repeats.

- `pub fn track(&self, k: usize) -> Track`

  Track `k` as it was last written.

- `pub fn hold(&self, k: usize, value: f32)`

  Track `k` standing at `value`.

- `pub fn to(&self, k: usize, to: f32, length: Duration, ease: Ease)`

  Track `k` moving from where it stands now to `to` over `length`: a
  retarget mid-flight starts from the value, and with a spring the
  velocity, it had reached.

- `pub fn throw(&self, k: usize, to: f32, velocity: f32, stiffness: f32, damping: f32) -> Duration`

  Track `k` thrown from where it stands at `velocity` (units per
  second) and drawn back to `to` on a spring: a bounce off a list's end.
  Returns how long it takes to settle. Under `still` it stands at `to`.

- `pub fn segment(&self, k: usize, from: f32, to: f32, start: Instant, length: Duration, ease: Ease, repeat: Repeat)`

  Track `k` as a whole segment, for a value whose start is known: an
  analyser frame at its own time, a playhead from its anchor, a drift
  that breathes (`Repeat::Alternate`).

- `pub fn set_color(&self, index: usize, color: Hsla)`

  `color` into the four uniforms from `index`, as red, green, blue and
  alpha. A colour naming a colour slot (the accent) is read from the
  slot at each present, so it moves in the program with no frame drawn.

- `pub fn set_all(&self, start: usize, values: &[f32])`

  Uniforms from `start` on.

- `pub fn get(&self, index: usize) -> f32`

  Uniform `index` as it stands.

- `pub fn set_origin(&self, origin: Instant)`

  The moment `time` in the program counts from.

- `pub fn run(&self, running: bool)`

  Whether the clock runs: presented every refresh while it does, for a
  drawing that moves with `time`.

- `pub fn run_for(&self, length: Duration)`

  Runs the clock for `length` from now and then stops by itself, with
  nothing to stop it: a transition.

- `pub fn is_running(&self) -> bool`

  Whether the clock runs; a `run_for` past its length has stopped.

- `pub fn attach(&self, decorative: bool, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)`

  For an element drawing the quad itself rather than through
  `AnimatedQuad`: call as it is laid out (in prepaint), with whether its
  motion is decorative, then `paint` it.

- `pub fn paint_layer(&self, program: &'static ShaderProgram, bounds: Bounds<Pixels>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App, paint: impl FnOnce(&mut Window, &mut App))`

  Paints what `paint` paints into a texture of its own, composed over
  `bounds` by `program` sampling it (#1859).

- `pub fn paint(&self, program: &'static ShaderProgram, image: Option<Arc<RenderImage>>, bounds: Bounds<Pixels>, window: &mut Window)`

  Paints the quad over `bounds`, drawn by `program`, sampling `image`.

#### `WeakAnimated`

*struct* · An `Animated` held without keeping it alive: a clock that follows
another's running for as long as its quad is drawn.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct WeakAnimated(_);
```

- `pub fn upgrade(&self) -> Option<Animated>`

  The handle again, while something still holds it.

- `pub fn is(&self, animated: &Animated) -> bool`

  Whether it is the handle `animated` was taken from.

#### `AnimatedQuad`

*struct* · An element drawn by `program` from `animated`, sized by its parent or by
its own style.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct AnimatedQuad { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(program: &'static ShaderProgram, animated: &Animated) -> Self`

  A quad drawn by `program`, reading its keyframes from `animated`.

- `pub fn image(self, image: Arc<RenderImage>) -> Self`

  The image the program samples (`koan_sample`).

- `pub fn decorative(self) -> Self`

  Motion for its own sake, such as a drift: its clock stops while the
  person asks for less motion. Progress (a playhead, a download) is not
  decorative.

Implements `RenderOnce`, `Styled`.

### components::answer

A control answering what was done to it: a small mark drawn by the GPU
around or under the control for a moment, then gone. The kit's controls
call it from their own handlers (a button pressed, a toggle turned, a
heart filled, something copied), so every page that uses them answers
alike with no code of its own.

Subtle by rule: one shader quad over the control and a little past its
edge, in the accent at low opacity, never over the control's text, for
no longer than `Motion::SETTLE`. Its progress is one track the GPU moves,
and the quad leaves the scene when it ends, so a control at rest, or off
screen, costs nothing. Under Reduce Motion nothing is drawn.

The showpieces answer through the glyph itself (`GLYPH`): a heart's fill
popping in with particles or deflating, play folding into pause, shuffle's
arrows crossing, repeat spinning round, a skip nudged its way. The glyph
is drawn by the program from the icon's own strokes on the 24-point grid
while the answer runs, in place of the icon, which comes back as it ends.

#### `Answer`

*enum* · What an answer draws.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Answer {
    /// One ring leaving the control's outline: a button pressed, a toggle
    /// turned on.
    Ring,
    /// Six dots leaving the control and fading: a heart filled.
    Burst,
    /// One ring closing in on the control: a heart emptied.
    Close,
    /// A line drawn under the control from its leading edge: something
    /// copied, something arrived at a destination.
    Sweep,
    /// The control's ground lifting to the accent and settling back: a
    /// bordered button pressed, rows landing.
    Lift,
    /// The first of rows dropped into a list: its ground lifting as
    /// `Lift`'s, and the insertion line it landed at closing into it from
    /// both ends along its top edge.
    Landed,
    /// A short streak leaving the glyph the way it points: a skip, forward
    /// (`true`) or back.
    Streak(bool),
    /// A heart filling: the fill pops in past its size and settles, small
    /// particles thrown out round it.
    Fill,
    /// A heart emptying: the fill deflates, squashing, into the outline.
    Empty,
    /// The glyph turning into another (`Morph`).
    Morph(Morph),
    /// A skip's glyph nudged the way it points and back: forward (`true`)
    /// or back.
    Nudge(bool),
    /// A tick drawing itself in along its stroke (`true`), or erasing.
    Tick(bool),
    /// The underline of a choice drawn in from the side the last choice
    /// stood: from the leading edge (`true`) or the trailing one.
    Slide(bool),
    /// The glyph's raster moved as its verb means (`GESTURE`).
    Gesture(Gesture),
}
```

- `pub fn glyph(self) -> bool`

  Drawn through the glyph rather than around it: the control draws it
  with `Answerer::glyph_layer`, its icon hidden meanwhile.

#### `Morph`

*enum* · How a glyph turns into another.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Morph {
    /// Play's triangle folding into pause's bars: its left side becomes
    /// the left bar, its slanted sides the right.
    ToPause,
    ToPlay,
    /// Shuffle turned on: the arrows swing apart and cross again.
    ShuffleOn,
    /// Shuffle turned off: the arrows sway, less.
    ShuffleOff,
    /// Repeat spinning round a turn.
    Spin,
    /// Repeat spinning round, its "one" popping in.
    SpinToOne,
    /// Repeat spinning round, its "one" shrinking away.
    SpinFromOne,
    /// Copy's two sheets shrinking away as a tick draws in.
    CopyToTick,
    /// The tick erasing as the sheets come back.
    TickToCopy,
}
```

#### `ANSWER`

*static* · The answers as one program. Uniforms: 0 the answer, 1 which way a streak
goes, 2..6 the colour, 6
the spill and 7 a point, in device pixels; track 0 the answer's time
from 0 to 1, which its fade follows, and track 1 its travel, on the
answer's own curve.

```rust
pub static ANSWER: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-answer",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    if (t >= 1.0) { return float4(0.0); }
    float e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float spill = u[6];
    float pt = u[7];
    float2 inner = size - 2.0 * spill;
    float2 c = size * 0.5;
    float half_side = 0.5 * min(inner.x, inner.y);
    float d = length(position - c);
    float a = 0.0;
    int kind = int(u[0] + 0.5);
    if (kind == 0) {
        float r = half_side + spill * 0.9 * e;
        a = 0.35 * (1.0 - t) * clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(d - r) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    } else if (kind == 1) {
        float r = mix(half_side * 0.9, half_side + spill * 0.9, e);
        float dot = 1.2 * pt * (1.0 - 0.5 * t);
        for (int i = 0; i < 6; i++) {
            float ang = 0.5236 + float(i) * 1.0472;
            float2 p = c + r * float2(cos(ang), sin(ang));
            a = max(a, clamp(dot - length(position - p) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
        }
        a *= 0.6 * (1.0 - t);
    } else if (kind == 2) {
        float r = mix(half_side + spill * 0.9, half_side * 0.8, e);
        a = 0.3 * (1.0 - t) * clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(d - r) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    } else if (kind == 3) {
        float line = size.y - pt;
        float reach = u[1] < 0.0 ? size.x - position.x : position.x;
        bool under = position.y >= line && reach <= size.x * e;
        a = under ? 0.6 * (1.0 - t * t) : 0.0;
    } else if (kind == 5) {
        float x = (position.x - c.x) * u[1];
        float half_w = 0.5 * inner.x;
        float head = half_w + spill * e;
        float tail = mix(half_w, head, t);
        float across = clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(position.y - c.y) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
        a = (x >= tail && x <= head) ? 0.45 * (1.0 - t) * across : 0.0;
    } else {
        a = 0.12 * (1.0 - e);
        if (u[1] > 0.5) {
            float half_w = 0.5 * size.x * (1.0 - e);
            if (position.y < 2.0 * pt && abs(position.x - c.x) <= half_w) {
                a = max(a, 0.7 * (1.0 - t));
            }
        }
    }
    return float4(u[8], u[9], u[10], u[11] * a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    if (t >= 1.0) { return vec4<f32>(0.0); }
    let e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let spill = u[1].z;
    let pt = u[1].w;
    let inner = size - 2.0 * spill;
    let c = size * 0.5;
    let half_side = 0.5 * min(inner.x, inner.y);
    let d = length(position - c);
    var a = 0.0;
    let kind = i32(u[0].x + 0.5);
    if (kind == 0) {
        let r = half_side + spill * 0.9 * e;
        a = 0.35 * (1.0 - t) * clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(d - r) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    } else if (kind == 1) {
        let r = mix(half_side * 0.9, half_side + spill * 0.9, e);
        let dot_r = 1.2 * pt * (1.0 - 0.5 * t);
        for (var i = 0; i < 6; i++) {
            let ang = 0.5236 + f32(i) * 1.0472;
            let p = c + r * vec2<f32>(cos(ang), sin(ang));
            a = max(a, clamp(dot_r - length(position - p) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
        }
        a = a * 0.6 * (1.0 - t);
    } else if (kind == 2) {
        let r = mix(half_side + spill * 0.9, half_side * 0.8, e);
        a = 0.3 * (1.0 - t) * clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(d - r) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    } else if (kind == 3) {
        let line = size.y - pt;
        var reach = position.x;
        if (u[0].y < 0.0) { reach = size.x - position.x; }
        if (position.y >= line && reach <= size.x * e) {
            a = 0.6 * (1.0 - t * t);
        }
    } else if (kind == 5) {
        let x = (position.x - c.x) * u[0].y;
        let half_w = 0.5 * inner.x;
        let head = half_w + spill * e;
        let tail = mix(half_w, head, t);
        let across = clamp(0.75 * pt - abs(position.y - c.y) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
        if (x >= tail && x <= head) {
            a = 0.45 * (1.0 - t) * across;
        }
    } else {
        a = 0.12 * (1.0 - e);
        if (u[0].y > 0.5) {
            let half_w = 0.5 * size.x * (1.0 - e);
            if (position.y < 2.0 * pt && abs(position.x - c.x) <= half_w) {
                a = max(a, 0.7 * (1.0 - t));
            }
        }
    }
    let rgba = u[2];
    return vec4<f32>(rgba.xyz, rgba.w * a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `GLYPH`

*static* · The glyph answers as one program, drawing the icons' strokes on their
24-point grid (`apps/ios/Resources/Icons`). Uniforms: 0 the answer, 1
which way a nudge goes, 2 the spill, 4..8 the glyph's colour, 8..12 the
accent (a heart's fill, its particles), 12 the glyph's side, all in
device pixels; each colour is a whole block of four; track 0 the answer's time from 0 to 1, track 1 the same on
its curve.

It holds its end pose once the time has run, which is the icon itself,
so the frame between the answer's end and the control drawing its icon
again shows the icon either way. Open strokes have square caps and the
closed triangles mitred corners, as the icons are drawn.

```rust
pub static GLYPH: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-glyph",
    msl: r#"
float kg_seg(float2 p, float2 a, float2 b) {
    float2 pa = p - a;
    float2 ba = b - a;
    float h = clamp(dot(pa, ba) / dot(ba, ba), 0.0, 1.0);
    return length(pa - ba * h);
}
float kg_boxd(float2 p, float2 a, float2 b) {
    float2 ba = b - a;
    float l = max(length(ba), 0.0001);
    float2 d = ba / l;
    float2 pa = p - a;
    float along = dot(pa, d);
    float perp = abs(d.x * pa.y - d.y * pa.x);
    return max(perp, abs(along - 0.5 * l) - 0.5 * l);
}
float kg_line(float2 p, float2 a, float2 b, float k) {
    return clamp((0.75 - kg_boxd(p, a, b)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
}
float kg_edge(float2 p, float2 a, float2 b, float2 inside) {
    float2 n = normalize(float2(b.y - a.y, a.x - b.x));
    if (dot(inside - a, n) > 0.0) { n = -n; }
    return dot(p - a, n);
}
float kg_tri(float2 p, float2 a, float2 b, float2 c, float k) {
    float2 m = (a + b + c) / 3.0;
    float d = max(max(kg_edge(p, a, b, m), kg_edge(p, b, c, m)), kg_edge(p, c, a, m));
    return clamp((0.75 - abs(d)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
}
float kg_heart(float2 g) {
    float d1 = length(g - float2(9.0, 10.0)) - 4.243;
    float d2 = length(g - float2(15.0, 10.0)) - 4.243;
    float d3 = (abs(g.x - 12.0) + abs(g.y - 13.0) - 6.0) * 0.7071;
    return min(min(d1, d2), d3);
}
float kg_back(float t) {
    float s = t - 1.0;
    return 1.0 + 2.70158 * s * s * s + 1.70158 * s * s;
}
float kg_tick(float2 g, float p, float k) {
    float2 a = float2(5.0, 12.5);
    float2 b = float2(10.0, 17.5);
    float2 c = float2(19.0, 7.0);
    float along = clamp(p, 0.0, 1.0) * 20.963;
    float cov = 0.0;
    if (along > 0.0) {
        cov = kg_line(g, a, mix(a, b, min(along / 7.071, 1.0)), k);
    }
    if (along > 7.071) {
        cov = max(cov, kg_line(g, b, mix(b, c, (along - 7.071) / 13.892), k));
    }
    return cov;
}
float kg_copy(float2 g, float s, float k) {
    if (s <= 0.01) { return 0.0; }
    float2 h = float2(12.0, 12.0) + (g - float2(12.0, 12.0)) / s;
    float d = min(kg_boxd(h, float2(4.5, 16.5), float2(4.5, 3.5)), kg_boxd(h, float2(4.5, 3.5), float2(15.5, 3.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, float2(8.5, 7.5), float2(19.5, 7.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, float2(19.5, 7.5), float2(19.5, 20.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, float2(19.5, 20.5), float2(8.5, 20.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, float2(8.5, 20.5), float2(8.5, 7.5)));
    return clamp((0.75 - d * s) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * s;
}
float2 kg_turn(float2 g, float a) {
    float2 v = g - float2(12.0, 12.0);
    float cs = cos(a);
    float sn = sin(a);
    return float2(12.0, 12.0) + float2(cs * v.x - sn * v.y, sn * v.x + cs * v.y);
}
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float k = max(u[12], 1.0) / 24.0;
    float2 g = (position - u[2]) / k;
    float pi = 3.14159265;
    float line = 0.0;
    float fill = 0.0;
    int kind = int(u[0] + 0.5);
    if (kind == 10 || kind == 11) {
        float2 c = float2(12.0, 13.0);
        line = clamp((0.75 - abs(kg_heart(g))) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
        float2 s2;
        if (kind == 10) {
            float s = max(kg_back(t), 0.001);
            s2 = float2(s, s);
            for (int i = 0; i < 7; i++) {
                float ang = -1.5708 + float(i) * 0.8976;
                float2 p = float2(12.0, 12.5) + (7.5 + 5.5 * e) * float2(cos(ang), sin(ang));
                float dot_r = 1.6 * sin(pi * min(t * 1.4, 1.0));
                fill = max(fill, clamp((dot_r - length(g - p)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * (1.0 - t));
            }
        } else {
            float q = sin(pi * t);
            float s = max(1.0 - e, 0.001);
            s2 = float2(s * (1.0 + 0.25 * q), s * (1.0 - 0.2 * q));
        }
        float d = kg_heart(c + (g - c) / s2) * min(s2.x, s2.y);
        fill = max(fill, clamp(-d * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
    } else if (kind == 12 || kind == 13) {
        float m = kind == 12 ? e : 1.0 - e;
        float bars = kg_line(g, mix(float2(7.0, 5.0), float2(8.5, 5.0), m), mix(float2(7.0, 19.0), float2(8.5, 19.0), m), k);
        bars = max(bars, kg_line(g, mix(float2(7.0, 5.0), float2(15.5, 5.0), m), mix(float2(19.0, 12.0), float2(15.5, 12.0), m), k));
        bars = max(bars, kg_line(g, mix(float2(19.0, 12.0), float2(15.5, 12.0), m), mix(float2(7.0, 19.0), float2(15.5, 19.0), m), k));
        float tri = kg_tri(g, float2(7.0, 5.0), float2(19.0, 12.0), float2(7.0, 19.0), k);
        line = mix(tri, bars, smoothstep(0.0, 0.3, m));
    } else if (kind == 14 || kind == 15) {
        float m = 1.0 - (kind == 14 ? 1.0 : 0.4) * sin(pi * e);
        float2 a3 = mix(float2(16.0, 7.0), float2(16.0, 17.0), m);
        float2 a4 = mix(float2(20.5, 7.0), float2(20.5, 17.0), m);
        float2 b3 = mix(float2(16.0, 17.0), float2(16.0, 7.0), m);
        float2 b4 = mix(float2(20.5, 17.0), float2(20.5, 7.0), m);
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(3.0, 7.0), float2(8.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(8.0, 7.0), a3, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, a3, a4, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(3.0, 17.0), float2(8.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(8.0, 17.0), b3, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, b3, b4, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(18.0, 4.5), float2(20.5, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(20.5, 7.0), float2(18.0, 9.5), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(18.0, 14.5), float2(20.5, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, float2(20.5, 17.0), float2(18.0, 19.5), k));
    } else if (kind >= 16 && kind <= 18) {
        float2 r = kg_turn(g, -2.0 * pi * e);
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(4.0, 11.0), float2(4.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(4.0, 7.0), float2(19.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(16.5, 4.5), float2(19.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(19.0, 7.0), float2(16.5, 9.5), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(20.0, 13.0), float2(20.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(20.0, 17.0), float2(5.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(7.5, 14.5), float2(5.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, float2(5.0, 17.0), float2(7.5, 19.5), k));
        float sb = kind == 17 ? kg_back(t) : (kind == 18 ? 1.0 - e : 0.0);
        if (sb > 0.01) {
            float2 b = float2(12.0, 12.0) + (g - float2(12.0, 12.0)) / sb;
            float d = min(kg_boxd(b, float2(11.0, 10.5), float2(12.5, 10.0)), kg_boxd(b, float2(12.5, 10.0), float2(12.5, 14.0))) * sb;
            line = max(line, clamp((0.75 - d) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
        }
    } else if (kind == 19) {
        float x = g.x - 2.2 * sin(pi * t) * u[1];
        float2 n = float2(u[1] < 0.0 ? 24.0 - x : x, g.y);
        line = max(kg_tri(n, float2(5.0, 6.0), float2(15.0, 12.0), float2(5.0, 18.0), k), kg_line(n, float2(19.0, 6.0), float2(19.0, 18.0), k));
    } else if (kind == 20 || kind == 21) {
        line = kg_tick(g, kind == 20 ? e : 1.0 - e, k);
    } else if (kind == 22 || kind == 23) {
        float m = kind == 22 ? e : 1.0 - e;
        line = max(kg_copy(g, 1.0 - m, k), kg_tick(g, m, k));
    }
    float la = line * u[7];
    float fa = fill * u[11];
    float a = max(la, fa);
    if (a <= 0.0) { return float4(0.0); }
    float3 rgb = mix(float3(u[8], u[9], u[10]), float3(u[4], u[5], u[6]), la / (la + fa));
    return float4(rgb, a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn kg_seg(p: vec2<f32>, a: vec2<f32>, b: vec2<f32>) -> f32 {
    let pa = p - a;
    let ba = b - a;
    let h = clamp(dot(pa, ba) / dot(ba, ba), 0.0, 1.0);
    return length(pa - ba * h);
}
fn kg_boxd(p: vec2<f32>, a: vec2<f32>, b: vec2<f32>) -> f32 {
    let ba = b - a;
    let l = max(length(ba), 0.0001);
    let d = ba / l;
    let pa = p - a;
    let along = dot(pa, d);
    let perp = abs(d.x * pa.y - d.y * pa.x);
    return max(perp, abs(along - 0.5 * l) - 0.5 * l);
}
fn kg_line(p: vec2<f32>, a: vec2<f32>, b: vec2<f32>, k: f32) -> f32 {
    return clamp((0.75 - kg_boxd(p, a, b)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
}
fn kg_edge(p: vec2<f32>, a: vec2<f32>, b: vec2<f32>, inside: vec2<f32>) -> f32 {
    var n = normalize(vec2<f32>(b.y - a.y, a.x - b.x));
    if (dot(inside - a, n) > 0.0) { n = -n; }
    return dot(p - a, n);
}
fn kg_tri(p: vec2<f32>, a: vec2<f32>, b: vec2<f32>, c: vec2<f32>, k: f32) -> f32 {
    let m = (a + b + c) / 3.0;
    let d = max(max(kg_edge(p, a, b, m), kg_edge(p, b, c, m)), kg_edge(p, c, a, m));
    return clamp((0.75 - abs(d)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
}
fn kg_heart(g: vec2<f32>) -> f32 {
    let d1 = length(g - vec2<f32>(9.0, 10.0)) - 4.243;
    let d2 = length(g - vec2<f32>(15.0, 10.0)) - 4.243;
    let d3 = (abs(g.x - 12.0) + abs(g.y - 13.0) - 6.0) * 0.7071;
    return min(min(d1, d2), d3);
}
fn kg_back(t: f32) -> f32 {
    let s = t - 1.0;
    return 1.0 + 2.70158 * s * s * s + 1.70158 * s * s;
}
fn kg_tick(g: vec2<f32>, p: f32, k: f32) -> f32 {
    let a = vec2<f32>(5.0, 12.5);
    let b = vec2<f32>(10.0, 17.5);
    let c = vec2<f32>(19.0, 7.0);
    let along = clamp(p, 0.0, 1.0) * 20.963;
    var cov = 0.0;
    if (along > 0.0) {
        cov = kg_line(g, a, mix(a, b, min(along / 7.071, 1.0)), k);
    }
    if (along > 7.071) {
        cov = max(cov, kg_line(g, b, mix(b, c, (along - 7.071) / 13.892), k));
    }
    return cov;
}
fn kg_copy(g: vec2<f32>, s: f32, k: f32) -> f32 {
    if (s <= 0.01) { return 0.0; }
    let h = vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0) + (g - vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0)) / s;
    var d = min(kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(4.5, 16.5), vec2<f32>(4.5, 3.5)), kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(4.5, 3.5), vec2<f32>(15.5, 3.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(8.5, 7.5), vec2<f32>(19.5, 7.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(19.5, 7.5), vec2<f32>(19.5, 20.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(19.5, 20.5), vec2<f32>(8.5, 20.5)));
    d = min(d, kg_boxd(h, vec2<f32>(8.5, 20.5), vec2<f32>(8.5, 7.5)));
    return clamp((0.75 - d * s) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * s;
}
fn kg_turn(g: vec2<f32>, a: f32) -> vec2<f32> {
    let v = g - vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0);
    let cs = cos(a);
    let sn = sin(a);
    return vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0) + vec2<f32>(cs * v.x - sn * v.y, sn * v.x + cs * v.y);
}
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let k = max(u[3].x, 1.0) / 24.0;
    let g = (position - u[0].z) / k;
    let pi = 3.14159265;
    let heading = u[0].y;
    var line = 0.0;
    var fill = 0.0;
    let kind = i32(u[0].x + 0.5);
    if (kind == 10 || kind == 11) {
        let c = vec2<f32>(12.0, 13.0);
        line = clamp((0.75 - abs(kg_heart(g))) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
        var s2 = vec2<f32>(1.0, 1.0);
        if (kind == 10) {
            let s = max(kg_back(t), 0.001);
            s2 = vec2<f32>(s, s);
            for (var i = 0; i < 7; i++) {
                let ang = -1.5708 + f32(i) * 0.8976;
                let p = vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.5) + (7.5 + 5.5 * e) * vec2<f32>(cos(ang), sin(ang));
                let dot_r = 1.6 * sin(pi * min(t * 1.4, 1.0));
                fill = max(fill, clamp((dot_r - length(g - p)) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * (1.0 - t));
            }
        } else {
            let q = sin(pi * t);
            let s = max(1.0 - e, 0.001);
            s2 = vec2<f32>(s * (1.0 + 0.25 * q), s * (1.0 - 0.2 * q));
        }
        let d = kg_heart(c + (g - c) / s2) * min(s2.x, s2.y);
        fill = max(fill, clamp(-d * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
    } else if (kind == 12 || kind == 13) {
        var m = e;
        if (kind == 13) { m = 1.0 - e; }
        var bars = kg_line(g, mix(vec2<f32>(7.0, 5.0), vec2<f32>(8.5, 5.0), m), mix(vec2<f32>(7.0, 19.0), vec2<f32>(8.5, 19.0), m), k);
        bars = max(bars, kg_line(g, mix(vec2<f32>(7.0, 5.0), vec2<f32>(15.5, 5.0), m), mix(vec2<f32>(19.0, 12.0), vec2<f32>(15.5, 12.0), m), k));
        bars = max(bars, kg_line(g, mix(vec2<f32>(19.0, 12.0), vec2<f32>(15.5, 12.0), m), mix(vec2<f32>(7.0, 19.0), vec2<f32>(15.5, 19.0), m), k));
        let tri = kg_tri(g, vec2<f32>(7.0, 5.0), vec2<f32>(19.0, 12.0), vec2<f32>(7.0, 19.0), k);
        line = mix(tri, bars, smoothstep(0.0, 0.3, m));
    } else if (kind == 14 || kind == 15) {
        var reach = 0.4;
        if (kind == 14) { reach = 1.0; }
        let m = 1.0 - reach * sin(pi * e);
        let a3 = mix(vec2<f32>(16.0, 7.0), vec2<f32>(16.0, 17.0), m);
        let a4 = mix(vec2<f32>(20.5, 7.0), vec2<f32>(20.5, 17.0), m);
        let b3 = mix(vec2<f32>(16.0, 17.0), vec2<f32>(16.0, 7.0), m);
        let b4 = mix(vec2<f32>(20.5, 17.0), vec2<f32>(20.5, 7.0), m);
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(3.0, 7.0), vec2<f32>(8.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(8.0, 7.0), a3, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, a3, a4, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(3.0, 17.0), vec2<f32>(8.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(8.0, 17.0), b3, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, b3, b4, k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(18.0, 4.5), vec2<f32>(20.5, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(20.5, 7.0), vec2<f32>(18.0, 9.5), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(18.0, 14.5), vec2<f32>(20.5, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(g, vec2<f32>(20.5, 17.0), vec2<f32>(18.0, 19.5), k));
    } else if (kind >= 16 && kind <= 18) {
        let r = kg_turn(g, -2.0 * pi * e);
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(4.0, 11.0), vec2<f32>(4.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(4.0, 7.0), vec2<f32>(19.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(16.5, 4.5), vec2<f32>(19.0, 7.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(19.0, 7.0), vec2<f32>(16.5, 9.5), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(20.0, 13.0), vec2<f32>(20.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(20.0, 17.0), vec2<f32>(5.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(7.5, 14.5), vec2<f32>(5.0, 17.0), k));
        line = max(line, kg_line(r, vec2<f32>(5.0, 17.0), vec2<f32>(7.5, 19.5), k));
        var sb = 0.0;
        if (kind == 17) { sb = kg_back(t); }
        if (kind == 18) { sb = 1.0 - e; }
        if (sb > 0.01) {
            let b = vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0) + (g - vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0)) / sb;
            let d = min(kg_boxd(b, vec2<f32>(11.0, 10.5), vec2<f32>(12.5, 10.0)), kg_boxd(b, vec2<f32>(12.5, 10.0), vec2<f32>(12.5, 14.0))) * sb;
            line = max(line, clamp((0.75 - d) * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0));
        }
    } else if (kind == 19) {
        let x = g.x - 2.2 * sin(pi * t) * heading;
        var nx = x;
        if (heading < 0.0) { nx = 24.0 - x; }
        let n = vec2<f32>(nx, g.y);
        line = max(kg_tri(n, vec2<f32>(5.0, 6.0), vec2<f32>(15.0, 12.0), vec2<f32>(5.0, 18.0), k), kg_line(n, vec2<f32>(19.0, 6.0), vec2<f32>(19.0, 18.0), k));
    } else if (kind == 20 || kind == 21) {
        var p = e;
        if (kind == 21) { p = 1.0 - e; }
        line = kg_tick(g, p, k);
    } else if (kind == 22 || kind == 23) {
        var m = e;
        if (kind == 23) { m = 1.0 - e; }
        line = max(kg_copy(g, 1.0 - m, k), kg_tick(g, m, k));
    }
    let la = line * u[1].w;
    let fa = fill * u[2].w;
    let a = max(la, fa);
    if (a <= 0.0) { return vec4<f32>(0.0); }
    let rgb = mix(u[2].xyz, u[1].xyz, la / (la + fa));
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `Answerer`

*struct* · A control's answers, taken as the control is drawn and moved into its
handlers: keyed state found again from a handler would not be the
control's own.

The state is made only when something is answered or followed: a button
at rest, or scrolled past in a long list, holds an empty cell in its
element state and nothing else, no entity and no observer. The view that
drew the control is told directly when an answer starts and ends.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Answerer { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn of(id: &ElementId, window: &mut Window, _: &mut App) -> Self`

  The answers of the control `id`.

- `pub fn start(&self, answer: Answer, color: Hsla, cx: &mut App)`

  Starts `answer` in `color`, from the control's own handler. Nothing
  happens under Reduce Motion or where the renderer draws no shaders.

- `pub fn follow(&self, mark: u64, answer: Answer, color: Hsla, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)`

  Answers a change the control is told of rather than one done to it
  (a favourite set from a shortcut, music arriving from a menu), as
  the control draws: `mark` names the state, and a draw that finds it
  moved starts `answer`. The first draw only notes it, so a control
  scrolled in or drawn fresh shows its state still, and a change the
  control answered from its own handler a moment ago is not answered
  twice.

- `pub fn note(&self, mark: u64, cx: &mut App)`

  Notes `mark` without answering: a state the control answers only one
  way into (an option becoming the chosen one), passed while it stands
  in the other, so the next move in answers.

- `pub fn announce(&self, mark: u64, answer: Answer, color: Hsla, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)`

  As `follow`, for something whose appearing is the news (a notice):
  its first draw answers too.

- `pub fn live(&self, cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether an answer is being drawn.

- `pub fn glyph_live(&self, cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether a glyph answer is being drawn: the control hides its icon
  meanwhile, and draws `glyph_layer` in its place.

- `pub fn glyph_layer(&self, side: Pixels, color: Hsla, fill: Option<Hsla>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> Option<AnyElement>`

  The glyph answer being drawn, in place of the icon `side` points
  square that the control hides meanwhile, its strokes in `color` and
  what fills in `fill` (the accent if `None`);
  nothing at rest. The quad stands over the icon and a little past it,
  for what is thrown out; the icon's parent is `relative`.

- `pub fn gesture_layer(&self, icon: crate::theme::Icon, points: f32, color: Hsla, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> Option<AnyElement>`

  The gesture being drawn, in place of `icon` beside type of `points`
  that the control hides meanwhile, in `color`; nothing at rest. As
  `glyph_layer`, over the icon and a little past it.

- `pub fn layer(&self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> Option<AnyElement>`

  The answer being drawn, as a quad over the control and a little past
  it, for the control to add as its last child; nothing at rest. The
  control is `relative`.

### components::appear

Sheets, questions and the command bar coming and going. As one comes up,
the window's veil fades in under it (`Veil`) and its panel rises a step
as it fades in (`Rise`), over `Motion::ARRIVE`. One put away fades out
where it stood over `Motion::LEAVE` (`Fading`): its owner keeps it for
that long and draws it as it stood, inert, taking no input. Both take the
ease-in-out curve: on the quick-out a fade this short is mostly gone by
its first frame and reads as a cut.

Each is a layer the GPU composes (`AnimatedLayer`), so no frame is drawn
while it moves. Under Reduce Motion or Low Power Mode, and where the
renderer draws no shaders, they come and go at once.

An entrance runs once per place: its tracks are kept under the element
ids around it, so a sheet replaced in place does not rise again, and one
fading out, drawn where it stood, does not replay its entrance.

#### `Veil`

*struct* · The dim behind a sheet, covering its parent in `color`, fading in as the
sheet comes up.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Veil { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(color: Hsla) -> Self`

  A dim in `color` fading in over the window behind a sheet.

- `pub fn leaving(self, leaving: bool) -> Self`

  Lifting, over `Motion::SETTLE`, for a panel going back down under
  touch.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Rise`

*struct* · A sheet's panel coming up: fading in as it rises a small step into
place. Laid out as the panel is, so it stands where the panel would.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Rise { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  On a phone, where the scrim stands the panel on the window's foot,
  it comes up from below the window instead, as an iOS sheet does.

- `pub fn in_place(child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The small rise on every platform: a panel hung near the top.

- `pub fn leaving(self, leaving: bool) -> Self`

  On a phone, a panel going back down to the foot it came from.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `SlideUp`

*struct* · A screen coming up from the foot of the window, as a phone's full-screen
player does from its mini player: the whole height, easing out, on the
GPU. Opaque throughout; Reduce Motion shows it at once.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct SlideUp { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn leave_length(cx: &App) -> Duration`

  How long a screen started down now takes to leave (`RISE`, its rise
  in reverse), or nothing where it goes at once. The owner lets it go
  after. A panel that `fits` leaves over `settle_length`.

- `pub fn new(child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `child` rising from the window's foot as it is first drawn.

- `pub fn fits(self) -> Self`

  A sheet's panel rather than a screen: as tall as its content, up to
  the room above the keyboard and the system's insets.

- `pub fn leaving(self, leaving: bool) -> Self`

  Going back down, inert, before its owner drops it: a screen over
  `leave_length`, its rise in reverse; a panel over `Motion::SETTLE`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Flying`

*struct* · A picture flying from one place to another across the window, scaled and
moved on the GPU: `from` and `to` are window bounds, `child` drawn at
`to`, the whole window its layer. `id` names the flight; `length` and
`ease` are the owner's, to match what moves with it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Flying { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, from: Bounds<Pixels>, to: Bounds<Pixels>, length: Duration, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `child` drawn over the window, moving from `from` to `to` over `length`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Shifted`

*struct* · A row that moved when the rows around it changed: drawn where it now
stands, offset by `dy` points back to where it was and easing into place
on the GPU over `Motion::SETTLE`. `id` names the move: it runs once. At
once under Reduce Motion.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Shifted { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, delta: Point<Pixels>, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `child` drawn `delta` from where it is laid out, gliding to its place.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `ZoomIn`

*struct* · Something opening out of a point: a menu from the press that held it, a
picture from the sleeve tapped. Scaled up on the GPU from `FROM` as it
fades in over `Motion::ARRIVE`, on a spring-like quick-out; at once under
Reduce Motion. `at` is the point in window coordinates, clamped into what
it opens, so a menu pushed in from the window's edge still opens from the
side nearest the press.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ZoomIn { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(at: Point<Pixels>, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `child` growing out of `at`, the point that was pressed.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Hang`

*struct* · A notice hanging under the bar: it drops out from behind the bar as it
arrives and goes back up behind it, over `Motion::SETTLE`, on the GPU.
Its owner keeps drawing it `leaving` for `Hang::LEAVE` and lets it go
after. At once under Reduce Motion.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Hang { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `child` dropping in from behind the bar above it.

- `pub fn leaving(self, leaving: bool) -> Self`

  Going back behind the bar, inert, before its owner drops it.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Fading`

*struct* · Something put away, fading out where it stood. Its owner keeps what it
drew beside this, draws it through `draw` in the place it stood until
it has run, `LENGTH` after it started, and lets it go then.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Fading { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn length(cx: &App) -> Duration`

  How long something put away goes on being drawn: under touch its
  panel slides back down over `Motion::SETTLE`.

- `pub fn start(cx: &App) -> Option<Self>`

  A fade starting now, or none where things go at once. Under touch the
  layer stands whole and its panel and veil, told they are `leaving`,
  go by themselves.

- `pub fn began_by(&self, when: Instant) -> bool`

  Whether it began fading by `when`: an owner's timer, set going with a
  fade and waking `LENGTH` later, lets go of every fade this is true of,
  and leaves a later one to its own timer.

- `pub fn draw(&self, child: impl IntoElement) -> AnyElement`

  `child` fading out, composed until its owner lets it go, and drawn
  inert: the pointer passes through it to the window, its scrim
  included, and the keyboard never tabs into it, so the window takes
  input the moment it is put away.

### components::arrive

A picture arriving: a sleeve that fades in over what stood before it (the
record's colour, or the ensō drawing itself), drawn as a shader quad that
samples the picture, its opacity a track the GPU moves (#1859). The view
that shows it draws once as it arrives and once as it lands, to let go of
what stood beneath; no frame between.

#### `Arrival`

*struct* · One picture's arrival: its opacity moving from nothing to whole over
`length` from when it was made. Kept by whoever shows the picture, for as
long as it fades.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Arrival { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(length: Duration) -> Self`

  An arrival that begins now and lasts `length`.

- `pub fn since(start: Instant, length: Duration) -> Self`

  An arrival that began at `start`: a picture decoded a moment before
  the view that shows it heard of it.

- `pub fn lands(&self) -> Instant`

  When the picture stands whole: the moment to draw once more, without
  what stood beneath it.

- `pub fn landed(&self) -> bool`

  Whether it has finished, so its owner can draw the picture plainly.

#### `arriving`

*function* · `image` filling its bounds, fading in by `arrival`. Under Reduce Motion,
and where the renderer draws no shaders, it stands whole at once.

```rust
pub fn arriving(image: Arc<RenderImage>, arrival: &Arrival) -> AnyElement
```

### components::availability

Where a track's file is, or how its download is going: the mark every row
that says so draws, right-aligned in its slot so a column of them lines
up. The Mac's `AvailabilityMark`.

#### `RingGlide`

*type alias* · A running transfer's glide in the colour its ring is drawn in: a glide
carries its ring's colour, so a picked row's ring has its own.

```rust
pub type RingGlide = Rc<dyn Fn(Hsla, &mut App) -> Glide>;
```

#### `Availability`

*enum* · Where a track's file is, as `AvailabilityMark` draws it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub enum Availability {
    /// Waiting at the front of the downloads: nothing here yet.
    Pending,
    /// Waiting behind others: nothing here yet.
    Queued,
    Failed,
    /// Offline, with no file here: not a failure, and drawn apart from one.
    NotHere,
    /// Arriving: a ring filling as the bytes land, from the transfer's
    /// `Glide` (its share at the last report and its rate), reckoned on the
    /// GPU per present with no frame drawn; turning while the size is
    /// unknown.
    Transferring(RingGlide),
    Stored { on_server: bool, on_disk: bool },
}
```

- `pub fn help(&self) -> Option<&'static str>`

  What the mark means, for the row's tooltip, where it needs saying.

#### `AvailabilityMark`

*struct* · `fine`-sized, in `muted` with what is kept here in `ink`, a failure in
`bad` and the next download in the accent; a word in place of the glyph
when the look hides icons. On a picked row everything but a failure takes
`on_selection`.

A download waiting to start is the ring a running one fills, empty and
faint: the same family as a transfer, and nothing like the kept cloud, so
a column of queued tracks is never read as an album on this device.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct AvailabilityMark { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn id(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  Names the mark, so it says what it means in a tooltip.

- `pub fn new(state: Option<Availability>) -> Self`

  `None` draws an empty slot: a track with no file anywhere.

- `pub fn picked(self, picked: bool) -> Self`

  Drawn on a picked row's selection colours.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::bar_control

A control in a bar of glyphs, such as a player's shuffle, previous,
next and repeat: the glyph in `body`, or its word where the look hides
icons, 44 points wide to hit and as tall as the controls' row, but only
the glyph's width in the layout, so the bar sets the gaps between the
glyphs while the hit area reaches into them. One that toggles is `ink`
underlined in the accent while on and `muted` off. A press is answered
with a ring.

#### `BarControl`

*struct* · A control in a bar of glyphs: a glyph, or its word where icons are
hidden, with a finger's target.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct BarControl { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, icon: Icon, text: impl AsRef<str>, help: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `text` is its word where the look hides icons; `help` what its
  tooltip says, its shortcut included.

- `pub fn answer(self, answer: Answer) -> Self`

  What a press draws, where a ring would not say it: a skip's streak.

- `pub fn on(self, on: bool) -> Self`

  A control that toggles, and whether it is on.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press does.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `bar_item`

*function* · An item in a bar's trailing group (a badge, a menu's glyph) around
`base`: centred across a finger's height, in `meta`,
reaching half `TransportMetrics::trailing_gap` either side, and a glyph
alone further, to `HIT`. A glyph's reach can pass its neighbour's at
small gaps: each item blocks the ones drawn before it, so a press in the
overlap goes to one item, never two.

```rust
pub fn bar_item(base: Stateful<Div>, glyph_only: bool, look: &Look) -> Stateful<Div>
```

### components::bars

The playing bars: three bars standing as high as the music's low, middle
and high bands, beside whatever is playing.

Where the renderer draws shader quads (macOS), the bars are an
`AnimatedQuad` whose program draws them from the bands in its uniforms
(`BarLevels`, an `Animated` driven by values). A change of the bands is
presented at the next refresh with no frame drawn; the quad is painted
once and replayed with its row's cached view. Elsewhere the bars are three
quads per slot, painted over the window's content by whoever holds the
slots' places (`BarsPlace::paint`, inside `bars_overlay`).

What moves the bands is the app's: the element only asks, as it is laid
out, for the levels to draw (`PlayingBars::new`).

#### `FLOOR`

*constant* · How far a bar stands with nothing playing through it, so a still bar reads
as a mark rather than as nothing.

```rust
pub const FLOOR: f32 = 0.15;
```

#### `BARS`

*static* · The bars as a fragment program. Uniforms: 4..8 the colour, 8 a bar's
width and 9 the gap, in device pixels, 10 the floor; tracks 0..3 the
bands, each gliding to the analyser's latest frame.

```rust
pub static BARS: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-playing-bars",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float pitch = u[8] + u[9];
    int i = int(floor(position.x / pitch));
    if (i < 0 || i > 2 || position.x - float(i) * pitch > u[8]) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float top = size.y * (1.0 - max(koan_track(u, uint(i), time), u[10]));
    float cover = clamp(position.y - top + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    return float4(u[4], u[5], u[6], u[7] * cover);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let pitch = u[2].x + u[2].y;
    let i = i32(floor(position.x / pitch));
    if (i < 0 || i > 2 || position.x - f32(i) * pitch > u[2].x) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let top = size.y * (1.0 - max(koan_track(u, u32(i), time), u[2].z));
    let cover = clamp(position.y - top + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    return vec4<f32>(u[1].xyz, u[1].w * cover);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `BarLevels`

*struct* · One slot's bands, written from any thread as keyframes: the GPU glides
each bar to its latest level at the display's rate.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct BarLevels(_);
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  Levels at rest, all bars down.

- `pub fn hold(&self, bands: [f32; 3])`

  Each bar standing at its band, 0 to 1, low band to high.

- `pub fn glide(&self, bands: [f32; 3], over: std::time::Duration)`

  Each bar gliding from where it stands to its band over `over`: an
  analyser frame, reached as the next arrives.

- `pub fn target(&self, i: usize) -> f32`

  Where bar `i` is gliding to.

#### `BarsPlace`

*struct* · Where a slot was laid out and the clip it was laid out under, for painting
its bars as quads over the window's content.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct BarsPlace {
    pub window: AnyWindowHandle,
    /* private fields */
}
```

- `pub fn paint(&self, window: &mut Window, bands: [f32; 3], color: Hsla)`

  The bars at `bands` in `color`, as quads. Called from `bars_overlay`.

#### `PlayingBars`

*struct* · A slot of playing bars in `color`, `BarMetrics` in size. As it is laid
out, `attach` is told where, and returns the levels to draw as a shader,
or `None` where the bars are painted as quads (`BarsPlace::paint`). Keep
a slot's state under its element id (`window.use_keyed_state`).

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct PlayingBars { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(color: Hsla, attach: impl Fn(BarsPlace, &mut Window, &mut App) -> Option<BarLevels> + 'static) -> Self`

  Bars in `color`. `attach` is asked for the levels once the bars know
  where they are drawn; returning `None` draws them still.

- `pub fn prism(self, prism: Option<bool>) -> Self`

  Drawn as ribbons of the rainbow appearance's palette (brat's when
  `Some(true)`) rather than in `color`, where the bars are a shader.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `bars_overlay`

*function* · Covers its parent and runs `paint` after everything drawn before it: where
a window's root paints the bars that are quads.

```rust
pub fn bars_overlay(paint: impl Fn(&mut Window) + 'static) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `renderer_mark`

*function* · In the playing bars' place while the music plays on a renderer that is
handed the file itself: nothing passes through here to measure, so the
slot says where it plays rather than standing still, and its tooltip why.

```rust
pub fn renderer_mark(color: Hsla) -> impl IntoElement
```

### components::bars_feed

What moves the playing bars (`PlayingBars`): one feed of bands for the
whole app, read only while a bar is on screen in a window that shows.
With none, the source's stream is dropped, so whatever produces the
bands can park. A hidden window keeps its rows, and with them its slots,
so the windows' roots say when they hide and show (`bars_visibility`).
Nothing is polled: the source yields a frame and the follow, on the
background executor, wakes on it.

Where the renderer draws shaders, each slot is a shader quad, painted
once by its row and replayed with the row's cached view. The follow
writes the bands straight into the slots' levels (`BarLevels`), and each
change wakes the window to present its last frame again: a moving bar
costs a small encode, never a frame, and still bars cost nothing.

Elsewhere the bars are quads. A row draws an empty slot, which records
where it was laid out and under which clip, and each window's root paints
the bars over its content (`paint_bars`), so a frame of the bars redraws
the root alone, whose content is a cached view and is replayed.

The app says where the bands come from (`install_bars`): a stream with
an item for every frame of its analyser, the heights, 0 to 1, low band
to high, where they moved and `None` where they did not. Every frame
is a moment to stop at once nothing shows, and the gap a glide runs
over is the gap between frames.

#### `BandSource`

*type alias* · Where the bands come from: asked for a stream when a bar first shows,
which is dropped when none does.

```rust
pub type BandSource = Arc<
    dyn Fn(&mut App) -> BoxStream<'static, Option<[f32; 3]>> + Send + Sync,
>;
```

#### `BarsFeed`

*struct* · What a window's root observes: notified when that window's bars move,
where they are quads.

```rust
pub struct BarsFeed;
```

#### `install_bars`

*function* · Sets up the bars' feed, with the bands from `source`.

```rust
pub fn install_bars(source: BandSource, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `bars_feed`

*function* · The entity `window`'s root observes to paint its bars again; none where
the feed was not installed, as in an offscreen render.

```rust
pub fn bars_feed(window: WindowId, cx: &mut App) -> Option<Entity<BarsFeed>>
```

#### `bars_visibility`

*function* · `window` was hidden or shown: a hidden window's bars keep nothing
running, and one showing again starts them, since it replays its rows
rather than drawing them.

```rust
pub fn bars_visibility(window: WindowId, shows: bool, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `bars_prism`

*function* · `window`'s prism, fed the bands while it shows, or none to stop.

```rust
pub fn bars_prism(window: WindowId, prism: Option<Prism>, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `live_bars`

*function* · The playing bars in `color`, moved by the feed. Inside an element with
an id, which keys the slot's state.

```rust
pub fn live_bars(color: Hsla) -> PlayingBars
```

#### `paint_bars`

*function* · Paints this window's bars over everything drawn before it, where they
are quads. Drawn last by a window's root.

```rust
pub fn paint_bars(cx: &App) -> impl IntoElement + use<>
```

### components::button

#### `ButtonKind`

*enum* · A button's weight and shape. The rules for which a screen uses are the
spec's: one prominent action at most, compact for repeated and secondary
controls.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ButtonKind {
    /// The accent outline: what the screen is for.
    Prominent,
    /// The label in `ink`, no outline.
    Standard,
    /// As standard, smaller and tighter.
    Compact,
    /// Compact, in a `muted` outline: an action standing in a form row.
    Bordered,
    /// Underlined `muted` text: anything that goes somewhere.
    Link,
    /// `muted` text in a bar, `ink` under the pointer; `ink` at rest under
    /// touch.
    Text,
    /// A transport glyph, no outline, a 44-point target.
    Icon,
    /// Play/pause: a transport glyph in a square `ink` outline.
    IconOutlined,
    /// A glyph in a 36-point cell, no ground, answering in colour alone:
    /// `ink`; `strong` under the pointer; the accent pressed. Selected it
    /// rests at `strong` and the pointer takes it to the accent.
    Toolbar,
}
```

- `pub fn glyph_side(self, look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The side a glyph-only button of this kind draws at: its glyph, its
  padding either side and its outline, where it has one. What a layout
  around the button measures from, rather than copying the parts.

#### `Button`

*struct* · A button: its kind's weight and shape, a label or a glyph or both, and
its states (hover, press, focus, disabled, selected). Made with `new` or
`icon_only`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Button { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, kind: ButtonKind) -> Self`

  A button of `kind`, given its label or glyph by the methods below.

- `pub fn icon_only(id: impl Into<ElementId>, icon: Icon, help: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A glyph alone, which says what it does only in its tooltip: `help`
  names the action, with its keys where it has them ("back (⌘[)"). The
  way to make one, so none goes without.

- `pub fn icon_outlined(id: impl Into<ElementId>, icon: Icon, help: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `icon_only`, outlined: the transport's play button.

- `pub fn help(self, help: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The tooltip, in place of a glyph-only button's label, and on a button
  with words too: "back (⌘[)", as the Mac's buttons say.

- `pub fn label(self, text: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  The label, an app word, drawn lowercase. A glyph-only button keeps it
  for its tooltip.

- `pub fn text(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A label that holds data, a device's or a profile's name, which keeps
  its case: the caller lowercases the app's own words in it.

- `pub fn icon(self, icon: Icon) -> Self`

  The glyph: beside the label with "show icons" on, or alone in a glyph
  button whatever the setting.

- `pub fn tone(self, tone: Tone) -> Self`

  Its label and glyph in `tone` rather than the kind's: a favourite in
  the accent while it is on.

- `pub fn destructive(self, destructive: bool) -> Self`

  In `bad`, for a compact or bordered button that destroys something.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Drawn at 40 % and taking no press or focus.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Sized to `role` rather than the kind's type: a glyph button at
  `Meta` is the compact one, for a line of secondary controls. The hit
  area keeps the kind's least size.

- `pub fn selected(self, selected: bool) -> Self`

  A toolbar toggle's on state (the lyrics, the sidebar): the pressed fill.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press, Enter or Space on the focused button does.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::chain

A signal chain drawn down the page: where it starts and ends

accent while the chain is lit (a match, say) and as a rule otherwise.
kōan's EQ page draws a device's chain with it.
