### components::chart

A chart: axes, series, rules and points over a plot, drawn by the GPU
from keyframes, and interactive by pointer and by touch.

A page declares what to draw each time it renders: a `Chart` with an x
and a y `Axis` (linear or logarithmic, ticks chosen or given), `Series`
(lines, dashed or solid, and areas from a line to a level or to another
line), horizontal `Rule`s with an optional label, and `ChartPoint`s that
can be dragged within ranges. It compares what it is given with what it
drew and writes only what changed, as keyframes:

- **Data.** A series whose values change gets an image holding where it
  is drawn now and its new values (`data.rs`), and its phase track moves
  from one to the other: eased (`DataMotion::Ease`), interpolated over a
  sample interval and so drawn one sample behind (`DataMotion::Interpolate`),
  or drawn current and carried on along its last rate until the next
  sample corrects it (`DataMotion::Extrapolate`), or, for a series that
  takes one new sample at its right and drops its oldest, slid left by a
  sample over the interval as the newest enters from the right edge
  (`DataMotion::Scroll`). Samples arriving at their own rate are drawn at
  the display's.
- **Axes.** A range change eases each axis's affine map; every mark
  reads it as tracks, so the series, the grid, the points and the labels
  move together with no frame drawn.
- **Points.** A point moved by its owner settles where it was put; one
  being dragged stands under the pointer.

Only layout runs on the CPU: the ticks, which labels fit (`scale::thin`)
and the images, made again when the data, the plot's size or the range
changes. A chart at rest presents nothing, and under Reduce Motion every
change lands at once. Where the renderer draws no shaders the chart
paints its resting state as paths.

#### `DataMotion`

*enum* · How a series meets new values.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum DataMotion {
    /// Drawn at once.
    Instant,
    /// Eased from where it is drawn, over the length given.
    Ease(Duration),
    /// A sample every interval, each reached as the next arrives: drawn one
    /// interval behind, exactly through every sample.
    Interpolate(Duration),
    /// A sample every interval, drawn as it lands and carried on along the
    /// rate of the last two for one more interval; the next corrects it.
    Extrapolate(Duration),
    /// A sample every interval, evenly spaced in x, the newest at the right
    /// and the oldest dropped off the left, against an x axis that stays
    /// put: each new set slides the whole series left by one sample,
    /// linearly over the interval, as the newest enters from the right
    /// edge, rather than easing each value to its neighbour's. A series
    /// shorter than the axis stands at its right. Drawn as a smooth curve
    /// through the samples that rounds peaks off without passing beyond
    /// them.
    Scroll(Duration),
}
```

Implements `Default`.

#### `Ticks`

*enum* · Where an axis puts its ticks.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum Ticks {
    /// Round values: about one per 60 points across, or 40 up, for a
    /// linear axis; 1, 2 and 5 of each decade for a logarithmic one.
    Auto,
    /// Every multiple of this step.
    Every(f64),
    /// These values.
    At(Vec<f64>),
}
```

#### `Axis`

*struct* · One of a chart's axes: its scale, its range, and its ticks.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Axis { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn linear(lo: f64, hi: f64) -> Self`

  An axis from `lo` to `hi`, evenly spaced.

- `pub fn log(lo: f64, hi: f64) -> Self`

  An axis from `lo` to `hi` on a logarithmic scale, as frequency is.

- `pub fn ticks(self, ticks: Ticks) -> Self`

  Where the ticks fall, rather than chosen from the range.

- `pub fn format(self, format: impl Fn(f64) -> String + 'static) -> Self`

  How a tick's label reads; by default its value to the tick step's
  precision, thousands as `k` on a logarithmic axis.

- `pub fn no_grid(self) -> Self`

  No rule across the plot at each tick.

- `pub fn no_labels(self) -> Self`

  Ticks with no labels.

#### `Base`

*enum* · Where an area is filled to.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]

    /// A level on the y axis.

    /// Another line, one value for each of the series' samples.

}
```

#### `Series`

*struct* · A series of values against x, drawn as a line or as an area.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]

```

- `pub fn line(id: impl Into<SharedString>, xs: impl Into<Arc<[f64]>>, ys: impl Into<Arc<[f64]>>) -> Self`

  A line through `ys` at `xs`, which rise. Solid, 1.5 points, in ink.

- `pub fn area(id: impl Into<SharedString>, xs: impl Into<Arc<[f64]>>, ys: impl Into<Arc<[f64]>>) -> Self`

  The area between the line through `ys` and the level 0.

- `pub fn width(self, points: f32) -> Self`

  A line's width, in points.

  A line's dash: on and off lengths in points, up to two pairs. Empty
  is solid.

  An area filled to `base` rather than to 0.

- `pub fn color(self, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  In `color` rather than the next of the chart's colours.

- `pub fn motion(self, motion: DataMotion) -> Self`

  How a change of its data moves.

#### `Chart`

*struct* · The chart. See the module's description.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Chart { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, x: Axis, y: Axis) -> Self`

  A chart over `x` and `y`, given its series, rules and points by the
  methods.

- `pub fn series(self, series: impl IntoIterator<Item = Series>) -> Self`

  Drawn in order, each over the last.

- `pub fn rules(self, rules: impl IntoIterator<Item = Rule>) -> Self`

  Drawn under the series.

- `pub fn points(self, points: impl IntoIterator<Item = ChartPoint>) -> Self`

  Drawn over the series.

- `pub fn height(self, height: Pixels) -> Self`

  The plot's height with its x labels; it is as wide as its container.

- `pub fn fill(self) -> Self`

  As tall as its container leaves it, legend included: a chart in a
  frame that folds or a pane that grows. The container's height must
  be definite.

- `pub fn legend(self, legend: Legend) -> Self`

  A legend under the plot.

- `pub fn y_gutter(self, width: Pixels) -> Self`

  The y labels' column, rather than as wide as its widest label.

- `pub fn on_press(self, f: impl Fn(&ChartDrag, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A press on the plot, on a point within reach or on the plot itself.

- `pub fn on_step(self, f: impl Fn(&ChartDrag, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Each move of a drag. A point dragged is drawn where its owner puts
  it on the next render, so the owner moves it.

- `pub fn on_release(self, f: impl Fn(&ChartDrag, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  The drag's end: let go, or the button seen up.

- `pub fn report_points(self, places: PointPlaces) -> Self`

  Where each point is drawn, written to `places` every frame, for a page
  that lays something out beside them.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::collection

A page of different kinds of thing answering one question (what was
favourited, played lately, downloaded, or matches a search): the Mac's
`MixedCollection`. Sections stacked under the page's header, each with its
heading: pills wrapping left to right, record tiles in as many columns as
fit, rows the full width.

Sections rather than a picker of kinds, for the reason the Mac gives:
they are all answers to one question, and a mode to remember is a worse
way to find a record.

The collection owns the layout: the margins, the space between sections,
the pills' flow, the tiles' columns and gaps. The owner gives it the
header, each section's heading and its items, and reads the tile grid it
should build tiles for from the collection's `CollectionWidth`, which the
collection measures and keeps up to date. It scrolls as one column, for
the previews it is built for; a listing too long to lay out whole is the
browsers' job.

#### `CollectionWidth`

*struct* · The width a collection was last laid out at, for the tile grid its owner
builds tiles for. Held by the owning view; the collection notifies that
view when the width changes.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct CollectionWidth(_);
```

- `pub fn grid(&self, look: &Look) -> Option<TileGrid>`

  The grid the collection lays tiles out on, once it has been measured.

#### `CollectionSection`

*struct* · One kind of thing in a collection, under its heading.

```rust
pub struct CollectionSection { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn pills(heading: SectionHeading, pills: impl IntoIterator<Item = Pill>) -> Self`

  Things named as chips (`Pill`s), wrapping.

- `pub fn tiles(heading: SectionHeading, grid: TileGrid, tiles: impl IntoIterator<Item = AnyElement>) -> Self`

  Record tiles on `grid`, each `grid.side` wide and `grid.tile_height()`
  tall, in rows.

- `pub fn rows(heading: SectionHeading, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = AnyElement>) -> Self`

  Table rows, the full width; each row insets itself by the page's
  margin, as `TableRow` does.

#### `Collection`

*struct* · Sections of pills or tiles in rows that wrap, under an optional page
header, with an empty state when there are no sections.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Collection { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, width: &CollectionWidth) -> Self`

  A collection at `width`, the page's measure of how many fit a row.

- `pub fn under_bar(self) -> Self`

  A page that scrolls under a phone's bar and its foot
  (`ScrollArea::under_bar`).

- `pub fn under_foot(self) -> Self`

  A page held clear of a phone's bar that scrolls under its foot alone
  (`ScrollArea::under_foot`).

- `pub fn follow(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Brings the page's keyboard cursor into view as the keys move it,
  wherever it is laid out: a pill in its flow, a tile in its row.

- `pub fn header(self, header: PageHeader) -> Self`

  The page header above the sections.

- `pub fn section(self, section: CollectionSection) -> Self`

  A section; one with nothing in it is left out, heading and all.

- `pub fn empty(self, empty: EmptyState) -> Self`

  What the page says when no section has anything in it.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::command_bar

A search over everything, centred over the window: Arc's and Dia's
command bars. The query large across the top, what it finds under it in
headed groups of `SuggestionRow`s, the one lit by the arrows on
`surface` and kept in view, and along the foot what the keys do. The
window behind is dimmed and takes no clicks; a click on it closes the
bar.

The bar is two parts its owner draws apart: the query's field
(`CommandBar::field`) and what was found (`CommandResults`). An owner
keeps each in a view of its own, as a filter field is kept beside the
listing it narrows, so a keystroke draws the field and nothing else, and
the results are drawn only when an answer lands. The owner holds the
query's `InputState`, decides what is found and what each key does.

#### `CommandSection`

*struct* · A group of results under its heading.

```rust
pub struct CommandSection {
    pub title: SharedString,
    pub rows: Vec<SuggestionRow>,
}
```

#### `CommandBar`

*struct* · The command bar over the window: the owner's field and the results
under it, closed by a press on the dimmed window behind.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct CommandBar { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, field: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `field` is the query's field as `CommandBar::field` draws it, or a
  view drawing it.

- `pub fn field(input: &Entity<InputState>) -> CommandField`

  The query's field: bare, in `title small`, named "search" for a
  screen reader, with a clear mark at its trailing edge while it holds
  text, as a filter field has. The owner runs the same clear from a key
  with `FieldState::clear`.

- `pub fn field_height(window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Pixels`

  How tall the field is: what a view drawing it, cached, is sized to.

- `pub fn results(self, results: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  What was found and the keys' foot, as `CommandResults` draws them,
  or a view drawing them, cached in the room under the query
  (`flex_1`, `min_h_0`, `w_full`).

- `pub fn on_dismiss(self, handler: impl Fn(&(), &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A click on the dimmed window around the bar.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `CommandField`

*struct* · The command bar's query field, as `CommandBar::field` makes it: the
text, and the clear mark while there is any. Escape is the owner's.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct CommandField { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn label(self, text: &str) -> Self`

  What a screen reader names it, in the app's words, in place of
  "search": what is searched.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Its text and mark in `role` rather than `title small`. A view
  drawing it cached is sized to that role's line, not
  `CommandBar::field_height`.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Not editable, faded and with no clear mark: while what it feeds is
  under way.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `CommandResults`

*struct* · What the bar found, under its query: headed groups of rows, scrolling
between the query and the keys when they outgrow the panel, and along the
foot what the keys do.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct CommandResults { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  Results with no sections yet.

- `pub fn last(self, row: SuggestionRow) -> Self`

  The row after every section, under no heading: what goes further
  than the list does ("All results…"). Shown only with results.

- `pub fn section(self, title: impl AsRef<str>, rows: Vec<SuggestionRow>) -> Self`

  A group of results; one with none is left out.

- `pub fn lit(self, lit: Option<usize>) -> Self`

  The row the keyboard is on, counted across the sections.

- `pub fn key(self, keys: impl Into<SharedString>, verb: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A key and what it does, along the foot, in the app's words.

- `pub fn empty(self, text: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  What it says when no section has a row.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::control_group

Controls side by side on one rhythm, each a touch target wide.

#### `ControlGroup`

*struct* · Controls side by side, each in a slot exactly a touch target wide (`HIT`)
with none between, so the targets touch and the glyphs, whatever their
widths, sit an even step apart with no gap of their own. A slot never
grows with its control, so the group keeps one width whatever the
controls show and what sits beside it keeps its room. A press in a
slot is the control's: it does not reach what the group sits in, such
as a bar that opens on a tap. It never shrinks.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement, Default)]
pub struct ControlGroup { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  An empty group.

- `pub fn control(self, control: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A control after those already given.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::copy

Copying a value: a button that says what it copies ("copy link"), and a

every button's does), keeping that moment itself: a page holds no state

### components::cover

A cover: a record's sleeve, an artist's photo, one tile of a playlist's
face. Square unless asked otherwise, filled edge to edge, and while
there is no picture the `Placeholder`. What the picture is comes from the
caller as `CoverArt`; koan-kit knows nothing of where it was fetched.

#### `CoverArt`

*enum* · What a cover shows.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub enum CoverArt {
    /// The picture, decoded at the size it is drawn.
    Image(Arc<RenderImage>),
    /// The picture's average colour, standing in while its bitmap uploads:
    /// it already carries the record.
    Colour(Hsla),
    /// Still being fetched or decoded: the placeholder's ensō drawing
    /// itself, on the very mark `Missing` shows.
    Loading,
    /// No picture.
    Missing,
}
```

#### `Cover`

*struct* · A sleeve at a size, or the placeholder while there is none.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Cover { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(art: CoverArt, side: Pixels) -> Self`

  `art` as a square of `side`.

- `pub fn id(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  Names the cover, so a picture that arrives after it showed loading
  fades in over the spinner rather than replacing it.

- `pub fn sized(art: CoverArt, size: Size<Pixels>) -> Self`

  A tile of a mosaic: a cover cut to `size`, its middle kept.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Mosaic`

*struct* · A playlist's face: its first four records, square and edge to edge. One
fills the square, two split it down the middle, three put the third
across the foot; an empty playlist is a plain square of `rule`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]

```

- `pub fn new(arts: Vec<CoverArt>, side: Pixels) -> Self`

  Up to four sleeves in a square of `side`, as a playlist's cover.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::dense

A dense table, as a sheet holds one: an EQ's bands, a curve's points.
Columns are declared once, fixed or flexing, aligned at their start or
their end, in points that grow with the text size; the header and every
row lay their cells out on them, so a figure lines up down the table
whatever its row holds. A cell takes one column or spans several, and a
row may end in a trailing cell (an options menu) past the columns.

#### `Align`

*enum* · Where a dense table's cell sits in its column.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Align {
    Start,
    End,
}
```

#### `Column`

*struct* · A column of a `DenseTable`: fixed at a width or taking what is left.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Column { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn fixed(points: f32, align: Align) -> Self`

  `points` wide at 100 % text, its cells aligned as `align` says.

- `pub fn flex() -> Self`

  Taking the width the fixed columns leave, its cells at its start.

#### `DenseRow`

*struct* · A row's cells: each takes one column, or `span` of them.

```rust
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct DenseRow { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A row with no cells yet.

- `pub fn cell(self, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A cell in the next column.

- `pub fn span(self, columns: usize, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  One cell across `columns` columns.

- `pub fn trailing(self, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Past the columns, at the row's end.

#### `DenseTable`

*struct* · A dense table for an editor: columns of fixed or flexible width, an
optional header, and tight rows of cells.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct DenseTable { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(columns: impl Into<Rc<[Column]>>) -> Self`

  A table of `columns`.

- `pub fn header(self, titles: impl IntoIterator<Item = impl Into<SharedString>>) -> Self`

  The columns' titles, in `fine`, `muted`, as written: they are units
  ("Hz", "dB") as often as words.

- `pub fn trailing_width(self, width: Pixels) -> Self`

  Room at every row's end for a trailing cell `width` wide, the
  header's and those without one included, so the columns line up down
  the table whichever rows have one.

- `pub fn row(self, row: DenseRow) -> Self`

  A row, under the last.

- `pub fn rows(self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = DenseRow>) -> Self`

  Rows, under the last.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::drag

What a drag shows: the chip under the pointer, saying what is carried,
and the target lit while a drag it accepts is over it. A target lights
only for the payloads it takes, so a drag over something that refuses it
shows nothing. Between rows, where a drop lands is the list's insertion
line, `INSERTION`, in the accent. Under touch a drag entering a target
is felt as a tick, and a drop that lands as a tap (`landed`).

#### `INSERTION`

*constant* · The insertion line's thickness: a hairline, doubled to read as a place.

```rust
pub const INSERTION: Pixels = px(2.);
```

#### `DragChip`

*struct* · The drag's preview: the first record's sleeve, when there is one, and
what is carried ("3 tracks", or the one thing's name).

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct DragChip { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(label: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A chip naming what is carried.

- `pub fn sleeve(self, sleeve: CoverArt) -> Self`

  The first record's sleeve beside the name.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `DropTarget`

*trait* · A drop target lit while a drag of `T` is over it, and felt as the drag
enters it.

```rust
pub trait DropTarget: InteractiveElement + Sized {
    fn drop_highlight<T: 'static>(self, look: &Look) -> Self;
}
```

#### `landed`

*function* · A drop that landed, felt as a light tap. Every drop handler that takes
what it is given calls it; one that refuses stays still.

```rust

```

### components::empty

#### `EmptyState`

*struct* · An empty page: the place's glyph in `muted`, a title in `body`, `ink`,
and what to do about it in `meta`, `muted`, centred. In an area too small
for it (a narrow column, the lyrics beside a small window) it is drawn
smaller, down to half size, and past that clipped to the area rather than
drawn over what is beside it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct EmptyState { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(icon: Icon, title: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  An empty state with the place's `icon` and `title`, an app word drawn
  lowercase.

- `pub fn warning(self) -> Self`

  Something went wrong rather than nothing being there: the glyph in
  `bad`, as the Mac's startup error draws its warning.

- `pub fn in_panel(self) -> Self`

  In a panel that already stands clear of the window's foot (the
  lyrics between the toolbar and the transport): centred in the panel,
  with no room left for the foot again.

- `pub fn id(self, id: impl Into<gpui::ElementId>) -> Self`

  The id it keeps its measured area under, its title unless given.
  Two empty states with one title side by side each need their own,
  or they share one area.

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A sentence, which keeps its case.

- `pub fn action(self, action: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Something quiet to do about it, under the words.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::fade

What arrives after its page (a photograph, a biography) fading in rather
than appearing, over `Motion::NORMAL` on the quick-out curve; with Reduce
Motion or Low Power Mode it appears. `Reveal` also makes room for it: the
slot grows from nothing to its size, moving what is beside it along.

Where the renderer draws shaders, what they hold is an `AnimatedLayer`:
drawn once into a texture and faded or revealed by its tracks on the GPU,
with no frame drawn while it moves (#1859). A reveal's slot takes its full
size at once, so what is beside it moves along in one step while its
content is revealed. Elsewhere both run on GPUI frames for their length.

The wrapper takes no size of its own: in a row it is as wide as what it
holds, in a column as wide as the column, so the element lays out exactly
as it would unwrapped.

#### `FadeIn`

*struct* · `child` fading in when its id changes, and out when `leaving`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct FadeIn { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `id` names the arrival: the fade runs once per id.

- `pub fn leaving(self, leaving: bool) -> Self`

  Fading back out, on the GPU, for its owner to stop drawing it `LEAVE`
  later; told otherwise before then, it comes back from where it got to.

- `pub fn fill(self) -> Self`

  As large as what holds it, for content that fills its parent (a
  pane, a centred sleeve) rather than sizing to itself.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Reveal`

*struct* · A slot of `size` that, when `arriving`, grows from nothing to its size
over `Motion::NORMAL`, what is beside it moving along to make room, while
what it holds fades in. Not arriving (present when the page first drew),
it stands at its size. What it holds is laid out at its full size and
clipped while the slot grows, never squeezed.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Reveal { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, size: Size<Pixels>, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `id` names the arrival: it runs once per id.

- `pub fn arriving(self, arriving: bool) -> Self`

  Fading in, rather than standing as it is.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::field

kōan's text fields: `TextField` for a line, `TextArea` for several,
both drawing a `FieldState` the page owns.

The editing is the platform's: an input method composes in place
(GPUI's `EntityInputHandler`), the selection takes clicks, drags, Shift
with the arrows and double and triple clicks, words and lines move as
the Mac's fields move them, the clipboard and undo work, a line too long
for the field scrolls to keep the caret in view, and a screen reader
hears a text field with its value and selection. The look is kōan's:
the type and ground of a `control`, no bezel and no focus ring (the
caret says where typing goes), the selection the accent at 30 %, the
placeholder in `muted`, and the caret a shader quad whose blink draws
nothing.

#### `AreaState`

*type alias* · What a `TextArea` edits: a `FieldState` made with `.rows(n)`.

```rust
pub type AreaState = FieldState;
```

#### `TextField`

*struct* · A text field: `control` type in `ink` on a square `surface` field with no
bezel, or on nothing at all without `ground`, as the sidebar's search is.
As on the Mac, a field draws no focus ring: the caret shows where typing
goes, and the field answers taking the keyboard with a line drawn under
it. The owner makes the state
(`cx.new(|cx| FieldState::new(window, cx))`), which carries the
placeholder, drawn in `muted`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct TextField { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(state: &Entity<FieldState>) -> Self`

  A field over `state`, which its owner keeps and listens to.

- `pub fn width(self, width: FieldWidth) -> Self`

  Drawn as wide as what it takes (`FieldWidth`).

- `pub fn label(self, label: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What a screen reader names it, where no visible label beside it
  does: "search", "name". The placeholder is read as a hint after it.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Not editable, and faded: while what it feeds is under way.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Its text in `role` rather than `control`: a search box's large
  query.

- `pub fn bare(self) -> Self`

  No field of its own, on the page's ground.

- `pub fn cell(self) -> Self`

  Bare, as a figure in a table's row: under touch it takes a tap
  anywhere across a finger's height, and a tap there keeps the
  keyboard, rather than its line of text alone.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `FieldWidth`

*enum* · How wide a field is drawn for what it takes, on the size scale so it
grows with the text; without one it takes the width it is given.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FieldWidth {
    /// A short figure: a year. 64 points.
    Figure,
    /// A number of a few digits: a port, a size. 120 points.
    Number,
    /// A name: a username. 200 points.
    Name,
}
```

#### `TextArea`

*struct* · Several lines of text pasted or typed (a measurement's rows): `control`
type in `ink` on a square `surface` ground, as a `TextField`, the state
made with `.rows(n)` for its height in lines.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct TextArea { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(state: &Entity<AreaState>) -> Self`

  A field of several lines over `state`, which its owner keeps.

- `pub fn label(self, label: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What a screen reader names it, as `TextField::label`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `labelled`

*function* · `child`, with `label` naming any text field inside it that has no label
of its own: a form row's label reaching its control, for a screen

its parent lays it out, inside this element's layout.

```rust
pub fn labelled(label: impl Into<SharedString>, child: impl IntoElement) -> Labelled
```

#### `Labelled`

*struct* · What `labelled` makes: its child, with the label its text fields take.


### components::filter_field

The toolbar's filter field, the sidebar's search field's look: no box,
the glyph and a lowercase prompt in `muted`, on the page's ground. With
text in it, a clear mark at its trailing edge (the Mac's), `muted` and
`ink` under the pointer, empties it and keeps the keyboard there; Escape
does the same, and with the field empty goes on to whatever Escape means
around it. The caller owns the `InputState` and listens to it: emptying
the field is a change like any other. What was cleared fades out
leftward for a moment, a layer the GPU moves, so the clear is seen.

#### `FilterField`

a clear mark while it holds text.

  What a screen reader calls the field, in the app's words: "filter"

Implements `RenderOnce`.

  What a press anywhere on it does: usually opening the search it stands

#### `FieldLead`

*struct* · What leads a phone's list: its filter or search field, at the page's

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::follow

Motion a finger drives, on the composition layer: what it moves is drawn
once into a layer, each gesture event writes the layer's offset as a held
track, and the release hands the GPU the rest of the way as a segment. No
frame is drawn while the finger moves or the motion settles; the owner
draws again only when the layout changes, as the layer is mounted or let
go.

- `Follow`: a layer offset by a finger and released to a target at the
  finger's speed (Now Playing pulled down, a queue row swiped).
- `Speed`: how fast a finger moves, read from its steps, for the release.
- `SwipeAction`: a row slid toward the leading edge over an action that
  fills the room it leaves, both moved by one `Follow`.
- `Elastic`: a page's ends giving under a finger's pan or a trackpad's
  scroll and bouncing a fling; with a `Refresh`, its top pulled past a
  threshold and let go asks its owner to refresh.
- `Push`: a navigation stack's page sliding over the one under it, which
  shows a third of its way behind and dimmed, by one progress track (a
  page pushed, swiped back, or sent back by the bar's button).

Every release carries on at the speed the finger let go at, landing at
rest or leaving at its pace (`Follow::release`, `Follow::fling`,
`Push::release`, the rubber band's spring), never starting again from
rest.

Under Reduce Motion a finger is still followed, and a release lands at
once: the settle returns no wait.

#### `settle_length`

*function* · How long a settle started now takes: `Motion::SETTLE`, or nothing where
it lands at once. The owner lets the layer go after it.

```rust
pub fn settle_length(cx: &App) -> Duration
```

#### `Follow`

*struct* · A layer offset by a finger: tracks 1 and 2 of a standing `LAYER`, in
device pixels. Clones share it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Follow(_);
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A layer standing in place.

- `pub fn follow(&self, by: Point<Pixels>, window: &Window)`

  The offset standing at `by`, as a gesture event reports it.

- `pub fn settle(&self, to: Point<Pixels>, window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  The offset easing from where it stands to `to`, on the quick-out over
  `Motion::SETTLE`; how long that takes.

- `pub fn release(&self, to: Point<Pixels>, velocity: Point<f32>, window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  The offset carried from where it stands to `to` by a release moving
  at `velocity` (points a second, as `Speed` reads it), landing at
  rest: it starts at the finger's speed rather than from rest, and
  slows into place without passing it. How long it takes; nothing under
  Reduce Motion, which puts it at `to` at once.

- `pub fn fling(&self, to: Point<Pixels>, velocity: Point<f32>, window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  `release`, for a layer leaving: it goes as fast as it was thrown and
  ends at its average pace, never slowing as it goes off.

- `pub fn at(&self) -> Point<f32>`

  Where the offset stands now, in device pixels.

- `pub fn layer(&self, child: impl IntoElement) -> AnimatedLayer`

  `child` drawn as the layer the offset moves; composed at rest too,
  since a finger holds it still away from its place. Mounted only while
  a finger or a settle moves it.

- `pub fn animated(&self) -> &Animated`

  The uniforms the layer reads, for an element that draws it.

Implements `Default`.

#### `Speed`

*struct* · How fast a finger moves along one axis, from the steps it reports: what
its release carries into the motion it starts. Smoothed over the last few
steps; a finger that stood still before lifting threw nothing.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Speed { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A finger landing: no speed yet.

- `pub fn step(&mut self, delta: f32)`

  A step of `delta` (points, or any unit the speed is read in) now.
  A step that moves nothing, as a lift usually is, changes nothing.

- `pub fn get(&self) -> f32`

  The speed, in its unit a second; none once the finger has stood
  still for a moment.

Implements `Default`.

#### `Refresh`

*struct* · What a page's top, pulled past a threshold and let go, asks of its
owner, given to `Elastic::refresh` each time the owner draws.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Refresh {
    /// Where the gap the page leaves begins, from the page's top: the room
    /// a bar drawn over the page takes.
    pub top: Pixels,
    /// The owner is refreshing: the gap shows a spinner, and a page let go
    /// past the threshold stays open until this ends.
    pub busy: bool,
    /// The ring's and the spinner's colour.
    pub color: Hsla,
    /// Starts the refresh. Not called while `busy`.
    pub start: RefreshStart,
}
```

#### `RefreshStart`

*type alias* · What starts a `Refresh`.

```rust
pub type RefreshStart = std::rc::Rc<dyn Fn(&mut Window, &mut App)>;
```

#### `Elastic`

*struct* · A page whose ends give, as a UIKit scroll view's: a vertical pan the page
has no more of to show pulls the page off its place, less the further it
goes, and a fling that reaches an end bounces. The whole page is the
layer the GPU moves, so a list of any kind has it without knowing, and
nothing is drawn while it moves. One per stage; the page goes in through
`wrap`.

A finger on a touchscreen and a trackpad's scroll (AppKit's phases, with
precise deltas) give it; a mouse wheel does not. A list's scroll reaches
the page whether or not the list took it, so on a trackpad the
`Scrollbar` over the list says whether the list stood at an end.

Given a `Refresh`, the top pulled past `PULL` and let go starts it, and
the page stays open by `HELD` with a spinner until the owner says it is
no longer busy. The gap shows a ring filling as the pull nears the
threshold, drawn on the GPU from the page's offset.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Elastic { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A page's edges at rest, with no refresh.

- `pub fn wrap(&self, page: impl IntoElement, window: &mut Window, _: &mut App) -> AnyElement`

  `page`, its ends giving.

- `pub fn refresh(&self, page: u64, refresh: Option<Refresh>, window: &Window, cx: &mut App)`

  What the top of `page` (the owner's key for the page showing)
  refreshes, or nothing: given each time the owner draws, with whether
  it is busy. A page held open closes once its owner has been busy and
  is no longer, or was never busy within `ASKED` of being asked, or no
  longer refreshes; another page showing drops it at once.

- `pub fn swap_travelled(&self, travelled: f32) -> f32`

  Swaps what the finger has moved the page by for `travelled`: the
  page showing has changed, and this was the last one's.

- `pub fn scrolled(&self) -> bool`

  Whether the page has been scrolled since it last stood at its top: a
  rewind has somewhere to come from.

- `pub fn rewound(&self, over: Pixels, view: EntityId, window: &Window, cx: &mut App)`

  Back at its top by a jump: the page comes the last part of the way as
  a scroll arriving there would, on the GPU. `over` is how far, in
  points. Nothing happens if it had not been scrolled.

Implements `Default`.

#### `PULL_RING`

*static* · The ring in the gap over a page pulled from its top: it rides in the
middle of the gap and fills from twelve o'clock as the pull nears the
threshold. Track 2 is the page's offset in device pixels, `u[0]` the
threshold and `u[1]` the ring's side, both in device pixels, `u[8..12]`
the colour. What the page covers is not drawn.

```rust
pub static PULL_RING: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-pull-ring",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float offset = koan_track(u, 2, time);
    if (position.y > offset) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float share = clamp(offset / max(u[0], 1.0), 0.0, 1.0);
    float side = u[1];
    float stroke = side * 0.125;
    float2 d = position - float2(size.x * 0.5, min(offset, size.y) * 0.5);
    float radius = (side - stroke) * 0.5;
    float edge = clamp(stroke * 0.5 - abs(length(d) - radius) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    if (edge <= 0.0) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float a = atan2(d.x, -d.y) / 6.28318530718;
    if (a < 0.0) {
        a += 1.0;
    }
    float shown = clamp((share - a) * side * 3.14159265, 0.0, 1.0);
    float strength = share >= 1.0 ? 1.0 : 0.3 + 0.4 * share;
    return float4(u[8], u[9], u[10], u[11] * edge * shown * strength);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let offset = koan_track(u, 2u, time);
    if (position.y > offset) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let share = clamp(offset / max(u[0].x, 1.0), 0.0, 1.0);
    let side = u[0].y;
    let stroke = side * 0.125;
    let d = position - vec2<f32>(size.x * 0.5, min(offset, size.y) * 0.5);
    let radius = (side - stroke) * 0.5;
    let edge = clamp(stroke * 0.5 - abs(length(d) - radius) + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    if (edge <= 0.0) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    var a = atan2(d.x, -d.y) / 6.28318530718;
    if (a < 0.0) {
        a += 1.0;
    }
    let shown = clamp((share - a) * side * 3.14159265, 0.0, 1.0);
    var strength = 0.3 + 0.4 * share;
    if (share >= 1.0) {
        strength = 1.0;
    }
    return vec4<f32>(u[2].xyz, u[2].w * edge * shown * strength);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `SWIPE_ACTION`

*static* · What fills the room a row slid toward the leading edge leaves: drawn
whole under the row and shown only on the strip the row uncovers, moved
so that its middle stays in the middle of that strip. Track 1 is the
row's offset in device pixels, zero or less.

```rust
pub static SWIPE_ACTION: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-swipe-action",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float dx = koan_track(u, 1, time);
    float edge = size.x + dx;
    if (position.x < edge) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float2 uv = (position - float2(edge * 0.5, 0.0)) / size;
    if (any(uv < 0.0) || any(uv > 1.0)) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float4 c = koan_sample(image, tile, uv);
    float3 rgb = c.a > 0.0 ? c.rgb / c.a : float3(0.0);
    return float4(rgb, c.a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let dx = koan_track(u, 1u, time);
    let edge = size.x + dx;
    if (position.x < edge) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let uv = (position - vec2<f32>(edge * 0.5, 0.0)) / size;
    if (any(uv < vec2<f32>(0.0)) || any(uv > vec2<f32>(1.0))) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let c = koan_sample(tile, uv);
    var rgb = vec3<f32>(0.0);
    if (c.a > 0.0) {
        rgb = c.rgb / c.a;
    }
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, c.a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `SwipeAction`

*struct* · A row a finger slides toward the leading edge, over `action`, which is
laid out over the row's whole place (absolute, inset to it) and shows on
the strip the row uncovers. Both move by `follow`'s horizontal offset.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct SwipeAction { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(follow: &Follow, row: impl IntoElement, action: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  `row` over `action`, both moved by `follow`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `PUSH_FRONT`

*static* · The page in front: drawn over the stage, moved toward the trailing edge
by track 0, the push's progress (0 in place, 1 gone).

```rust
pub static PUSH_FRONT: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-push-front",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float progress = koan_track(u, 0, time);
    float2 uv = (position - float2(size.x * progress, 0.0)) / size;
    if (any(uv < 0.0) || any(uv > 1.0)) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float4 c = koan_sample(image, tile, uv);
    float3 rgb = c.a > 0.0 ? c.rgb / c.a : float3(0.0);
    return float4(rgb, c.a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let progress = koan_track(u, 0u, time);
    let uv = (position - vec2<f32>(size.x * progress, 0.0)) / size;
    if (any(uv < vec2<f32>(0.0)) || any(uv > vec2<f32>(1.0))) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let c = koan_sample(tile, uv);
    var rgb = vec3<f32>(0.0);
    if (c.a > 0.0) {
        rgb = c.rgb / c.a;
    }
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, c.a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `PUSH_UNDER`

*static* · The page under: shown on the strip the page in front uncovers alone, a
third of its way behind, under a veil (uniforms 0 to 3, its colour at
rest) that fades as the page in front goes when uniform 4 is set.

```rust
pub static PUSH_UNDER: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-push-under",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float progress = koan_track(u, 0, time);
    if (position.x > size.x * progress) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float2 uv = (position + float2(size.x * 0.3 * (1.0 - progress), 0.0)) / size;
    float4 c = float4(0.0);
    if (all(uv >= 0.0) && all(uv <= 1.0)) {
        c = koan_sample(image, tile, uv);
    }
    float veil = u[3] * (u[4] > 0.5 ? 1.0 - progress : 1.0);
    // The veil over the page, both premultiplied.
    float3 rgb = float3(u[0], u[1], u[2]) * veil + c.rgb * (1.0 - veil);
    float a = veil + c.a * (1.0 - veil);
    return float4(a > 0.0 ? rgb / a : float3(0.0), a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let progress = koan_track(u, 0u, time);
    if (position.x > size.x * progress) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let uv = (position + vec2<f32>(size.x * 0.3 * (1.0 - progress), 0.0)) / size;
    var c = vec4<f32>(0.0);
    if (all(uv >= vec2<f32>(0.0)) && all(uv <= vec2<f32>(1.0))) {
        c = koan_sample(tile, uv);
    }
    var veil = u[0].w;
    if (u[1].x > 0.5) {
        veil = veil * (1.0 - progress);
    }
    let rgb = u[0].xyz * veil + c.rgb * (1.0 - veil);
    let a = veil + c.a * (1.0 - veil);
    var straight = vec3<f32>(0.0);
    if (a > 0.0) {
        straight = rgb / a;
    }
    return vec4<f32>(straight, a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `Push`

*struct* · A navigation stack's push: one progress track both pages read, 0 with
the page in front in place and 1 with it gone past the trailing edge.
Clones share it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Push(_);
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A push at rest, the page in front in place.

- `pub fn moves(cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether a push moves at all: where it would land at once, its owner
  goes straight to where it ends.

- `pub fn follow(&self, progress: f32)`

  The progress standing where a finger has it.

- `pub fn settle(&self, to: f32, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  The progress easing from where it stands to `to`; how long that
  takes.

- `pub fn release(&self, to: f32, velocity: f32, width: Pixels, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  The progress carried from where it stands to `to` by a release moving
  at `velocity` (progress a second), over a page `width` points wide:
  started at the finger's speed and landing at rest. How long it takes.

- `pub fn enter(&self, cx: &App) -> Duration`

  A page coming in from the trailing edge over the one left; how long
  that takes.

- `pub fn at(&self) -> f32`

  Where the progress stands now.

- `pub fn front(&self, child: impl IntoElement) -> AnimatedLayer`

  The page in front, laid out over the whole stage.

- `pub fn under(&self, child: impl IntoElement, veil: Hsla, fades: bool) -> AnimatedLayer`

  The page under, laid out over the whole stage, under `veil` (its
  colour with the page in front in place), which fades as that page
  goes when `fades`.

Implements `Default`.

### components::foot_card

### components::form

The form: the Mac's `KoanForm` with its sections and rows. The container
decides every gap: the page's padding, the space between sections, a
header's space above, the label column and the space between rows. A
pane declares sections and rows and nothing about where they sit.

On the Mac a form is a column of sections on the ground, no cards or
boxes, each row its label in a fixed column and what it holds after it.

#### `Form`

*struct* · A form: sections down a column that scrolls, padded from the window.

```rust
pub struct Form { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  A form with no sections yet.

- `pub fn section(self, section: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A section, under the last.

- `pub fn sections(self, sections: impl IntoIterator<Item = AnyElement>) -> Self`

  Sections, under the last.

- `pub fn build(self, look: &Look) -> ScrollArea`

  The form's column, laid out at the window's look.

#### `FormSection`

*struct* · A section: its header, its rows, and a footer saying what it means.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement, Default)]

```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A section with no heading.

- `pub fn titled(title: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  Headed, in the app's words.

- `pub fn row(self, row: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A row, under the last: a `FormRow`, a control that is a row of its own
  (a `Toggle`), or a button.

- `pub fn rows(self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = AnyElement>) -> Self`

  Rows, under the last.

- `pub fn footer(self, footer: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::gesture

A glyph answering a press with a motion that says what its verb does:
queue drops, share lifts, settings wiggles, add pops, remove wipes away,
rescan turns, back nudges back. One program moves the icon's own raster,
so any icon answers without its strokes written out again; the
showpieces whose parts move apart (play and pause, shuffle, repeat, copy,
the heart) keep their hand-drawn answers in `GLYPH`.

#### `Gesture`

*enum* · How a glyph moves when its button is pressed.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Gesture {
    /// Dips and lands on a line drawn under it: something put somewhere
    /// (queued, added to a playlist, downloaded, saved).
    Drop,
    /// Rises a step and settles: something sent out (shared, exported).
    Lift,
    /// Rocks on its centre: something opened or looked at.
    Wiggle,
    /// Swells past its size and settles: something added or chosen.
    Pop,
    /// Squeezes thin and fades a little, then comes back: something taken
    /// away.
    Wipe,
    /// Turns once round, clockwise: something run again.
    Turn,
    /// Swings a little way and back, anticlockwise (`false`) or clockwise:
    /// undo and redo.
    Swing(bool),
    /// Steps the way it points and back.
    Nudge(Heading),
    /// Play next's triangle steps forward while the lines under it make
    /// room.
    PlayNext,
}
```

- `pub fn of(icon: Icon) -> Option<Gesture>`

  The motion `icon` answers with, or `None` for a glyph answered some
  other way: the showpieces drawn in `GLYPH` and the heart. Matched
  without a wildcard, so a new icon is given its motion here.

- `pub fn code(self) -> f32`

  The program's code for it.

- `pub fn heading(self) -> (f32, f32)`

  Which way it goes, on the glyph's grid: a nudge's heading, a swing's
  turn (1 clockwise).

#### `Heading`

*enum* · Which way a nudge goes.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Heading {
    Left,
    Right,
    Up,
    Down,
    UpRight,
}
```

#### `gesture_raster`

*function* · `icon` beside type of `points`, `side` device pixels square, as an
image the program samples: the sprite's own SVG, so its strokes match
the icon it stands in for.

```rust
pub fn raster(
    icon: Icon,
    points: f32,
    side: f32,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> Option<Arc<RenderImage>>
```

#### `GESTURE`

*static* · The gestures as one program, moving the icon's raster on its 24-point
grid. Uniforms as `GLYPH`'s: 0 the gesture, 1 its heading's x, 2 the
spill, 3 its heading's y, 4..8 the glyph's colour, 8..12 the accent (a
drop's line), 12 the glyph's side, in device pixels; track 0 the time
from 0 to 1, track 1 the same on its curve. At either end the raster
stands where the icon does.

```rust
pub static GESTURE: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-gesture",
    msl: r#"
float kq_mask(texture2d<float> image, float4 tile, float2 h) {
    if (h.x < 0.0 || h.y < 0.0 || h.x > 24.0 || h.y > 24.0) { return 0.0; }
    return koan_sample(image, tile, h / 24.0).a;
}
float2 kq_turn(float2 g, float a) {
    float2 v = g - float2(12.0, 12.0);
    float cs = cos(a);
    float sn = sin(a);
    return float2(12.0, 12.0) + float2(cs * v.x + sn * v.y, -sn * v.x + cs * v.y);
}
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float k = max(u[12], 1.0) / 24.0;
    float2 g = (position - u[2]) / k;
    float2 c = float2(12.0, 12.0);
    float2 dir = float2(u[1], u[3]);
    float pi = 3.14159265;
    float bump = sin(pi * t);
    float glyph = 0.0;
    float accent = 0.0;
    int kind = int(u[0] + 0.5);
    if (kind == 1) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, g - float2(0.0, 2.5 * bump));
        float reach = 9.0 * e;
        float d = max(abs(g.y - 25.0) - 0.6, abs(g.x - 12.0) - reach);
        accent = clamp(-d * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * sin(pi * min(t * 1.3, 1.0));
    } else if (kind == 2) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, g + float2(0.0, 3.0 * bump));
    } else if (kind == 3) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, kq_turn(g, 0.3 * sin(3.0 * pi * t) * (1.0 - t)));
    } else if (kind == 4) {
        float s = 1.0 + 0.3 * bump * (1.0 - 0.4 * t);
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, c + (g - c) / s);
    } else if (kind == 5) {
        float sx = max(1.0 - 0.6 * bump, 0.05);
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, c + (g - c) / float2(sx, 1.0)) * (1.0 - 0.5 * bump);
    } else if (kind == 6) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, kq_turn(g, 2.0 * pi * e));
    } else if (kind == 7) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, kq_turn(g, 0.9 * bump * dir.x));
    } else if (kind == 8) {
        glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, g - dir * 2.5 * bump);
    } else if (kind == 9) {
        float2 tri = g - float2(4.0 * bump, 0.0);
        if (tri.x < 10.5 && tri.y < 11.5) { glyph = kq_mask(image, tile, tri); }
        float2 lines = g - float2(0.0, 1.5 * bump);
        if (!(lines.x < 10.5 && lines.y < 11.5)) { glyph = max(glyph, kq_mask(image, tile, lines)); }
    }
    float la = glyph * u[7];
    float aa = accent * u[11];
    float a = max(la, aa);
    if (a <= 0.0) { return float4(0.0); }
    float3 rgb = mix(float3(u[8], u[9], u[10]), float3(u[4], u[5], u[6]), la / (la + aa));
    return float4(rgb, a);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn kq_mask(tile: vec4<f32>, h: vec2<f32>) -> f32 {
    if (h.x < 0.0 || h.y < 0.0 || h.x > 24.0 || h.y > 24.0) { return 0.0; }
    return koan_sample(tile, h / 24.0).a;
}
fn kq_turn(g: vec2<f32>, a: f32) -> vec2<f32> {
    let v = g - vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0);
    let cs = cos(a);
    let sn = sin(a);
    return vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0) + vec2<f32>(cs * v.x + sn * v.y, -sn * v.x + cs * v.y);
}
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let e = clamp(koan_track(u, 1u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let k = max(u[3].x, 1.0) / 24.0;
    let g = (position - u[0].z) / k;
    let c = vec2<f32>(12.0, 12.0);
    let dir = vec2<f32>(u[0].y, u[0].w);
    let pi = 3.14159265;
    let bump = sin(pi * t);
    var glyph = 0.0;
    var accent = 0.0;
    let kind = i32(u[0].x + 0.5);
    if (kind == 1) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, g - vec2<f32>(0.0, 2.5 * bump));
        let reach = 9.0 * e;
        let d = max(abs(g.y - 25.0) - 0.6, abs(g.x - 12.0) - reach);
        accent = clamp(-d * k + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0) * sin(pi * min(t * 1.3, 1.0));
    } else if (kind == 2) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, g + vec2<f32>(0.0, 3.0 * bump));
    } else if (kind == 3) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, kq_turn(g, 0.3 * sin(3.0 * pi * t) * (1.0 - t)));
    } else if (kind == 4) {
        let s = 1.0 + 0.3 * bump * (1.0 - 0.4 * t);
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, c + (g - c) / s);
    } else if (kind == 5) {
        let sx = max(1.0 - 0.6 * bump, 0.05);
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, c + (g - c) / vec2<f32>(sx, 1.0)) * (1.0 - 0.5 * bump);
    } else if (kind == 6) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, kq_turn(g, 2.0 * pi * e));
    } else if (kind == 7) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, kq_turn(g, 0.9 * bump * dir.x));
    } else if (kind == 8) {
        glyph = kq_mask(tile, g - dir * 2.5 * bump);
    } else if (kind == 9) {
        let tri = g - vec2<f32>(4.0 * bump, 0.0);
        if (tri.x < 10.5 && tri.y < 11.5) { glyph = kq_mask(tile, tri); }
        let lines = g - vec2<f32>(0.0, 1.5 * bump);
        if (!(lines.x < 10.5 && lines.y < 11.5)) { glyph = max(glyph, kq_mask(tile, lines)); }
    }
    let la = glyph * u[1].w;
    let aa = accent * u[2].w;
    let a = max(la, aa);
    if (a <= 0.0) { return vec4<f32>(0.0); }
    let rgb = mix(u[2].xyz, u[1].xyz, la / (la + aa));
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, a);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

### components::guide

The parts a guided flow is built from: a choice between ways forward
(`ChoiceCards`), where the flow has got to (`Steps`), and what is in place
on the machine and what is not (`CheckItem`).

#### `ChoiceCards`

*struct* · Ways forward to choose one of, each a block the width of the column: a
glyph, a title and a sentence saying what choosing it means. The one
chosen takes the accent in its outline and glyph, with a tick at its end
so the choice reads without colour too. Like `Segmented`, the group is
one stop for the keyboard, where the arrow keys move the choice, and a
radio group to a screen reader; a click on a card chooses it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ChoiceCards { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, chosen: Option<usize>) -> Self`

  `chosen` is the card chosen, if any is yet.

- `pub fn named(self, name: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  What the group is called to a screen reader: the question it answers.

- `pub fn card(self, icon: Icon, title: impl AsRef<str>, detail: Option<impl Into<SharedString>>) -> Self`

  A card: `title` is an app word, drawn in the theme's case; `detail`
  a sentence, which keeps its case.

- `pub fn on_change(self, handler: impl Fn(&usize, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What choosing a card does, given its index.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Steps`

*struct* · Where a flow of several steps has got to: each step's name in a line,
those done ticked in `muted`, the one showing in `strong` over the accent,
those to come in `muted`. It shows the way, and is not a control: the
flow's own buttons move along it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Steps { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new<S: AsRef<str>>(id: impl Into<ElementId>, names: impl IntoIterator<Item = S>, at: usize) -> Self`

  `names` are app words, drawn in the theme's case; `at` is the step
  showing, from zero.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Check`

*enum* · Whether something a flow needs is in place.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Check {
    Done,
    ToDo,
    /// Not known yet: still being looked for.
    Looking,
}
```

#### `CheckItem`

*struct* · One thing a flow needs, as a line of a checklist: a box ticked in the
accent when it is in place, empty in `muted` when it is not, a pending
glyph while it is being looked for; its title and what it is for; and the
verbs that put it in place at the end.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct CheckItem { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, check: Check, title: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `title` is a sentence, which keeps its case.

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A line under the title saying more.

- `pub fn action(self, action: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A button beside the item, such as one that does the step.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::heart

The heart on anything that can be favourited, the Mac's `FavouriteButton`:
the outline off, filled in the accent on. Its own click answers it: the
fill pops in past its size and settles with small particles thrown out
round it, and deflates, squashing, into the outline when emptied, drawn
by the GPU from the heart's own strokes (`Answer::Fill`, `Answer::Empty`). A change made elsewhere (a shortcut, another device)
answers alike. A heart its owner hides at rest (a row's, shown under the
pointer) shows while it answers, so a heart emptied elsewhere is seen to
close; one its owner does not draw at all at rest follows its favourite
through `answering`. The owner says what a click
does; a heart without one is a mark, with no tooltip or pointer, inside
a control of its owner's (an album tile). Where the heart shows (always,
or only under the pointer) is its styling, set as on a `div`.

#### `Heart`

*struct* · The heart: an outline when off, filled in the accent when on, answering
its own press on the GPU.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Heart { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn answering(id: impl Into<ElementId>, on: bool, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> bool`

  For a heart its owner does not draw at rest (a tile's, shown under
  the pointer or for a favourite): follows `on` while it is not drawn,
  and says whether it is answering a change, for which the owner draws
  it until the answer has run.

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, on: bool) -> Self`

  A heart, on or off; a click does what `on_click` says.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  The type the glyph is sized to.

- `pub fn rest(self, tone: Tone) -> Self`

  The outline's tone while off: `muted` in a list, `ink` over a sleeve.

- `pub fn help(self, help: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What its tooltip says, where it says more than "favourite" or
  "remove from favourites": the shortcut, on the playing track's heart.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a click does: the owner toggles the favourite. The click stops
  here, so a heart on a row does not also act on the row.

Implements `InteractiveElement`, `RenderOnce`, `StatefulInteractiveElement`, `Styled`.

### components::insets

What is drawn over a window's pages: the system's status bar, notch,
home indicator and software keyboard on a phone (zero on the desktop),
and the bars a shell draws over its pages, frosted while content runs
under them.

Grounds and the wash run under all of it; content is held clear. An
inset change redraws the window, so an element reading these in its
render follows rotation and the keyboard.

#### `Clear`

*enum* · Which of the system's insets content keeps clear of.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Clear {
    /// The status bar, the notch and the home indicator.
    System,
    /// Those and the keyboard while it is up: for what a field sits in.
    Keyboard,
}
```

#### `system_insets`

*function* · The insets `clear` names, per edge.

```rust
pub fn system_insets(clear: Clear, window: &Window) -> gpui::Edges<Pixels>
```

#### `KoanInsets`

*trait* · Padding that keeps an element's content clear of what the system draws
over the window, for any element.

```rust
pub trait KoanInsets: Styled + Sized {
    /// Padding that holds the element's children clear of what the system
    /// draws over the window, while the element's own ground fills it.
    fn clear_of_system(self, clear: Clear, window: &Window) -> Self;
}
```

#### `bar_inset`

*function* · The room a page leaves at its top for the bar drawn over it (zero where
nothing is): the first row of a page that scrolls under the bar starts
this far down inside its scroll, and a page that does not scroll is held
this far down.

```rust
pub fn bar_inset(window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Pixels
```

#### `foot_inset`

*function* · The room a page leaves at its end for the foot drawn over it (zero where
nothing is): the last row of a page that scrolls under the foot ends
this far up from the end of its scroll, and what sits at a page's foot
in flow is held this far up.

```rust
pub fn foot_inset(window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Pixels
```

#### `set_bar_inset`

*function* · Sets `bar_inset`. A change draws every view again, cached ones included,
since each page reads the inset in its own render.

```rust
pub fn set_bar_inset(inset: Pixels, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `set_foot_inset`

*function* · Sets `foot_inset`, as `set_bar_inset` does.

```rust
pub fn set_foot_inset(inset: Pixels, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `bar_probe`

*function* · An element filling its parent that keeps `bar_inset` at the parent's
height: in what a shell draws over the top of its pages.

```rust
pub fn bar_probe() -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `bounds_probe`

*function* · An element filling its parent that tells `told` its bounds as it is laid
out: where something stands, for what moves to it.

```rust
pub fn bounds_probe(
    told: impl FnOnce(gpui::Bounds<gpui::Pixels>, &mut gpui::App) + 'static,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `foot_probe`

*function* · An element filling its parent that keeps `foot_inset` at the parent's
height: in what a shell draws over the foot of its pages.

```rust
pub fn foot_probe() -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `keep_clear`

*function* · An element filling its parent, the part of a page a list scrolls in, that
holds the list's keyboard cursor clear of the bar and the foot drawn over
the page each time the keys move it: the list brings the row on screen
by its own lights, then this nudges it out from under them. After the
list among the parent's children, so the row has been laid out.

```rust
pub fn keep_clear<S: ScrollSource>(spot: &CursorSpot, source: &S) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `report_scroll_edges`

*function* · Tells the window's frost whether the page's content now runs under its
bar and under its foot: what a scroll passing under them reports as it
is laid out. A change fades the frost in or out on the GPU, with no
frame drawn.

```rust
pub fn report_scroll_edges(
    under_bar: bool,
    under_foot: bool,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,

```

#### `page_shown`

*function* · Names the page the window shows, as its shell draws: when it is another
page than last time, the scroll edges are cleared, so its own scroll
reports them again as it is laid out and a page with none leaves the
frost clear.

```rust
pub fn page_shown(page: impl Hash, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `Frost`

*struct* · The ground of what a shell draws over its pages, filling its parent,
which is `relative`, and drawn before the rest of it: clear while
nothing is under it, and frosted as the system's bars are while the
page's content runs under it (what lies under blurred, and under a foot
tinted with the page's ground; a bar is tinted only where the renderer
cannot blur). With less
transparency asked for, opaque and unblurred while content is under it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Frost { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn bar() -> Self`

  Under a bar at the top of the pages.

- `pub fn foot() -> Self`

  Under a foot at the end of the pages.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `KoanFrosted`

*trait* · A frosted ground for any element: what lies under it blurred and
tinted, or opaque where less transparency is asked for.

```rust
pub trait KoanFrosted: Styled + Sized {
    /// A frosted ground standing as it is, whatever is under it: what lies
    /// under blurred. With less
    /// transparency asked for, the ground opaque and nothing blurred. Where
    /// the renderer cannot blur, the tint alone. A bar a page scrolls under
    /// takes `Frost`, which stays clear while nothing is.
    fn frosted(self, look: &Look) -> Self;
}
```

### components::inspector

`SyncedLines` (`lyrics.rs`) is the body a panel of timed text takes.

- `pub fn trailing(self, trailing: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  At the head's far edge: where the content came from, or that it is
  still coming.

- `pub fn body(self, body: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  What the panel shows under its head.

### components::layer

What an element paints, drawn once into a texture of its own and composed
by the GPU at each present (#1859): faded, moved or revealed by tracks,
with no frame drawn while it moves. What `FadeIn` and `Reveal` are made of.

The content is laid out and painted as it would be unwrapped, so it takes
the pointer where it stands; only how it shows moves.

#### `LAYER`

*static* · The layer's composition. Tracks: 0 the opacity, 1 and 2 the offset in
device pixels, 3 how much of it shows, from the top left, 0 to 1.

```rust
pub static LAYER: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-layer",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float shown = koan_track(u, 3, time);
    if (position.x > size.x * shown || position.y > size.y * shown) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float2 offset = float2(koan_track(u, 1, time), koan_track(u, 2, time));
    float2 uv = (position - offset) / size;
    if (any(uv < 0.0) || any(uv > 1.0)) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    // The layer was drawn over clear: its colours are premultiplied.
    float4 c = koan_sample(image, tile, uv);
    float3 rgb = c.a > 0.0 ? c.rgb / c.a : float3(0.0);
    return float4(rgb, c.a * koan_track(u, 0, time));
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let shown = koan_track(u, 3u, time);
    if (position.x > size.x * shown || position.y > size.y * shown) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let offset = vec2<f32>(koan_track(u, 1u, time), koan_track(u, 2u, time));
    let uv = (position - offset) / size;
    if (any(uv < vec2<f32>(0.0)) || any(uv > vec2<f32>(1.0))) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let c = koan_sample(tile, uv);
    var rgb = vec3<f32>(0.0);
    if (c.a > 0.0) {
        rgb = c.rgb / c.a;
    }
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, c.a * koan_track(u, 0u, time));
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `AnimatedLayer`

*struct* · `child` drawn as a layer whose opacity, offset and reveal `animated`'s
tracks move (`LAYER`). Decorative: under Reduce Motion it stands at its
tracks' ends.

```rust
pub struct AnimatedLayer { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(animated: &Animated, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A layer standing whole until `animated`'s tracks say otherwise.

- `pub fn room_below(self, below: Pixels) -> Self`

  Composed over `below` more than its bounds at the foot, so content
  its offset moves down is drawn there rather than cut off.

- `pub fn room_toward(self, delta: Point<Pixels>) -> Self`

  Composed out as far as `delta` on the side it points to, so content
  offset that far is drawn there rather than cut off.

- `pub fn program(self, program: &'static ShaderProgram) -> Self`

  Composed by `program` rather than `LAYER`, at rest as well as in
  motion: a layer whose look is its program's (the lyrics' opacities
  and edges).

- `pub fn moving_program(self, program: &'static ShaderProgram) -> Self`

  Composed by `program` rather than `LAYER` while it moves, and painted
  plainly at rest.

- `pub fn informative(self) -> Self`

  Its motion says something (which line is sung): under Reduce Motion
  its tracks still move, as their owner sets them.

- `pub fn placed(self, placed: impl Fn(Bounds<Pixels>, f32) + 'static) -> Self`

  `placed` writes the uniforms that follow where the layer is painted,
  with its bounds and the window's scale, each paint: a scroll that
  moves what is inside it.

Implements `Element`.

### components::layout

Layout and type on the theme's scale, for what a page lays out itself:
`Stack`, a flex container whose gap and insets are steps of `Space`, and
`Text`, a run of text in a type role and a tone. Each is a `div` that
reads the window's `Look` as it lays out, so a page names a step or a
role and never a length or a colour; everything else a `div` takes
(flex, alignment, ids, handlers) goes on as it would on the `div`.

#### `Inset`

*trait* · Insets on the spacing scale, shared by `Stack` and `Text`.

```rust
pub trait Inset: Sized {
    /// The element's spacing, which the methods below set.
    #[doc(hidden)]
    fn spacing(&mut self) -> &mut Spacing;
    /// The same inset on every edge.
    fn inset_all(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The same inset on the leading and trailing edges.
    fn inset_x(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The same inset on the top and bottom edges.
    fn inset_y(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The inset on the top edge.
    fn inset_top(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The inset on the bottom edge.
    fn inset_bottom(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The inset on the left edge.
    fn inset_left(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The inset on the right edge.
    fn inset_right(mut self, space: Space) -> Self;
    /// The page's margin either side (`RowMetrics::page_margin`): what a
    /// page's content keeps to.
    fn page_margins(mut self) -> Self;
    /// A page header's insets above and below (`RowMetrics::page_top`,
    /// `RowMetrics::header_gap`): clear of the page's top, and a step off
    /// what follows it.
    fn page_head(mut self) -> Self;
}
```

#### `Stack`

*struct* · A flex container on the spacing scale: `Stack::column().space(Space::L)`.

```rust
pub struct Stack { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn column() -> Self`

  Children one under another.

- `pub fn block() -> Self`

  Children laid out as a plain `div` lays them out, one under another
  at their own heights: for insets around content that is not a list
  of parts.

- `pub fn row() -> Self`

  Children side by side.

- `pub fn wrapping() -> Self`

  Children side by side, wrapping onto further lines when they do not
  fit: a set of buttons on a narrow window.

- `pub fn space(self, space: Space) -> Self`

  The gap between children.

- `pub fn measure(self, measure: Measure) -> Self`

  As wide as its parent up to `measure`, centred in what is left.

- `pub fn type_role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  The type role its text takes, unless a child says otherwise.

- `pub fn tone(self, tone: Tone) -> Self`

  The tone its text takes, unless a child says otherwise.

Implements `Inset`.

#### `Text`

*struct* · A run of text in a type role, in a tone or the one it sits in:
`Text::new(TypeRole::Meta).tone(Tone::Muted).child(words)`.

```rust
pub struct Text { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Text in `role`, in the tone it sits in until `tone` says otherwise.

- `pub fn in_tone(tone: Tone) -> Self`

  Text in the role of what it sits in (a table's cell), in `tone`.

- `pub fn tone(self, tone: Tone) -> Self`

  The tone it is drawn in.

- `pub fn measure(self, measure: Measure) -> Self`

  As wide as its parent up to `measure`, from the leading edge.

- `pub fn icon(self, icon: Icon) -> Self`

  A glyph before the words, in their role and tone, where icons are
  shown: a mark that says what kind of line it is (a check, a warning).

- `pub fn picked(self, picked: bool) -> Self`

  On a picked row: in `on_selection`, whatever the tone.

Implements `Inset`.

#### `Divider`

*struct* · A hairline in `rule` between two parts of a page: across it

- `pub fn vertical() -> Self`
