### components::level

A level between two glyphs: a renderer's volume.

#### `Level`

*struct* · A whole number from 0 to `max` on a `Slider`, `low` and `high` glyphs at
its ends in `muted`. While it is dragged it shows where the pointer is and
sends nothing; the value is committed once, when the drag is let go or an
arrow key steps it. For a value set over the network, where every step of
a drag would be a round trip and the value reported back lags the hand.

A committed value is held until the value given agrees with it or
`LANDING` passes, so the slider does not jump back to the old value while
the device takes the new one and reports it, as the seek bar's landing
holds the playhead.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Level { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, value: u32, max: u32) -> Self`

  A level at `value` of `max`, between the volume glyphs unless given
  others.

- `pub fn ends(self, low: Icon, high: Icon) -> Self`

  The glyphs at either end; a volume's speakers by default.

- `pub fn numbered(self) -> Self`

  The value written after the high glyph, such as "40".

- `pub fn on_commit(self, handler: impl Fn(&u32, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What letting go does, given the value let go at.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::link_text

Names that lead somewhere: a record's title, a credited artist. Plain
text until the pointer is on one that leads somewhere, then underlined in
`ink`. A line of them shares one baseline and, too long for its room,
ends each name in an ellipsis rather than cutting it mid-glyph.

#### `link_text`

*function* · One name. With `on_click` it leads somewhere: the pointer underlines it
in `ink`, and a click on it is not also a click on what it sits in.

```rust
pub fn link_text(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    text: impl Into<SharedString>,
    ink: Hsla,
    on_click: Option<Handler<ClickEvent>>,
) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `link_action`

*function* · What a name leads to when clicked.

```rust
pub fn link_action(
    run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static,
) -> Handler<ClickEvent>
```

#### `name_line`

*function* · One line of names on one baseline in `role` and `color`, as wide as its
container lets it be; a name that does not fit ends in an ellipsis.

```rust
pub fn name_line(
    children: Vec<AnyElement>,
    role: TypeRole,
    color: Hsla,
    look: &Look,
) -> Div
```

### components::list_keys

The keys every list answers to, and where its keyboard cursor is: one
behaviour for every list in the app, so the queue, a record's tracks,
the history and a playlist move the same way.

A list is one stop in the window's Tab order; inside it, ↑ and ↓ move the
cursor and pick the row under it, with ⇧ extending the pick, Home and End
go to either end, ← → move a tile in a grid (where ↑ ↓ move a row of
it), Page Up and Page Down move a screenful, Return plays or
opens the row, Ctrl-Space adds it to the pick or takes it out (Space
stays play and pause), and the menu key or ⇧F10 opens its context menu. The list
sets the `List` key context and handles the actions; `ListCursor` works
out where each key leaves the cursor and what it picks. The app binds the
keys, from the table its shortcuts sheet lists.

#### `LIST_CONTEXT`

*constant* · The key context a list sets on the element that holds its focus.

```rust
pub const LIST_CONTEXT: &str = "List";
```

#### `ListCursor`

*struct* · Where a list's keyboard cursor is, and where a ⇧ pick extends from.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ListCursor {
    pub at: Option<usize>,
    pub anchor: Option<usize>,
}
```

- `pub fn step(&mut self, by: isize, len: usize, extend: bool) -> Option<Moved>`

  Moves `by` rows through a list of `len`, held at its ends. A cursor
  not yet placed starts at the first row going down and the last going
  up. `extend` keeps the anchor and picks from it; otherwise the anchor
  moves with the cursor and the row alone is picked.

- `pub fn to(&mut self, to: usize, len: usize, extend: bool) -> Option<Moved>`

  To row `to` of a list of `len`: Home, End, or a row chosen otherwise.

- `pub fn place(&mut self, at: usize)`

  A click put the cursor on `at`: the keys carry on from there, and a
  ⇧ pick extends from it.

- `pub fn click(&mut self, at: usize, extend: bool)`

  A click put the cursor on `at`. A ⇧-click extends the pick from the
  anchor, which it keeps, so ⇧ and the arrows go on from the same
  origin; any other click is the new origin.

- `pub fn relocate<K: PartialEq>(&mut self, old: &[K], new: &[K])`

  The rows changed under the cursor (a reload, new rows above it): the
  cursor and the anchor stay on the rows they were on, found by key
  from `old` in `new`; one whose row is gone keeps its place, held
  within the new length.

- `pub fn clamp(&mut self, len: usize)`

  A list changed under the cursor: held within its new length, or
  cleared when it is empty.

#### `Moved`

*struct* · What a key did: the row the cursor is on now, where the pick extends
from (the anchor, or the row itself), and the rows to pick, in order,
inclusive: the one row, or from the anchor to it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Moved {
    pub to: usize,
    pub from: usize,
    pub pick: (usize, usize),
}
```

### components::lists

The virtualised lists a long page sits in, and their scrollbar: GPUI's
`uniform_list` (rows of one height, laid out a screenful at a time) and
`list` (rows that measure themselves), with the handles that keep their
place, and the kōan scrollbar over either. A page building rows of
`TableRow`s takes `table_list`; one laying out something else in rows,
such as a grid of tiles a row at a time, takes these.

#### `OVERDRAW`

*constant* · How far past the screen a `list` lays out rows, so a scroll lands on
rows already measured.

```rust
pub const OVERDRAW: Pixels = px(400.0);
```

#### `list_scrollbar`

*function* · The overlay scrollbar for a virtualised list's handle, over the list's
right edge.

```rust
pub fn list_scrollbar<H>(handle: &H) -> impl IntoElement
where
    H: super::ScrollSource,
```

#### `spoken_list`

*function* · What a virtualised list sits in for a screen reader: a list named for
what it holds, its rows (`TableRow::spoken`) its items.

```rust
pub fn spoken_list(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    name: impl Into<SharedString>,
) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `reveal_around`

*function* · Scrolls the row past row `ix` of a `uniform_list`'s `count` on screen, the
one before it where `ix` is at the top: a screen reader moving through the
list reaches only rows drawn, so the next one is drawn before it gets
there. One neighbour only, the one the reader is heading for: a second
scroll could undo the first. The caller draws the list again
(`TableRow::on_reveal` does).

```rust
pub fn reveal_around(handle: &UniformListScrollHandle, ix: usize, count: usize)
```

#### `reveal_around_list`

*function* · `reveal_around` for a `list` of rows that measure themselves.

```rust
pub fn reveal_around_list(state: &ListState, ix: usize)
```

#### `offset_down`

*function* · A scroll offset `y` down from the top, as a list's handle takes it.

```rust
pub fn offset_down(y: Pixels) -> Point<Pixels>
```

#### `rows_under_foot`

*function* · An element filling its parent that keeps the parent's width in `width`,
and redraws `owner` when it changes: a page laying out a grid or a list
by the width it is given.
How a uniform list of rows `row` high leaves a phone's foot room at its
end: the blank rows it adds after its last, and how far it reaches below
its parent's bottom, which clips it, so the blank rows come to exactly
the foot's height. Room left as padding would not do: a uniform list
draws rows only inside its padding, and nothing would pass under the
foot. Zero where nothing is drawn over the page's foot.

```rust
pub fn rows_under_foot(row: Pixels, window: &gpui::Window, cx: &App) -> (usize, Pixels)
```

#### `scrolls_away`

*function* · A page's header over a virtual list that scrolls it away with the rows:
drawn `above` the list's scroll, as the list lays it out, over the room the list leaves above its
first row, which is `height` once measured (`owner` is told).

```rust
pub fn scrolls_away(
    header: impl IntoElement,
    scroll: &UniformListScrollHandle,
    above: Pixels,
    height: Rc<Cell<Option<Pixels>>>,
    owner: EntityId,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `width_probe`

*function* · An element that reports the width it is laid out at into `width`, and
notifies `owner` when it changes: for a list that lays out its rows by
its own width.

```rust
pub fn width_probe(
    width: Rc<Cell<Option<Pixels>>>,
    owner: EntityId,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

### components::lyrics

Lines of timed text following the song: a song's lyrics in their panel.

The lines are one layer the GPU composes at each present (`LYRICS`), as
a karaoke screen is drawn. When the song reaches a line the column glides
up to it, the sung line eases into emphasis and the one before settles
back, each on a track the program evaluates at the present's time, so
nothing on the CPU runs while they move. Past lines are quieter than the
ones to come, and the panel's top and bottom edges fade, so the lines
enter and leave softly rather than being cut.

The sung line is drawn in `title small`, the rest in `control`, as the
Mac's panel draws them, all in `strong`; the program sets each line's
opacity. The line under the pointer, which a click seeks to, is lifted
nearly to the sung line's strength. Where the renderer draws no shaders,
the lines are drawn in their colours and the column steps.

#### `LYRICS`

*static* · How the lines show. Uniforms: 0 and 1 the sung line's top and bottom, 2
and 3 the line sung before it, in device pixels from the layer's top as
laid out; 4 a line to come's opacity, 5 a past line's, 6 the sung line's
opacity as it starts to ease in, 7 the line before's as it ends, 8 the
height of the edge's fade, 9 the line before's opacity as it starts to
ease out (1, or where a change still running had brought it), 10 and 11
the hovered line's top and bottom, 12 its least opacity. Tracks: 0 the emphasis moving from the line
before to the sung line, 0 to 1; 1 the column's offset down, in device
pixels, as it glides to its place.

```rust
pub static LYRICS: ShaderProgram = ShaderProgram {
    name: "koan-lyrics",
    msl: r#"
float4 shade(float2 position, float2 size, float time, constant float *u, texture2d<float> image, float4 tile) {
    float t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    float offset = koan_track(u, 1, time);
    float2 uv = float2(position.x, position.y - offset) / size;
    if (uv.y < 0.0 || uv.y > 1.0) {
        return float4(0.0);
    }
    float4 c = koan_sample(image, tile, uv);
    float3 rgb = c.a > 0.0 ? c.rgb / c.a : float3(0.0);
    float y = position.y - offset;
    float a;
    if (y >= u[0] && y < u[1]) {
        a = mix(u[6], 1.0, t);
    } else if (y >= u[2] && y < u[3]) {
        a = mix(u[9], u[7], t);
    } else if (y < u[0]) {
        a = u[5];
    } else {
        a = u[4];
    }
    if (y >= u[10] && y < u[11]) {
        a = max(a, u[12]);
    }
    float band = max(u[8], 1.0);
    float edge = smoothstep(0.0, band, position.y) * smoothstep(0.0, band, size.y - position.y);
    return float4(rgb, c.a * a * edge * edge);
}
"#,
    wgsl: r#"
fn shade(position: vec2<f32>, size: vec2<f32>, time: f32, u: array<vec4<f32>, 20>, tile: vec4<f32>) -> vec4<f32> {
    let t = clamp(koan_track(u, 0u, time), 0.0, 1.0);
    let offset = koan_track(u, 1u, time);
    let uv = vec2<f32>(position.x, position.y - offset) / size;
    if (uv.y < 0.0 || uv.y > 1.0) {
        return vec4<f32>(0.0);
    }
    let c = koan_sample(tile, uv);
    var rgb = vec3<f32>(0.0);
    if (c.a > 0.0) {
        rgb = c.rgb / c.a;
    }
    let y = position.y - offset;
    var a = u[1].x;
    if (y >= u[0].x && y < u[0].y) {
        a = mix(u[1].z, 1.0, t);
    } else if (y >= u[0].z && y < u[0].w) {
        a = mix(u[2].y, u[1].w, t);
    } else if (y < u[0].x) {
        a = u[1].y;
    }
    if (y >= u[2].z && y < u[2].w) {
        a = max(a, u[3].x);
    }
    let band = max(u[2].x, 1.0);
    let edge = smoothstep(0.0, band, position.y) * smoothstep(0.0, band, size.y - position.y);
    return vec4<f32>(rgb, c.a * a * edge * edge);
}
"#,
    prepare: None,
};
```

#### `SyncedLines`

*struct* · Lines of timed text, the one at `active` emphasised and kept mid-panel as
the song moves. A click on a line hands its index to `on_line`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct SyncedLines { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, lines: Vec<SharedString>, active: Option<usize>) -> Self`

  `lines`, with `active` the one being sung.

- `pub fn song(self, song: impl std::hash::Hash) -> Self`

  Which song these are the lines of (the track): another starts
  afresh, lyrics the same as the last song's included. Told by the
  lines' text otherwise.

- `pub fn since(self, at: Option<Instant>) -> Self`

  When the song reached the active line, by the playhead: its motion
  is timed from there rather than from when the line was drawn.

- `pub fn on_line(self, handler: impl Fn(&usize, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press on a line does, given its index.

- `pub fn cue(starts: &[f64], position: f64, playing: bool, now: Instant) -> Cue`

  Where lines starting at `starts` seconds stand at `position` seconds
  in, at `now`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Cue`

*struct* · Where a set of timed lines stands at a moment (`SyncedLines::cue`).

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Cue {
    /// The line being sung: the last one started.
    pub active: Option<usize>,
    /// When it started, while playing: what `SyncedLines::since` takes.
    pub since: Option<Instant>,
    /// How long until the next line starts, while playing: when to look
    /// again, so following the song costs one wake per line.
    pub next_in: Option<std::time::Duration>,
}
```

### components::menu

The theme's menu: the panel and its rows as a view of its own, opened as
a context menu at the pointer or below a button. Up and Down move through
it, Right and Return open a submenu, Return chooses, Escape and a click
outside close it. gpui-component's `PopupMenu` draws its own padding,
corners and type, which no theme setting reaches; this is the Mac's
`KoanMenu` instead.

#### `MenuEntry`

*struct* · An entry of a menu, built as `PopupMenuItem` is.

```rust
pub struct MenuEntry { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  An entry in the app's words, drawn lowercase.

- `pub fn action(self, icon: Icon, help: impl Into<SharedString>, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A quiet glyph at the row's end that acts on what the row names
  without choosing it ("move here", "forget this device"), with `help`
  as its tooltip. Every row of the menu keeps the same columns, glyphs
  with glyphs and words with words, so the actions line up; → reaches
  them from the keyboard.

- `pub fn text_action(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>, help: impl Into<SharedString>, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  An action of a word or two, quiet as the glyphs are: "move here". As
  a row's last action it takes the last slot, which every row then
  keeps as wide.

- `pub fn lit_action(self, icon: Icon, help: impl Into<SharedString>, lit: bool, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  `action`, its glyph in the accent while `lit`: a setting that is on
  for what the row names, as an output's EQ.

- `pub fn stays_open(self) -> Self`

  Choosing it leaves the menu open: a choice among outputs or devices,
  whose tick and details the menu shows moving as it is refilled.

- `pub fn keys(self, keys: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The keys that do the same, at the row's end in `muted`: a menu drawn
  in the window where there is no menu bar.

- `pub fn data(text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  An entry that is data (a playlist, a device), which keeps its case.

- `pub fn icon(self, icon: Icon) -> Self`

  A glyph before the label.

- `pub fn checked(self, checked: bool) -> Self`

  One of a set of choices, ticked when in force.

- `pub fn toggled(self, on: bool) -> Self`

  A setting turned on or off ("group by album"), not a choice among
  others: ticked while `on`, without the ground a chosen place takes.

- `pub fn rich(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A place to choose, drawn as the Mac's output picker draws one: its
  glyph large, the label over `detail`, the chosen one on `surface`.

- `pub fn warns(self, warns: bool) -> Self`

  The rich row's detail is a warning, in `bad`.

- `pub fn reach(self, reach: Icon) -> Self`

  A small glyph after the label: how the place is reached.

- `pub fn destructive(self) -> Self`

  In `bad`, for an entry that destroys something.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Dimmed and taking no press.

- `pub fn on_click(self, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What choosing the entry does.

#### `Menu`

*struct* · A menu: entries, separators, notes and submenus, kept by an entity so
the keyboard can move through it. Opened by `koan_menu` and its kin.

```rust
pub struct Menu { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn build(window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App, build: impl FnOnce(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu) -> Entity<Menu>`

  A menu made by `build`, as an entity the panel draws.

- `pub fn item(self, entry: MenuEntry) -> Self`

  An entry, under the last.

- `pub fn label(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A line of the menu that says something and does nothing.

- `pub fn control(self, make: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) -> AnyElement + 'static) -> Self`

  A control in a row of its own, in the rows' insets: a renderer's
  volume. Made each time the menu draws, chosen by nothing; it keeps its
  own focus and keys.

- `pub fn note(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A sentence about the entries around it, in `fine`, `muted`, wrapped
  at the menu's reading width rather than widening it.

- `pub fn refill(&mut self, cx: &mut Context<Menu>, build: impl FnOnce(Menu, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu)`

  Makes the entries again while the menu is open, for a menu whose
  content arrives after it opens (renderers found on the network). The
  highlight stays on its row while that row is still there.

- `pub fn min_width(self, points: f32) -> Self`

  Keep the panel at least `points` wide, so it holds its size as rows
  come, go and change while it is open.

- `pub fn width(self, points: f32) -> Self`

  Exactly `points` wide, so it never changes size while open: a name
  that does not fit is cut, as a detail is.

- `pub fn separator(self) -> Self`

  A rule between groups; one at the start, at the end or after another
  is left out.

- `pub fn section(self, title: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A section: a rule, then its name in `fine`, `muted`, over the
  entries that follow.

- `pub fn submenu(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Menu>, build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static) -> Self`

  An entry that opens the menu `build` makes beside it.

- `pub fn submenu_icon(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>, icon: Icon, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Menu>, build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static) -> Self`

  A submenu with the glyph the Mac's menu gives it.

Implements `EventEmitter`, `Focusable`, `Render`.

#### `KoanContextMenu`

*trait* · A context menu on any element: `build` makes the menu each time the
secondary button is pressed on it, or a touch is held on it, and the menu
opens where the press was.

```rust
pub trait KoanContextMenu: InteractiveElement + ParentElement + Sized {
    fn koan_context_menu(
        self,
        id: impl Into<ElementId>,
        window: &mut Window,
        cx: &mut App,
        build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static,
    ) -> Self;
    /// `koan_context_menu`, which the menu key also opens, under the
    /// element's keyboard cursor (`spot`) where one is laid out, and at the
    /// pointer otherwise.
    fn koan_context_menu_at(
        self,
        id: impl Into<ElementId>,
        spot: Option<super::CursorSpot>,
        window: &mut Window,
        cx: &mut App,
        build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static,
    ) -> Self;
    /// The same menu opened by a primary press too: a "more" button's.
    fn koan_menu_on_press(
        self,
        id: impl Into<ElementId>,
        window: &mut Window,
        cx: &mut App,
        build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static,
    ) -> Self;
    #[doc(hidden)]
    fn koan_menu(
        self,
        id: impl Into<ElementId>,
        spot: Option<super::CursorSpot>,
        primary: bool,
        window: &mut Window,
        cx: &mut App,
        build: impl Fn(Menu, &mut Window, &mut Context<Menu>) -> Menu + 'static,
    ) -> Self;
}
```

#### `on_menu_focus_lost`

*function* · Names what takes the keyboard when a menu closes and what held it as
it opened has since gone, its page left behind.

```rust
pub fn on_menu_focus_lost(
    fallback: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static,
    cx: &mut App,
)
```

#### `MenuSlot`

*struct* · Where a set of buttons keeps the menu one of them has open: the
transport's sleep, output and EQ, a toolbar's menus.

A trigger toggles its menu. A menu closes itself on any press outside
it, and the trigger is outside it, so without care a click meant to
close would close the menu on its press and open it again on its
release. The slot notes, as the press begins and before the menu hears
it, whether the trigger's menu was open then; a click from that press
only closes it. The Mac's menus had to be fixed for the same thing.

```rust
pub struct MenuSlot<K> { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn is_open(&self, which: K) -> bool`

  Whether the menu `which` is open.

- `pub fn close(&mut self)`

  Closes whichever menu is open.

- `pub fn menu(&self, which: K) -> Option<&Entity<Menu>>`

  The menu `which` opened, while it is open: to refill it as what it
  shows changes.

- `pub fn trigger<V: 'static, E>(&self, which: K, button: E, slot: fn(&mut V) -> &mut MenuSlot<K>, build: impl Fn(&mut V, &mut Window, &mut Context<V>) -> Option<Entity<Menu>> + 'static, cx: &mut Context<V>) -> Div where E: StatefulInteractiveElement + IntoElement + 'static,`

  The trigger for `which`: `button` with the press noted and the click
  toggling, the menu `build` makes opening beneath it (`None` when there
  is nothing to show). `slot` reaches this slot inside the view that
  holds it.

- `pub fn trigger_below<V: 'static, E>(&self, which: K, button: E, slot: fn(&mut V) -> &mut MenuSlot<K>, build: impl Fn(&mut V, &mut Window, &mut Context<V>) -> Option<Entity<Menu>> + 'static, cx: &mut Context<V>) -> Div where E: StatefulInteractiveElement + IntoElement + 'static,`

  `trigger` for a button along the top of the window, a toolbar's: the
  menu opens below it, its left edge at the button's.

Implements `Default`.

### components::meter

A horizontal bar filled from the left to a fraction: a speed, a latency,
a share. Where the renderer draws shaders the GPU eases the fill to each
new fraction from where it stood, so a meter fed at a poll moves between
the values without a frame drawn for it. The fill is kept by the meter's
id from one render to the next, so callers hold no state of their own.

#### `Meter`

*struct* · A bar filled to `fraction`, from 0 to 1, in the accent over the rule's
colour, unless given others.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Meter { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, fraction: f32) -> Self`

  A fraction that is not finite is drawn as 0; one outside 0..1 is
  clamped.

- `pub fn color(self, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  The fill's colour.

- `pub fn track(self, track: Hsla) -> Self`

  The colour of the bar behind the fill.

- `pub fn height(self, height: Pixels) -> Self`

  The bar's thickness, rather than the 3-point default.

- `pub fn ease(self, ease: Duration) -> Self`

  How long the fill takes to reach a new fraction. A meter fed at a
  poll takes the poll's interval, so it moves without stopping.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::metrics

Where rows and pages stand: the Mac's `RowMetrics`, and the page margin
the spec gives. Row heights follow the text size alone, their padding
and the margins the spacing scale, as on the Mac. Read from the window's
`Look`, so nothing passes them by hand.

#### `RowKind`

*enum* · A row's height, by what it carries.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum RowKind {
    /// One line: the title alone.
    Line,
    /// The title over a line of credit.
    Text,
    /// A sleeve beside the title and its credit.
    Art,
    /// A record's heading over its run in a queue or playlist: its sleeve
    /// (`RowMetrics::heading_sleeve`), the record over its artist and a
    /// line of detail.
    Heading,
}
```

#### `RowMetrics`

*struct* · Row heights, gaps, margins and widths for lists and panels, measured
from the Mac app and scaled by the look's text size.

```rust
pub struct RowMetrics;
```

- `pub fn height(kind: RowKind, look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The height of a row of `kind`, at least a finger's target under touch.

- `pub fn nav_height(detail: bool, look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A sidebar row's height, the Mac's sidebar measured at 2x: a place
  (one line) or a playlist (its sleeve beside its name over a detail
  line), the content centred in it.

- `pub fn nav_art(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A navigation row's face, square: a playlist's sleeve in the sidebar.

- `pub fn estimate(kind: RowKind) -> Pixels`

  A row's height at 100 %, for what has no window to ask: a list's
  guess for the rows it has not measured yet.

- `pub fn header_art(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A record page's sleeve in its header, square, the Mac's: fixed
  whatever the window's width, so a resize never moves it.

- `pub fn artist_photo(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  An artist's photo in their page's header: the size of a record's sleeve
  in its header, so the two pages line up.

- `pub fn heading_sleeve(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A record heading's sleeve, square.

- `pub fn lead_glyph(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A row's leading glyph, where it has no number: the glyph's own
  width, so it starts on the page's edge.

- `pub fn sleeve(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A row's sleeve, square.

- `pub fn padding(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Above and below a row's content.

- `pub fn lead_width(count: usize, look: &Look, window: &Window) -> Pixels`

  A lead column wide enough for the longest number in a list of
  `count` rows, measured in the face the numbers are drawn in (`meta`):
  two digits at least.

- `pub fn page_margin(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  A page's margin on every side, and what a row's content is inset by
  so it lines up under the page's header. One figure, so the page's
  edge is tuned in one place: 16, as the Swift lists use (the spec gives 32).

- `pub fn page_margin_on(scale: &Scale) -> Pixels`

  `page_margin` for a layout that has the scale and not the look: a
  tile grid working out its columns.

- `pub fn below_bar(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Between the bar a list scrolls under and the header it scrolls away
  with the rows (a phone's filter field): 10 points.

- `pub fn page_top(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Above a page's header: the page's margin, as the spec has it on
  every side.

- `pub fn header_gap(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Between a page's header and its list.

- `pub fn inspector(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  An inspector's width beside the page, the Mac's ideal for its
  lyrics, and what a double click on its edge restores; a drag keeps
  it between `inspector_min` and `inspector_max`.

- `pub fn inspector_min(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The narrowest an inspector panel is dragged to.

- `pub fn inspector_max(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The widest an inspector panel is dragged to.

- `pub fn column_gap(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Between columns of a row.

#### `BarMetrics`

*struct* · The playing bars, the Mac's: three 2-point bars 2 points apart, 11 tall.

```rust
pub struct BarMetrics;
```

#### `TransportMetrics`

*struct* · The transport bar's figures, the spec's: 64 tall, a 40-point cover, the
controls 22 apart over the seek bar.

```rust
pub struct TransportMetrics;
```

- `pub fn control_gap(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Between the controls' glyphs, as the Mac spaces them.

- `pub fn trailing_gap(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  Between the trailing group's items (the format badge, sleep, play on,
  output, EQ), the Mac's. Each item is as wide as its glyph or label in
  the layout, its hit area reaching half this gap either side.

- `pub fn centre_rows(look: &Look) -> CentreRows`

  Where the centre column's rows stand, measured from below the bar's
  rule, which the column sits under: the play button's outline, the
  controls' hit row and the seek row. The outline, the gap and the seek
  line's row are centred in the bar, so the space above the outline
  equals the space below the seek row, as the Mac's transport measures
  at 2x (7.5 points each in its 64). The controls' hit row is centred on
  the outline, as tall as `HIT` where there is room and never reaching
  above the rule or into the seek row, so a click in it never seeks.

- `pub fn trailing_icon(look: &Look) -> f32`

  A trailing glyph (sleep, play on, output, EQ), the Mac's 17 points
  at the chosen text size: larger than the `body` glyphs beside the
  bar's text, as the Mac draws its device and output buttons.

#### `CentreRows`

*struct* · The centre column's rows, from `TransportMetrics::centre_rows`, below
the bar's rule.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct CentreRows {
    pub outline_top: Pixels,
    pub outline: Pixels,
    /// The controls' hit row: they fill it, their glyphs on its middle.
    pub controls_top: Pixels,
    pub controls_height: Pixels,
    /// The seek row's top, `SEEK` tall.
    pub seek_top: Pixels,
    pub track_centre: Pixels,
}
```

#### `TileColumns`

*struct* · A grid of record tiles across a width: how many columns, how big each
tile, and the room between and beside them.

The tiles keep one size whatever the width. A window being resized moves
the tiles apart rather than growing them: the spare width is shared out
equally among the gaps, so the grid's first and last columns stand on the
page's margin, in line with its header and rows, and a column is added
only once another tile fits at the least gap, or dropped once one no
longer does. One tile alone stands on the left margin too, the spare
width beside it, so the first column is level with the header at every
width.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct TileColumns {
    pub columns: usize,
    pub side: Pixels,
    pub gap: Pixels,
    /// Each side's margin: the page's.
    pub inset: Pixels,
}
```

- `pub fn for_width(width: Pixels, margin: Pixels) -> Self`

  The columns for a list `width` wide with `margin` at either side.
  Narrower than one tile, the one tile shrinks to fit.

- `pub fn filling(width: Pixels, margin: Pixels) -> Self`

  The columns of a tablet's page, whose width is one of a few and does
  not follow a pointer: the tiles grow to take the row, so two columns
  never stand a tile apart.

#### `TileGrid`

*struct* · Record tiles in rows: `TileColumns` across the width, and each row's
height with the caption under the sleeve and the gap below it. Every tile
is the same size, so nothing is measured.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct TileGrid {
    pub columns: usize,
    pub side: Pixels,
    pub gap: Pixels,
    /// The margin asked for: above the first row and below the last.
    pub margin: Pixels,
    /// Either side of a row: the margin asked for.
    pub inset: Pixels,
    /// A row's height, its spacing below included.
    pub row: Pixels,
}
```

- `pub fn inside(width: Pixels, margin: Pixels, scale: &Scale) -> Self`

  The grid for a list `width` wide that keeps `margin` clear at each
  side, with captions on `scale`.

- `pub fn inside_on(width: Pixels, margin: Pixels, look: &Look) -> Self`

  The grid for a list `width` wide in this look: a tablet's tiles fill
  the row, and any other's keep their size.

- `pub fn for_width(width: Pixels, scale: &Scale) -> Self`

  The grid of a page `width` wide: the page's margin clear at each
  side, so the grid's edge is the header's and the rows'.

- `pub fn for_look(width: Pixels, look: &Look) -> Self`

  The grid of a page `width` wide in this look, as `for_width`.

- `pub fn same_rows(&self, other: &TileGrid) -> bool`

  Whether `other` places the rows as this does: the same columns and
  row height, so a row is where it was and only the room between tiles
  moved.

- `pub fn rows(&self, items: usize) -> usize`

  How many rows `items` tiles take.

- `pub fn caption_gap() -> Pixels`

  Between sleeve, title and credit, as laid out.

- `pub fn caption_lines(look: &Look, side: Pixels) -> (std::ops::Range<f32>, std::ops::Range<f32>)`

  Where a tile's title and credit lines run, down from the sleeve's
  top: what the tile's owner tells its parts apart by.

- `pub fn tile_height(&self) -> Pixels`

  A tile's own height: its sleeve and caption, without the gap below.

- `pub fn caption_height(scale: &Scale) -> Pixels`

  The title and credit under a sleeve.

#### `Handler`

*type alias* · A handler a component calls with the window and the app.

```rust
pub type Handler<T> = Arc<dyn Fn(&T, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static>;
```

#### `tab_stop`

*function* · A keyboard stop kept under `id`, for a control a page draws itself: its
element tracks it, and GPUI turns Return and Space on it into a click.

```rust
pub fn tab_stop(id: &ElementId, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> FocusHandle
```

#### `focus_ring`

*function* · The focus ring for `handle`, as the kit's own controls draw it: shown
while it holds the keyboard and the keyboard was used last.

```rust
pub fn focus_ring(handle: &FocusHandle, window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Option<Div>
```

#### `CursorSpot`

*struct* · Where a list's or a grid's keyboard cursor was last laid out, owned by
the page that has the cursor and handed to what draws it (`.spot`). The
cursor's ring writes its bounds here as it is laid out, so the page's
scroll area can bring it into view (`ScrollArea::follow`) and the menu
key can open under it, both from layout rather than from a global.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct CursorSpot(_);
```

- `pub fn moved(&self)`

  The keys moved the cursor: where it was is no longer where it is,
  and the next layout brings it into view.

- `pub fn bounds(&self) -> Option<Bounds<Pixels>>`

  Where the cursor's ring was laid out since it last moved.

#### `hover_tint`

*function* · A control of a page's own whose colour changes under the pointer as the
kit's controls' do: to `lit` at once as the pointer arrives, back to
`rest` over `Motion::LEAVE` once it has gone. Returns the element, named
`id` and following the pointer, and the colour its content draws in now.

```rust
pub fn hover_tint(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    rest: Hsla,
    lit: Hsla,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> (Stateful<Div>, Hsla)
```

#### `lit_row`

*function* · A row a click acts on, lit under the pointer as controls are (`surface`,
leaving over `Motion::LEAVE`): its content goes in as children, over the
fill.

```rust
pub fn lit_row(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `spoken`

*function* · A row's or a tile's parts as a screen reader says them: the non-empty
ones, in order, between commas. What `TableRow::spoken` takes.

```rust
pub fn spoken<S: AsRef<str>>(parts: impl IntoIterator<Item = S>) -> SharedString
```

### components::page

A page: its header and its body, with the margins the page decides and
the content does not. The Mac's `PageHeader` and the page edges around
it; a page declares what it shows and nothing about where it sits.

#### `title_text`

*function* · A title standing alone, outside a page's header: the About window's
name, a confirmation's question. `title`, `strong`, kept as given.

```rust
pub fn title_text(text: impl Into<SharedString>, look: &Look) -> Div
```

#### `PageHeader`

*struct* · The header every page shares: an optional cover, the title over a
detail line (a count, a duration), the page's actions under them, and
what trails at the far edge.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct PageHeader { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(title: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A title in the app's words ("albums", "history"), drawn lowercase.

- `pub fn data(title: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A title that is data (a record, an artist, a playlist), which keeps
  its case.

- `pub fn art(self, art: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The cover or photo, leading, top-aligned with the title.

- `pub fn lead(self, item: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Above the title, side by side: a record's `PlayButton` and
  `ShuffleButton`.

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Under the title, in `control`, `muted`: a count, or a credit with its
  links.

- `pub fn badge(self, badge: super::Badge) -> Self`

  After the detail, on its line: what kind of thing the page shows, where
  that changes what can be done to it ("smart", "read-only").

- `pub fn action(self, action: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Under the detail, side by side: the page's lesser verbs, compact
  buttons with their glyphs.

- `pub fn body(self, body: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Under the actions: a paragraph about the page's subject, an artist's
  biography.

- `pub fn trailing(self, trailing: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  At the far edge: a filter field, sorts, select.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `SectionHeading`

*struct* · The heading over one of a page's sections (an artist's albums, their
EPs and singles, the records they feature on), the Mac's: `body` in
`strong`, lowercase, with the room above and below it its own. A list
that measures its items takes `SectionHeading::height`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct SectionHeading { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(title: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A heading saying `title`, an app word drawn lowercase.

- `pub fn id(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  The heading's element id, for a list where one title heads several
  sections; otherwise it is named after its title.

- `pub fn in_row(self, height: Pixels) -> Self`

  Drawn in `height`, a row's, for a list whose items are all one height
  (a uniform list's letter headings): the title sits at the row's foot,
  nearer the rows under it than the one above.

- `pub fn on_reveal(self, reveal: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Run when a screen reader moves to the heading in a list that draws
  only what is on screen, as `TableRow::on_reveal` is for a row: the
  list scrolls the reader's next stop on screen and is drawn again.

- `pub fn count(self, count: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  How many the section holds in all, in `muted` beside the title: a
  preview of the first few says how many there are.

- `pub fn on_open(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  The whole heading opens what the section previews (a shelf's "see
  all"), a disclosure glyph after the count saying so.

- `pub fn trailing(self, item: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Beside the title: the section's own play and queue.

- `pub fn height(look: &Look) -> Pixels`

  The height a heading takes with the space above and below it, for a
  list that measures its rows.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `PlayButton`

*struct* · A header's play: the page's one prominent action, as the Mac draws it
in the theme. A square in a hairline accent outline with the play glyph
in the accent, 44 points to hit, and the spinner while what it plays is
still being read.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct PlayButton { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, label: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `label` names it to VoiceOver ("play the record").

- `pub fn loading(self, loading: bool) -> Self`

  A spinner in place of the glyph while what it plays is being fetched.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Nothing to play: dimmed, as `ShuffleButton::disabled`, and not
  clickable.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press does.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `ShuffleButton`

*struct* · Beside the header's play and quieter than it: the same tracks shuffled.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ShuffleButton { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, name: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `name` is what it shuffles, a record's or a playlist's, kept in its
  own case.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Dimmed and taking no press.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press does.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Page`

*struct* · A page: the header at the page's margins, then the body, which fills the
rest and lays its own rows out at the same margins (`RowMetrics`).

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Page { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(header: PageHeader) -> Self`

  A page under `header`.

- `pub fn bare() -> Self`

  A page with no header: a grid that scrolls its header away with it.

- `pub fn body(self, body: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  What the page shows under its header, filling the rest.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `page_head`

*function* · A page's header at the page's margins: a `Page`'s own, or one a list

### components::panel

#### `MenuPanel`

*struct* · The theme's popover and menu: a square `bg` panel in a hairline `rule`,
with no arrow, no shadow and no material. What it holds is its own; a
menu's rows are `MenuRow`s.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct MenuPanel { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  An empty panel, given its rows as children.

- `pub fn min_width(self, points: Option<f32>) -> Self`

  At least `points` wide, where given; 180 points otherwise.

- `pub fn fixed(self, fixed: bool) -> Self`

  Exactly its `min_width`, whatever its rows hold: they cut what does
  not fit.

Implements `Default`, `ParentElement`, `RenderOnce`.

#### `popover_panel`

*function* · The theme's popover holding controls rather than a menu's rows (a
browser's filters): the menu panel's ground and hairline, `points` wide at
100 %, its content `m` in from the edge and `s` apart.

```rust
pub fn popover_panel(points: f32, look: &Look) -> gpui::Div
```

#### `MenuRow`

*struct* · A row of a menu: its label in `control`, `ink`, after its glyph when
icons are on, on `surface` under the pointer or the arrow keys. A choice
that is in force is ticked, on a faint accent (`surface` with more
contrast); a destructive one is in `bad`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct MenuRow { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, text: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A row saying `text`, an app word drawn lowercase.

- `pub fn columns(self, columns: Rc<ActionColumns>) -> Self`

  Keep the menu's action columns at the row's end, its own actions in
  those of their kind. Where a column holds words, a choice's tick
  stands in it: what is chosen is not moved to, so a row has one or
  the other.

- `pub fn text_action(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>, text: impl Into<SharedString>, help: impl Into<SharedString>, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  An action of a word or two, quiet as the glyphs are: "move here".

- `pub fn lit_action(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>, icon: Icon, help: impl Into<SharedString>, lit: bool, run: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A quiet, compact glyph at the row's end that does something to what
  the row names without choosing it ("forget this device"), in the
  accent while `lit`. `help` is its tooltip and its name to a screen
  reader.

- `pub fn tick_key(self, key: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  Keeps the tick's answer under `key`, which names what the row is.

- `pub fn keyed_action(self, index: Option<usize>) -> Self`

  Lights action `index` of the row, the keyboard's place within it:
  what Return runs.

- `pub fn keys(self, keys: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The keys that do the same, as a menu bar shows them: "⌘K", "Ctrl+K".

- `pub fn data(id: impl Into<ElementId>, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A row whose label is data rather than the app's words (a preset's
  name, a device's), which keeps its case.

- `pub fn icon(self, icon: Icon) -> Self`

  A glyph before the label.

- `pub fn label_data(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A label that is data rather than the app's words, kept as it is.

- `pub fn checked(self, checked: bool) -> Self`

  One of a set of choices: ticked when in force, with room for the tick
  kept when not.

- `pub fn toggle(self, toggle: bool) -> Self`

  Its tick is a setting's state, not a choice: no chosen ground.

- `pub fn highlighted(self, highlighted: bool) -> Self`

  Lit by the arrow keys, as the pointer lights it.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Not a choice now: `muted`, and nothing under the pointer.

- `pub fn opens(self, opens: bool) -> Self`

  Opens a submenu: a chevron at the row's end.

- `pub fn expanded(self, expanded: bool) -> Self`

  The submenu it opens is showing: said to a screen reader.

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>, warns: bool) -> Self`

  A second line under the label, in `fine`, `muted`; in `bad` when it
  warns.

- `pub fn rich(self, rich: bool) -> Self`

  The rich row: a place to choose, with its glyph large beside a label
  and its detail, the chosen one on the choice's ground and its glyph in
  the accent.

- `pub fn reach(self, reach: Icon) -> Self`

  A small glyph after the label saying how a place is reached.

- `pub fn destructive(self, destructive: bool) -> Self`

  In `bad`, for a row that destroys something.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What choosing the row does.

Implements `MapChecked`, `RenderOnce`.

### components::picker

### components::pill

A thing named in a collection as a chip: an artist among records and

classical credit naming the soloist, orchestra and conductor) stops at
260 points and truncates rather than laying a paragraph on its side.

Listeners attach to it as to a `div` (click, drag, menus); what a click
does is the owner's.

### components::placeholder

What a sleeve, a photo or a playlist's face shows while it has none, or
none yet: the app icon's ensō, faded back on the ground every cover is
laid on. Calm rather than broken-looking, and koan's own rather than a
generic note or a grey box. The Mac's `EnsoPlaceholder`.

The mark is an SVG the icon assets serve, so GPUI rasterises it once per
size into the atlas and every placeholder after that is one sprite.

While what it stands for is still loading, the same ensō draws itself
instead: the `Spinner`, placed so its stroke lies exactly on the mark's,
same diameter, brush and centre, in the mark's colour. Loading turning
into no picture leaves the mark where the spinner was, with nothing
moving.

#### `enso_svg`

*function* · The mark as an SVG. Its stroke is lighter than the icon's own, as the
Mac's is: at placeholder size the icon's weight reads as a fat ring
rather than a brush mark.

```rust
pub fn enso_svg() -> String
```

#### `Placeholder`

*struct* · What a cover shows while it has no picture: the ensō, faded back, or
drawing itself while one is on its way.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Placeholder { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(side: Pixels) -> Self`

  `side` is the square it fills.

- `pub fn loading(self, loading: bool) -> Self`

  Still loading: the mark draws itself.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::popover

A control that opens a panel under it (`opens_panel`): a toolbar's menu,
a filter's choices, the selection's playlists. The panel is made each
time it opens and appears at once, as a menu does; the keyboard moves
into it and goes back to where it was when it closes. A press outside,
Escape, or the trigger again closes it.

#### `Selectable`

*trait* · A trigger drawn pressed while what it opened shows.

```rust
pub trait Selectable {
    fn selected(self, selected: bool) -> Self;
}
```

#### `opens_panel`

*function* · A control that opens a panel under it, made each time it opens: a
toolbar's menu, a filter's choices, the selection's playlists. The
trigger is drawn pressed while the panel shows; the panel's rows call
the `Dismiss` they are given before they act. A press outside closes it.

```rust
pub fn opens_panel<T: Selectable + IntoElement + 'static>(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    trigger: T,
    content: impl Fn(Dismiss, &mut Window, &mut App) -> AnyElement + 'static,

```

### components::prism

Prism: the hidden rainbow appearance drawn on the GPU. A layer over the
wash that flows with the music, the beat's glow at the window's edges,
a mirror ball, confetti, a rainbow sweep between records, a sheen
over what the pointer is on, and rainbow programs for the playing bars
and the seek bar's fill (`PlayingBars::prism`, `Slider::prism`).

Everything here is a fragment program on `Animated`: the analyser's bands
go into uniforms (`Prism::hear`), and the window presents each frame with
no GPUI frame drawn. Every quad is decorative, so its clock stops under
Reduce Motion, and an app mounts none of it while the appearance is off,
which leaves it costing nothing. Where the renderer draws no shaders
(`SHADED` false) there is nothing to mount.

Two palettes: hyperpop (hot pink, cyber blue, holographic lilac, brat
lime) and brat's lime alone. The beat is an onset of the low band over
its running average, written once as a track falling from its strength to
nothing, which the programs read (sharper decays as powers of it), so a
beat fades out with nothing more written. Bursts keep their time on a
track too, held at their end once played.

#### `Prism`

*struct* · A window's prism: the uniforms the wash layer, the pulse and the mirror
ball read, fed by the analyser. Clones share it; keep one per window,
since it wakes the window it was last drawn in.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Prism { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A prism at rest, waiting for the analyser.

- `pub fn hear(&self, bands: [f32; 3], over: Duration)`

  A frame from the analyser: the three bands, each 0 to 1. A low band
  well over its running average is a beat.

- `pub fn gate(&self) -> FlashGate`

  The window's one flash gate, which its bursts share (`Burst::sharing`):
  a beat and a burst, however they fall, start at most three times a
  second between them.

- `pub fn hold_bands(&self, bands: [f32; 3])`

  The bands standing where they are, for a prism newly fed.

- `pub fn style(&self, dark: bool, brat: bool, scale: f32)`

  The appearance, the palette and the window's scale.

- `pub fn run(&self, running: bool)`

  Whether its clock runs: while the appearance is on, a window of the
  app is in front and it shows. Still, it holds its last frame and the
  mirror ball stops turning.

- `pub fn wash(&self) -> AnimatedQuad`

  The layer over the wash, under the page.

- `pub fn window(&self, width: f32, height: f32)`

  The window's size in points, which the disco layer's spots wheel
  across.

- `pub fn ball_at(&self, right: f32, top: f32)`

  Where the mirror ball hangs, in points in from the window's right
  and down from its top, which the disco layer's spots wheel out from.

- `pub fn disco(&self) -> AnimatedQuad`

  The disco layer, over the page: spots, beams, glimmers, glitter and
  the beat's flash.

- `pub fn party(&self) -> AnimatedQuad`

  The party over the page: lasers, pyro on the beat, floating hearts,
  and the beat's glow at the window's edges.

- `pub fn ball(&self) -> AnimatedQuad`

  The mirror ball, as large as it is laid out.

- `pub fn hang_ball(&self)`

  Hangs the ball in the window's top right corner (`BALL_TOP`,
  `BALL_RIGHT`), where `hung_ball` lays it out: the disco layer's spots
  wheel out from its centre.

- `pub fn hung_ball(&self, look: &crate::theme::Look) -> impl IntoElement + use<>`

  The ball laid out where `hang_ball` says it hangs, over the window.

Implements `Default`.

#### `Prisms`

*struct* · Each window's prism, for the sheens and gradients drawn in it to run
with: registered by the window's root while the rainbow is drawn.

```rust
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Prisms(_);
```

- `pub fn set(window: WindowId, prism: Option<Prism>, cx: &mut App)`

  `prism` is `window`'s, or with `None` the window has none.

Implements `Global`.

#### `Burst`

*struct* · A burst drawn over the whole window, fired at a point: confetti, or the
rainbow sweep between records.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Burst { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn sharing(self, gate: &FlashGate) -> Self`

  Shares `gate` with the window's prism, so its throws and the beat's
  flashes keep one gap between them.

- `pub fn confetti() -> Self`

  Confetti thrown from the point it is fired at.

- `pub fn sticker() -> Self`

  The sticker: brat's when fired with `brat`, the chrome star
  otherwise.

- `pub fn sweep() -> Self`

  The rainbow's sweep across the window between records.

- `pub fn fire(&self, at: Point<Pixels>, scale: f32, brat: bool)`

  Throws it from `at`, in the window's coordinates, scaled `scale`
  device pixels to a point. Nothing is thrown under Reduce Motion: the
  quad is decorative, so its clock does not start. A throw within
  `MIN_BEAT_GAP` of the last flash its gate saw, a beat or a burst, is
  dropped, so flashes start at most three times a second.

- `pub fn fire_with(&self, at: Point<Pixels>, other: &Burst, other_at: Point<Pixels>, scale: f32, brat: bool)`

  Throws it from `at` and `other` from `other_at` as one start: two
  bursts landing together are one flash, so the gate is asked once and
  either both fly or neither does. The confetti and the sticker as the
  appearance comes out.

- `pub fn length(&self) -> Duration`

  How long it flies once fired.

- `pub fn live(&self) -> bool`

  Fired and still flying: mounted only then.

- `pub fn element(&self) -> AnimatedQuad`

  The quad, laid out over the whole window.

#### `FlashGate`

*struct* · The time the last flash started, shared by a window's prism and bursts.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct FlashGate(_);
```

#### `Sheen`

*struct* · A band of the palette passing over its parent while it is mounted: put it
in a `relative` element the pointer is on, or over a title.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Sheen { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  A sheen in the rainbow's palette.

- `pub fn glitch(self, glitch: bool) -> Self`

  Every five seconds, for a blink, slices of what it covers turn pink
  and cyan: the playing title's glitch.

- `pub fn brat(self, brat: bool) -> Self`

  In brat's palette.

- `pub fn strength(self, strength: f32) -> Self`

  How strongly the band shows, 0 to 1.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `PrismFill`

*struct* · The palette as a gradient filling its parent, moving while it is drawn:
what carries the accent in a component carries this instead while the
rainbow is drawn (`KoanTheme::prism_gradient`).

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct PrismFill { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, brat: bool) -> Self`

  A fill in the rainbow's palette, or in brat's.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::progress

Something under way, as a row: what it is, how far it has come in counts,
a line of detail, and a bar that fills as it goes or, with no measure,
runs a segment along it; a stop button where it can be stopped. The row
decides its own spacing, room below the bar included, so a list of them
at a sidebar's foot never sits on the rule beneath.

#### `ProgressBar`

*struct* · A bar on its own: `fraction` from 0 to 1, or `None` while there is no
measure, when a segment runs along it (still with Reduce Motion).

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ProgressBar { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, fraction: Option<f32>) -> Self`

  A bar filled to `fraction`, or running a segment along it while the
  amount is unknown.

- `pub fn thin(self) -> Self`

  A hairline high, as an edge it draws along: the mini player's top.

- `pub fn glide(self, glide: &super::Glide) -> Self`

  Filled to `glide`'s value as it moves, on the GPU, where the renderer
  draws shaders: a transfer's bar, fed by its reports, with no frame
  drawn between them. Elsewhere the bar shows `fraction`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `ProgressRow`

*struct* · Something under way, as a row: what it is, its counts and a line of
detail over a bar, with a stop button where it can be stopped.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ProgressRow { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, title: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `title` says what is under way ("scanning the library").

- `pub fn flush(self, flush: bool) -> Self`

  No padding of its own, for a container that pads it (a card).

- `pub fn counts(self, counts: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  How far it has come, in its own units ("1,204 of 5,000 files").

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  Under the bar: the file or record it is on.

- `pub fn fraction(self, fraction: Option<f32>) -> Self`

  From 0 to 1; left unset, the bar runs a segment.

- `pub fn on_stop(self, stop: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A stop button at the bar's end.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::progress_glides

Something's progress, reported now and then (a download, a scan), as
the `Glide`s its rings, slider and bar are drawn from: each report sets
where it stands and the rate it moves at, and the GPU fills between
reports with no frame drawn and nothing read on a timer. Past
`horizon` with no report, the glides rest where they reckoned rather
than run on.

#### `ProgressReport`

*struct* · One report: the share done while the whole is known, the share it grows
by each second, and when.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct ProgressReport {
    pub share: Option<f32>,
    pub rate: f32,
    pub at: Instant,
}
```

- `pub fn apply(self, glide: &Glide)`

  Into a glide: the share and its rate, or turning while the whole is
  unknown.

- `pub fn partial(self) -> bool`

  Under way and not all done.

#### `ProgressGlides`

*struct* · One thing's progress as glides: a ring's per colour (a ring writes its
colour into its glide), a slider's reach and a bar's, each styled by
what draws it.

```rust
pub struct ProgressGlides { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(horizon: Duration) -> Self`

  Glides reckoned at most `horizon` past a report: about the gap
  between a healthy feed's reports.

- `pub fn ring(&mut self, color: Hsla) -> Glide`

  A ring's glide in `color`, made now if none is: a new colour starts
  where the last report left the others, or turning before any.

- `pub fn reach(&self) -> &Glide`

  A slider's reach (`Slider::reach`).

- `pub fn bar(&self) -> &Glide`

  A bar's (`ProgressBar`).

- `pub fn last(&self) -> Option<ProgressReport>`

  The last report taken.

- `pub fn take(&mut self, report: ProgressReport) -> bool`

  Takes a report into every glide. A share below the last one is the
  work starting afresh (a download retried): the glides start again
  from it rather than standing where the last run finished, which a
  reckoned glide would otherwise hold. Whether it is now partial when
  it was not, or the other way, or this is the first report: what a
  page drawing only partial progress must draw again for.

- `pub fn end(&self)`

  It has ended: every glide stops where it stands.

### components::qr

A QR code as quads: its runs of dark modules worked out once, then
painted as one quad per run, dark on a light quiet zone whatever the
window's appearance, as phones read best.

#### `QrCode`

*struct* · A code's dark modules, as horizontal runs: (row, first column, length),
on a grid `side` modules square.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct QrCode { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(dark: &[bool], side: usize) -> Self`

  From the modules row by row, `side` to a row, `true` where dark. A
  module the slice falls short of is light: a short slice draws a code
  that does not read, rather than taking the window down.

- `pub fn element(&self, side: Pixels, margin: Pixels) -> impl IntoElement + use<>`

  The code drawn `side` across, inside a light quiet zone `margin` wide.

### components::ring

How far something has come, as a ring filling from twelve o'clock: a
download's bytes. With no size to measure against, seven-tenths of the
ring turn instead.

Given a `Glide` (`ProgressRing::glide`) and a renderer with shaders, the
ring is a shader quad: the share at a moment and its rate, reckoned per
present, so it fills smoothly between the transfer's reports with no
frame drawn, and turns while the size is unknown. Elsewhere, as the
spinner is drawn: the ring's dots laid out once per size, close enough to
read as one line, and a frame choosing how many to draw from where. An
indeterminate ring asks for frames only while it is painted.

#### `ProgressRing`

*struct* · How far something has come, as a ring filling from twelve o'clock, or
turning while there is no measure.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ProgressRing { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(progress: Option<f32>, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  `progress` from 0 to 1, or `None` while the size is unknown. Sized as
  a glyph beside `fine` type, as the availability marks are.

- `pub fn glide(self, glide: &Glide) -> Self`

  Filled from `glide`, the transfer's share at a moment and its rate,
  reckoned per present where the renderer draws shaders (`Glide::set`,
  or `set_unknown` while the size is unknown). Elsewhere the ring draws
  the glide's value as it was last set. The ring writes its colour into
  the glide, so a glide is drawn in one colour: one per colour, and
  never one a slider also draws.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Sized as a glyph beside `role` type instead.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::root

What every window's content sits in: Tab and Shift-Tab through the

(`focus_trap`), under the `Root` key context the app's bindings name.

*struct* · What every window's content sits in, made by `host`: Tab kept inside
an open sheet or menu, under the `Root` key context.

```rust
pub struct Root { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(view: impl Into<AnyView>) -> Self`

  A root over `view`.

Implements `Render`.

### components::row_list

A long list of rows of differing heights, the queue's and a playlist's:
only the rows on screen are made, each measured as it is drawn, with the
kōan scrollbar over the list's edge. The state is the page's to keep, so
its place survives the page drawing again; rows not yet measured are
guessed at from their kind, which sizes the scrollbar from the first
frame.

#### `RowListState`

*struct* · The list's place and measurements, kept by the page across frames.

The list may lead with one item before its rows, a page's header that
scrolls with them (`RowList::lead`); row indices count the rows alone.

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct RowListState(_, _, _);
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A list at its top with no rows yet.

- `pub fn set_lead(&self, lead: bool, count: usize, kind: RowKind)`

  Whether the list leads with a header. Rows are re-measured.

- `pub fn reset(&self, count: usize, kind: RowKind)`

  `count` rows, none measured, each guessed at as `kind`. The place is
  kept as far as the new rows reach.

- `pub fn top(&self) -> RowOffset`

  The row at the top; within the leading header, the first row.

- `pub fn scroll_to(&self, at: RowOffset)`

  Scrolls to `at`, a row and an offset into it.

- `pub fn scroll_to_top(&self)`

  Scrolls to the first row.

- `pub fn reveal(&self, ix: usize)`

  Scrolls as little as shows row `ix` whole.

- `pub fn centre(&self, ix: usize)`

  Scrolls row `ix` to the middle of what shows of the list between the
  bar and the foot drawn over it, as far as the list's ends allow. Done
  as the list next draws, which knows those insets.

- `pub fn reveal_around(&self, ix: usize)`

  Scrolls the neighbour of row `ix` a screen reader is heading for on
  screen, since only rows drawn are in the accessibility tree
  (`reveal_around_list`).

- `pub fn row_bounds(&self, ix: usize) -> Option<Bounds<Pixels>>`

  Where row `ix` was laid out last frame, in the window: for a row
  past the viewport's edge, outside it. `None` for a row the list has
  not laid out.

- `pub fn on_scroll(&self, f: impl Fn(usize, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static)`

  Called as the list scrolls, with the first row on screen. The list's
  own state is held while it runs, so it reads the page, not the list.

Implements `Default`.

#### `RowList`

*struct* · A long list of rows of differing heights, made only while on screen and
measured as drawn, under the kōan scrollbar.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct RowList { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(state: &RowListState, row: impl FnMut(usize, &mut Window, &mut App) -> AnyElement + 'static) -> Self`

  `row` makes row `ix` as it comes on screen.

- `pub fn lead(self, lead: impl FnMut(&mut Window, &mut App) -> AnyElement + 'static) -> Self`

  An item before the rows that scrolls with them: a page's header,
  which then passes under the bar with the rows rather than holding
  them below a line. The state must have been told (`RowListState::set_lead`).

- `pub fn under_bar(self) -> Self`

  A page's list that scrolls under a phone's bar and its foot: its
  leading item starts the bar's height down (`bar_inset`), its last row
  ends the foot's height up (`foot_inset`), and the rows pass under both
  as it scrolls. The state must lead (`RowListState::set_lead`).

- `pub fn under_foot(self) -> Self`

  A list under a header that holds it clear of the bar: its rows pass
  under the phone's foot alone (`foot_inset`).

- `pub fn follow(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Holds the keyboard cursor at `spot` clear of the bar and the foot the
  rows pass under as the keys move it (`keep_clear`).

- `pub fn named(self, name: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What the list is of, for a screen reader: "queue", a playlist's name.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::rows

#### `NavRow`

*struct* · A navigation row, as the sidebar's: the label in `body`, `muted` (`ink`
under touch, which has no hover to brighten it), after
its glyph when icons are on; where you are, in the accent with a 2-point
accent rule on the leading edge, and no fill.

A place with a face of its own (a playlist) carries its art before the
label and a line of detail under it. Listeners attach as to a `div`, so
a row can take drops.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct NavRow { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, text: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  A place in a navigation list, named by an app word drawn lowercase.

- `pub fn data(id: impl Into<ElementId>, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A place named by data (a playlist), which keeps its case.

- `pub fn arrivals(self, count: u64) -> Self`

  How many times something has arrived at the place (music queued or
  added to a playlist from a menu): each new arrival seen while the
  row is drawn glows the row's ground and lets it settle.

- `pub fn accept<T: 'static>(self, handler: impl Fn(&T, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Something dropped on the row (tracks on the queue, a record on a
  playlist): the row answers with a line drawn under it, so the person
  sees where it went, then `handler` takes it.

- `pub fn accept_if<T: 'static>(self, takes: impl Fn(&T) -> bool + 'static, handler: impl Fn(&T, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  `accept`, for what `takes` says the row takes: a drop it does not
  take is let go without the answer saying it arrived.

- `pub fn spot(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Where the cursor's ring tells its page it was laid out, while this
  is under the cursor: what the page's scroll area follows and the menu
  key opens under.

- `pub fn cursor(self, cursor: bool) -> Self`

  Under the keyboard's cursor: its list holds the keyboard and the
  arrows have come to it. Drawn with the focus ring while the keyboard
  was used last.

- `pub fn art(self, art: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The place's own face, square, before the label: a playlist's sleeve.
  Drawn whatever "show icons" says, as covers are.

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A line under the label, in `meta`, `muted`: a playlist's count.

- `pub fn trailing(self, figure: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  A figure at the trailing edge, in `fine`, `muted`: how many
  downloads are running. Only while there is one to say.

- `pub fn icon(self, icon: Icon) -> Self`

  The place's glyph, leading.

- `pub fn selected(self, selected: bool) -> Self`

  The place showing: on the selection's ground.

- `pub fn on_click(self, handler: impl Fn(&ClickEvent, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a press does: usually going to the place.

Implements `InteractiveElement`, `RenderOnce`, `StatefulInteractiveElement`.

### components::scroll

A page that scrolls, with the kōan scrollbar over its edge: shown while
it scrolls and while the pointer is over that edge, draggable, taking no
width from what scrolls (`Scrollbar`); a list keeps its own handle and
lays a `Scrollbar` over itself the same way.

#### `ScrollArea`

*struct* · A page that scrolls, with the kōan scrollbar over its edge.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct ScrollArea { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, content: impl IntoElement + Styled + 'static) -> Self`

  `content` is the column that scrolls, styled as the page needs.

- `pub fn items(id: impl Into<ElementId>, items: Vec<AnyElement>) -> Self`

  A column of items, one under another, any of which `reveal` can
  scroll into view: a list the arrow keys move through.

- `pub fn shrinks(self) -> Self`

  In a flex column whose height is only capped (a panel with a
  `max_h`): takes the room the column has left rather than its
  content's height, so it scrolls within the cap instead of being
  clipped by it.

- `pub fn under_bar(self) -> Self`

  A page that scrolls under a phone's bar and its foot: its content
  starts the bar's height down (`bar_inset`) and ends the foot's height
  up from the end of the scroll (`foot_inset`), so at either end
  nothing is under them, and passes under both as it scrolls. Not for a
  sheet, a menu or a panel, which neither is ever over.

- `pub fn under_foot(self) -> Self`

  The body of a page whose header holds it clear of the bar: it passes
  under the phone's foot alone (`foot_inset`).

- `pub fn handle(self, handle: &ScrollHandle) -> Self`

  Scrolled through the owner's handle, which reads how far it is
  scrolled: a page folding its header as its body scrolls.

- `pub fn reveal(self, ix: Option<usize>) -> Self`

  Scrolls item `ix` into view when it changes, and leaves the scroll
  to the pointer otherwise.

- `pub fn follow(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Brings a keyboard cursor into view each time the keys move it,
  wherever it is laid out inside the content (a pill in a flow, a tile
  in a row), the same item again included; the pointer's scrolling is
  left alone otherwise.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::scrollbar

The kōan scrollbar, which every scrolling surface lays over its right
edge: thin and square on no track, `muted` at rest and `ink` under the
pointer or held, taking no width from what scrolls. It shows at once
while the content scrolls or the pointer is over its edge, and after a
rest fades out quickly (`Motion::LEAVE`), on the GPU: a shader quad whose
opacity is a track, so the fade draws no frame. Drag the thumb to scroll,
or press the edge to bring the thumb there.

A screen reader scrolls the content itself, which GPUI reports as a
scroll area; the bar is how the content's place is drawn, and is left
out of the accessibility tree.

#### `ScrollSource`

*trait* · What a scrollbar reads and moves: a scrolling div's `ScrollHandle`, a
`uniform_list`'s handle or a `list`'s state. Offsets are GPUI's, zero at
the top and negative down the content.

```rust
pub trait ScrollSource: Clone + 'static {
    fn offset(&self) -> Point<Pixels>;
    fn set_offset(&self, offset: Point<Pixels>);
    /// How far the content scrolls: zero when it fits.
    fn max_offset(&self) -> Pixels;
    fn start_drag(&self);
    fn end_drag(&self);
}
```

#### `Scrollbar`

*struct* · The vertical scrollbar over its parent, which is `relative` and the
size of what scrolls.

```rust
pub struct Scrollbar<S: ScrollSource> { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn vertical(source: &S) -> Self`

  A scrollbar over `source`'s vertical scroll.

- `pub fn under_bar(self) -> Self`

  Over content that scrolls under a phone's bar and its foot: the
  track runs between them (`bar_inset`, `foot_inset`).

- `pub fn under_foot(self) -> Self`

  Over content that scrolls under a phone's foot alone: the track ends
  above it (`foot_inset`).

Implements `Element`.

  What choosing an option does, given its index.

Implements `RenderOnce`.
