### components::slide

A panel at the trailing edge of a page whose width eases in and out over
`Motion::SETTLE`: the lyrics, an inspector. The panel is laid out at its
full width and clipped, so it does not reflow; the page beside it does,
each frame. At once under Reduce Motion. The owner keeps the panel while
the width eases away and lets it go once its `SlideState` says it has
closed; a closed panel draws nothing.

Made `resizable`, the panel's leading edge carries `SplitView`'s grip:
dragging it left widens the panel, and a double click restores its reset
width. The width is the owner's to keep.

#### `SlideState`

*struct* · How far a slide stands open, as last drawn: held by the owner, which
lets the panel go once `closed`.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct SlideState(_);
```

- `pub fn closed(&self) -> bool`

  Whether the panel has eased fully shut, so its owner can drop it.

#### `Slide`

*struct* · A panel at the trailing edge of a page whose width eases in and out.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Slide { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, state: &SlideState, shown: bool, width: Pixels) -> Self`

  A panel `width` wide, shown or easing shut, its progress kept in
  `state`.

- `pub fn resizable(self, min: Pixels, max: Pixels, reset: Pixels) -> Self`

  A grip on the leading edge moves the width between `min` and `max`;
  a double click restores `reset`.

- `pub fn on_resize(self, f: impl Fn(&Pixels, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Each width a drag reaches, for the owner to draw.

- `pub fn on_commit(self, f: impl Fn(&Pixels, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  The width a drag ended on or a double click restored, for the owner
  to keep.

- `pub fn child(self, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The panel's content, laid out at its full width.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::slider

#### `Slider`

*struct* · A 1-point `rule` track with a 3-point accent fill up to the value, and a
square 8 × 8 `ink` thumb shown only on hover, focus or drag. The values
at either end sit in `fine`, `muted`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Slider { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, value: f32) -> Self`

  `value` is a fraction of the track, 0 to 1.

- `pub fn prism(self, prism: Option<bool>) -> Self`

  With a `glide`, the fill as a rainbow trail running back from the
  playhead: the hidden rainbow appearance's seek bar.

- `pub fn glide(self, glide: &Glide) -> Self`

  The value moves on its own from `value` as `glide` says; see `Glide`.

- `pub fn reach(self, reach: &Glide) -> Self`

  How far the value can go, as a share moving on its own: a seek bar's
  track still downloading, as far as its bytes reach. The track's rule
  is dimmed past it, as the Mac's is, so a file all here looks like the
  ordinary bar; drawn by the same shader as the glide where the
  renderer has shaders.

- `pub fn named(self, name: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  What it sets, as a screen reader names it ("seek").

- `pub fn ends(self, low: impl Into<SharedString>, high: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What sits at either end, such as the time played and the time left.

- `pub fn ends_width(self, chars: usize) -> Self`

  Both ends in slots `chars` characters wide, the low one's word at
  the slot's start and the high one's at its end: the sliders of a
  form, given the same width, start and stop their rails level with
  each other however long their words.

- `pub fn step(self, step: f32) -> Self`

  How far an arrow key moves it.

- `pub fn pending(self, pending: bool) -> Self`

  A change sent and not yet answered (a seek on its way to another
  device): the thumb stays shown where it was let go, in the muted ink,
  which says the slider heard and is waiting.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Dimmed and taking no drag.

- `pub fn compact(self) -> Self`

  At the desktop's density, 20 points tall rather than the touch
  target's 44: a transport's seek bar under its controls.

- `pub fn on_change(self, handler: impl Fn(&f32, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What each step of a drag or a key does, given the value from 0 to 1.

- `pub fn on_commit(self, handler: impl Fn(&(), &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Called once a change is finished: a drag let go, or an arrow key's
  step. For a value too costly to apply at every step of a drag.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Glide`

*struct* · A value that moves on its own between renders, as a seek bar's playhead
does: the share of the track at a moment and how fast it moves from it,
written as keyframes (#1859). Where the renderer draws shaders, the fill
and the thumb are a shader quad drawing the value's track at present time,
so the slider glides at the display's rate with no frame drawn and no
timer; elsewhere, and while dragged, the slider draws `value` as given.
The owner writes it from any thread when the anchor changes, and keeps it
across renders.

Tracks: 0 the value, 1 a small correction easing out, 2 the turn shown
while the size is unknown; plain value 0 is 1 while it is unknown, and 1
the value a second past the anchor, past which a ring does not reckon.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub struct Glide(pub(crate) _, _, _);
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A glide standing at nothing.

- `pub fn set(&self, value: f32, rate: f32, since: Instant)`

  `value` at `since`, moving `rate` of the track a second, until the
  end. A new anchor that disagrees a little with where the slider had
  reckoned it, while playing on at the same rate, is eased into over
  `SETTLE` rather than jumped to: the fill moves continuously and
  corrects without a step. A seek, a pause or any larger difference
  moves at once.

- `pub fn reckon_for(&self, horizon: Duration)`

  Reckons at most `horizon` past each anchor, then rests where that
  leaves it until the next: for a value whose rate is only a guess, as a
  download's, which can stall with no report saying so. A stalled one
  is then never drawn as further on than it can be, and stops
  presenting. A playhead, whose rate holds, reckons to the end.

- `pub fn set_moving(&self, moving: bool)`

  Not moving, the value comes to rest where it stands: nothing presents
  for it. Moving again, it runs on from the anchor it was last set to,
  so a window hidden and shown again, which sets the same anchor, does
  not leave it standing.

- `pub fn known(&self) -> bool`

  Whether a value has been set since the last `set_unknown`.

- `pub fn set_unknown(&self)`

  No value to show, as a download whose size is unknown: a ring turns
  instead, until a value is set.

- `pub fn drawn(&self) -> f32`

  Where the fill is drawn now: the reckoning and any correction still
  easing out, as the program draws them.

- `pub fn value(&self) -> f32`

  The value as last set, before any motion since.

### components::slot

Colour slots: colours that move on the GPU. A slot is a dynamic colour
(gpui's `ColorHandle`) whose value the kit works out at each present from
two ends and a track, mixed in sRGB or OKLab, so a colour fading or the
accent turning moves with no frame drawn. The kit's colour source
(`install`) evaluates every claimed slot at the present's time and says
until when each moves, so a shader or window stays presenting only while
a slot it draws with moves.

It also keeps the kit's present clock: the latest time a present was
made for, which a retarget samples a moving value at.

#### `present_now`

*function* · When a frame made now reaches the screen, as the last present said:
what a retarget samples a moving value at. Never before now.

```rust
pub fn present_now() -> Instant
```

#### `install_color_slots`

*function* · Installs the kit's colour source: every claimed slot evaluated at the
present's time. Called once, by `theme::init`.

```rust
pub fn install()
```

#### `ColorSlot`

*struct* · One colour slot, held while something draws with it and freed when
dropped.

```rust
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ColorSlot(_);
```

- `pub fn claim(color: Rgba) -> Option<Self>`

  A free slot standing at `color`, or `None` when every slot is taken.

- `pub fn hsla(&self, alpha: f32) -> Hsla`

  The colour to draw with: this slot, its alpha scaled by `alpha`.

- `pub fn at(&self, at: Instant) -> Rgba`

  The colour the slot shows at `at`.

- `pub fn to(&self, to: Rgba, track: Track)`

  Moves from the colour shown now to `to`, along `track`'s curve over
  its length, starting now; `track`'s ends and start are replaced.

- `pub fn to_oklab(&self, to: Rgba, track: Track)`

  Moves from the colour shown now to `to` as `to` does, mixed in OKLab:
  the hue turns rather than passing through grey.

- `pub fn hold(&self, color: Rgba)`

  Stands at `color`.

- `pub fn moving(&self, at: Instant) -> bool`

  Whether the slot moves at `at`.

Implements `Drop`.

### components::sparkline

A line through a series of values, small: an EQ curve's thumbnail on its
stage's block.

#### `Sparkline`

*struct* · `values` from `-range` to `range`, left to right across the line's box,
zero at the middle. Drawn at two points a pixel at most, which is all a
line this small can show. Nothing is drawn for fewer than two values.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Sparkline { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(values: Vec<f64>, range: f64, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  `values` as a line across its bounds, scaled to `range`, in `color`.

- `pub fn dash(self, dash: Vec<f32>) -> Self`

  Dashed: on and off lengths, in pixels.

- `pub fn size(self, size: Size<Pixels>) -> Self`

  The line's box, 64 × 26 by default.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::spinner

Something on its way: the ensō drawing itself, the brush's head running
round the circle and then its tail following it off, looping. The same
mark as the `Placeholder`, so loading and empty read as one family.

Where the renderer draws shaders, one `AnimatedQuad` on the clock: the
stroke is baked once into a small field (`FIELD`), each texel holding how
far along the brush its nearest point is and how far from the brush's
line it lies, and the program draws the part between the tail and the
head at each present, edges and ends antialiased at any size. A spinner
costs no frame while it turns, every spinner turns in step, and one
scrolled away or behind a page is not in the scene. With Reduce Motion
the whole mark stands still.

Elsewhere the stroke is laid out once per size as a run of round quads
close enough to read as one line, and each frame chooses which are drawn.

#### `Spinner`

*struct* · Something on its way: the ensō drawing itself, looping, or still under
Reduce Motion.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Spinner { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(side: Pixels) -> Self`

  `side` is the square it is drawn in.

- `pub fn announced(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>, label: impl AsRef<str>) -> Self`

  Standing for content on its way, as a loading pane's spinner does:
  a screen reader announces `label` ("loading keys") politely as it
  appears. A spinner beside words that already say so, or in a control
  that has its own name, stays decorative.

- `pub fn color(self, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  Drawn in `color` rather than `muted`: the placeholder's faded mark.

- `pub fn glow(self) -> Self`

  A faint halo round the stroke as it turns, in the accent unless a
  colour is given: a small wait beside a control (a check, a fetch) that
  should read as kōan working rather than as something greyed. Drawn
  past the spinner's square, which it leaves the layout as it was.
  Where the renderer draws no shaders the stroke turns without it.

- `pub fn reserving(self, room: Size<Pixels>) -> Self`

  Holds exactly `room`, the size of what is loading, with the spinner
  in its middle.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::split

A split view: a leading column the person can resize beside the rest, as
the Mac's sidebar. The divider is a hairline with a wider grip that shows
the resize cursor; dragging it moves the column between its least and
greatest widths, and a double click puts it back. The width is the
owner's, which keeps it (with the window's bounds, for the sidebar).

Hidden and shown, the column's width eases to nothing and back over
`Motion::SETTLE`. Its content is laid out at full width and clipped, so
nothing in it reflows; the page beside it is laid out again at each
frame's width. At once under Reduce Motion. A hidden column draws
nothing from the frame its width reaches nothing.

#### `SplitView`

*struct* · A leading column the person can resize beside the rest, as the Mac's
sidebar.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]

```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, width: Pixels, min: Pixels, max: Pixels, reset: Pixels) -> Self`

  `width` is the leading column's, held by the owner; `min` and `max`
  bound a drag, and `reset` is what a double click restores.

- `pub fn leading(self, leading: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The leading column; without one the trailing fills the view.

- `pub fn shown(self, shown: bool) -> Self`

  Whether the leading column shows; its width eases as this changes,
  so the owner hands it over whether shown or not.

- `pub fn trailing(self, trailing: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  What sits beside the column.

- `pub fn on_resize(self, f: impl Fn(&Pixels, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Each width a drag reaches, for the owner to draw.

- `pub fn on_commit(self, f: impl Fn(&Pixels, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  The width a drag ended on or a double click restored, for the owner
  to keep.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::stepper

#### `Stepper`

*struct* · A value moved a step at a time: the title leading in `body`, `ink`, and
two square buttons trailing, each disabled at its end of the range.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Stepper { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, title: impl AsRef<str>, value: i64, range: std::ops::RangeInclusive<i64>) -> Self`

  A row titled `title`, an app word drawn lowercase, holding `value`
  within `range`, with buttons to step it.

- `pub fn step(self, step: i64) -> Self`

  How far one press moves the value; 1 unless given.

- `pub fn shown(self, text: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The value as the row shows it, in `control`, `muted`, before the
  buttons.

- `pub fn disabled(self, disabled: bool) -> Self`

  Dimmed and taking no press.

- `pub fn on_change(self, handler: impl Fn(&i64, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What a step does, given the new value.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::suggestions

What a search field suggests as it is typed into: a square panel laid
under the field and over what follows it, headed groups of lines, each
line a sleeve or a glyph, a title and a line of detail. One line is lit
at a time, by the arrows or the pointer, on `surface`.

The panel is placed by its parent: it hangs from the bottom of the
nearest `relative` element and paints above everything else in the
window. What lighting and taking a line do is the owner's.

#### `SuggestionPanel`

*struct* · What a search field suggests as it is typed into: a panel under the
field, over what follows it.


  An empty panel, given its headings and rows as children.

Implements `InteractiveElement`, `ParentElement`, `RenderOnce`, `StatefulInteractiveElement`.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]

```

- `pub fn sleeve(look: &Look, tall: bool) -> Pixels`

  A suggestion names a thing, so its title keeps its case.

- `pub fn tall(self) -> Self`

  A full row; see [`SuggestionRow::sleeve`].

- `pub fn round(self) -> Self`

  The art drawn round, for an artist's photo.

  place, in the accent once picked.

- `pub fn figure(self, figure: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

- `pub fn detail(self, detail: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

### components::table

A table's row, as the Mac's lists draw theirs: fixed cells leading (a
status, a number, a sleeve), the title over its lines of detail flexing
and truncating, and fixed figure columns trailing, right-aligned (a
codec, a length). Its height, padding, inset and column gap are
`RowMetrics`', so every list's rows line up under the page's header.

It takes elements, not records: what a cell shows is the page's, where it
sits is the row's. Listeners attach to it as to a `div` (clicks, drags,
drops, menus), and its selection is drawn here. A row has no hover fill
(the user's call over the spec's 30 % `hover`): what shows under the
pointer is the play mark and the heart, through `ROW_GROUP`.

#### `table_list`

*function* · A virtualised list of `TableRow`s: only the rows on screen are made, so a
table of tens of thousands (a library's organise plan) costs a screenful.
`rows` makes the rows of a range; with `cx.processor` it reads the view.

```rust
pub fn table_list(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    count: usize,
    rows: impl 'static + Fn(
        std::ops::Range<usize>,
        &mut Window,
        &mut App,
    ) -> Vec<TableRow>,
) -> UniformList
```

#### `TableRow`

*struct* · A row of a table: leading cells, a title over lines that flexes and
truncates, and figure columns trailing, at the heights `RowMetrics`
gives its kind.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct TableRow { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, kind: RowKind) -> Self`

  A row of `kind`, given its cells by the methods.

- `pub fn landed(self, landing: Option<u64>) -> Self`

  The row has just landed where it is (dropped there, moved there,
  added from elsewhere), the `landing`th time its list has had rows
  land: its ground lifts to the accent and settles back, under its
  text, so the eye finds where the rows went. Drawn new, it answers
  too, since arriving is the news.

- `pub fn seam(self, first: bool) -> Self`

  Of rows landing, the first: the insertion line they were dropped at
  closes into it along its top edge as its ground lifts.

- `pub fn spoken(self, name: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The row as one node for a screen reader, read as `name` (the title,
  its credit and record, its length and state): a list item, or a
  heading for a record's heading. The controls inside stay reachable as
  its children.

- `pub fn place(self, ix: usize, count: usize) -> Self`

  Row `ix` of `count`, so a reader says where it is in a list that
  draws only what is on screen.

- `pub fn on_reveal(self, reveal: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  Run when a screen reader moves to the row: the list scrolls the
  neighbour the reader is heading for on screen, so it can go on past
  what was drawn (`RowListState::reveal_around`, `reveal_around`), and
  the view that drew the row is drawn again so the neighbour reaches the
  tree even on an idle window.

- `pub fn lead(self, points: f32, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A cell before the title, `points` wide, its content at its trailing
  edge so a column of numbers lines up.

- `pub fn lead_exact(self, width: Pixels, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A leading cell measured by the list, as a number column wide enough
  for its longest number.

- `pub fn title(self, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  The title, in `body` and `ink` unless the content says otherwise.

- `pub fn line(self, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A line under the title, in `meta` and `muted` unless the content says
  otherwise: a credit, a record, a count.

- `pub fn figure(self, points: f32, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A figure column after the title, `points` wide and right-aligned, in
  `meta` and `muted`.

- `pub fn figure_fitted(self, points: f32, content: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  A figure column at least `points` wide that grows to fit its
  content, taking the room from the title: for a figure whose words
  vary, which a fixed column would clip at a large text size.

- `pub fn selected(self, selected: bool) -> Self`

  Picked: on `selection`, its title, lines and figures in
  `on_selection` unless their content says otherwise, which a page
  that colours its cells does by `on_selection` too.

- `pub fn spot(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Where the cursor's ring tells its page it was laid out, while this
  is under the cursor: what the page's scroll area follows and the menu
  key opens under.

- `pub fn cursor(self, cursor: bool) -> Self`

  Under the keyboard's cursor: its list holds the keyboard and the
  arrows have come to it. Drawn with the focus ring while the keyboard
  was used last.

Implements `InteractiveElement`, `RenderOnce`, `StatefulInteractiveElement`, `Styled`.

#### `ROW_GROUP`

*constant* · The hover group every row is, for what shows only under the pointer:
the play button over the number, the heart.

```rust
pub const ROW_GROUP: &str = "koan-row";
```

`RowPlayButton`: the play mark in the accent, which plays from the row.


- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, number: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  `muted`, as the Mac's browsers do: the microphone for an artist.

- `pub fn current(self, current: bool) -> Self`

  The row is the one playing or paused: its number in the accent.

- `pub fn picked(self, picked: bool) -> Self`

  The row is picked: the number, glyph and play mark in `on_selection`,
  on the row's `selection` fill, where `muted` and the accent fade.

- `pub fn bars(self, bars: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Drawn in the number's place while the row is current, playing or
  paused: the bars fall flat on a pause rather than giving the number
  back.

- `pub fn on_play(self, play: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  What the play button over the number does.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::tile

A record's tile in a grid, as the album browser and an artist's page draw
it: the grid's geometry (`TileGrid`), the sleeve and what lies over it
(`TileArt`), and the measures a tile's owner hit-tests its parts by, since
a scrolling grid's tile is one interactive element rather than one per
part.

#### `TileSleeve`

*enum* · The sleeve as a tile shows it.

```rust
pub enum TileSleeve {
    /// The picture, fading in by `arrival` over `ground`, the record's
    /// colour shown before it, or over the loading ensō where no colour
    /// stood in.
    Image {
        image: Arc<RenderImage>,
        arrival: Option<super::Arrival>,
        ground: Option<Hsla>,
    },
    /// The picture's average colour, standing in while it uploads.
    Colour(Hsla),
    /// On its way: the ensō drawing itself.
    Loading,
    /// No picture, or none to be had: the ensō at rest.
    Missing,
}
```

#### `TileMark`

*enum* · What dims the sleeve: playing it, under the pointer, or starting to.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum TileMark {
    Play,
    Starting,
}
```

#### `TileArt`

*struct* · The sleeve square and everything over it: the codec top right and the
release's badge top left, the play mark over a dim, the playing bars
bottom left, the share on this device as a bar along the foot, the heart
on its disc bottom right, and while picking the tick and the picked
outline. The sleeve keeps its place and size whatever lies over it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct TileArt { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(id: impl Into<ElementId>, side: Pixels, sleeve: TileSleeve) -> Self`

  `id` names which record's art this is, for its heart's answer: tiles
  drawn side by side in one view each need their own.

- `pub fn id(self, id: impl Into<ElementId>) -> Self`

  Which record's art this is, for its heart's answer: tiles drawn
  side by side in one view each need their own.

- `pub fn codec(self, codec: Option<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The codec, top right: at tile size a rate is unreadable, and the
  codec is what says whether this is the good copy.

- `pub fn badge(self, badge: Option<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The release's badge, top left ("deluxe").

- `pub fn mark(self, mark: Option<TileMark>) -> Self`

  A mark over the sleeve's corner, or none.

- `pub fn playing(self, bars: Option<AnyElement>) -> Self`

  The playing bars, bottom left, while the record plays.

- `pub fn on_device(self, fraction: Option<f32>) -> Self`

  How much of the record is on this device, 0 to 1, while that is
  worth showing: a bar along the foot.

- `pub fn heart(self, favourite: bool, on_disc: bool, shows: bool) -> Self`

  The heart: whether it is a favourite, whether the pointer is on the
  heart's disc, and whether it shows (under the pointer, or for a
  favourite). A heart not showing still follows the favourite, and
  shows while it answers a change made elsewhere.

- `pub fn pick(self, picked: Option<bool>) -> Self`

  While picking: whether this record is picked.

- `pub fn sheen(self, sheen: Option<(ElementId, bool)>) -> Self`

  The rainbow appearance's sheen over the sleeve, under the id `id`
  (brat's palette when `brat`): for the tile the pointer is on.

- `pub fn cursor(self, cursor: bool) -> Self`

  Under the grid's keyboard cursor while the grid holds the keyboard:
  the focus ring, inside the sleeve, once the keyboard was used last.

- `pub fn spot(self, spot: &super::CursorSpot) -> Self`

  Where the cursor's ring tells its page it was laid out, while this
  is under the cursor: what the page's scroll area follows and the menu
  key opens under.

- `pub fn on_heart(side: Pixels, x: f32, y: f32) -> bool`

  Whether `(x, y)`, from the sleeve's top left, is on the heart's disc.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::toolbar

The toolbar: its items placed leading, in the middle or trailing, as

### components::touch

What a touch screen does in place of a pointer's secondary button: a
long press. Only under touch (`Look::touch`); a desktop never draws it.

#### `KoanLongPress`

*trait* · A long press on any element, under touch: `pressed` runs with where the
touch went down, once the press has been held long enough to be one. Of
several elements listening under the touch, the one drawn last (on top)
takes it. The press is claimed, so it becomes neither a tap nor a scroll.

```rust
pub trait KoanLongPress: ParentElement + Sized {
    fn koan_long_press(
        self,
        look: &Look,
        pressed: impl Fn(Point<Pixels>, &mut Window, &mut App) + 'static,
    ) -> Self;
}
```

#### `KoanTouchDrag`

*trait* · Under touch, an element dragged by the pointer (a slider, a handle, a
divider) dragged by a finger: a touch that lands on it is its drag from
the start, never a scroll, and arrives as the pointer's press, moves with
the button held and release, so its pointer handling serves both.

```rust
pub trait KoanTouchDrag: ParentElement + Sized {
    fn koan_touch_drag(self, look: &Look) -> Self;
}
```

### components::track

Tracks: one segment of a moving value, which a shader program evaluates
at present time through `koan_track`, so the CPU writes it when the
value's target changes and never per frame. The same evaluation is here,
for reading where a track stands (a retarget starts from there) and for
knowing until when a shader moves.

A shader's uniform block (gpui's `ShaderUniforms`, `SHADER_UNIFORMS`
floats) is laid out by koan-kit as `SHADER_PLAIN` plain values, then
`SHADER_TRACKS` tracks of `TRACK_FLOATS` each.

#### `TrackCurve`

*enum* · How a track moves between its ends.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum TrackCurve {
    Linear,
    /// A CSS cubic Bézier timing function, `cubic-bezier(x1, y1, x2, y2)`.
    Bezier(f32, f32, f32, f32),
    /// A damped spring of unit mass, set off with `velocity` (units of the
    /// value per second). Its `length` is how long it takes to settle.
    Spring { stiffness: f32, damping: f32, velocity: f32 },
}
```

#### `spring_length`

*function* · How long a spring takes to come within a thousandth of the distance it
set off from (or of a ten-thousandth of a unit, for one set off from its
end): the first moment a bound on how far it can still be falls under
that, for each regime's own motion.

```rust
pub fn spring_length(distance: f32, velocity: f32, stiffness: f32, damping: f32) -> f32
```

#### `track_at`

for a program's `prepare`.

```rust
pub fn track_at(u: &[f32; gpui::SHADER_UNIFORMS], k: usize, time: f32) -> f32
```

### components::two_lines

### components::viewer

A picture as large as the window allows, over everything: the Mac's

## `koan_kit::components::chart`

A chart: axes, series, rules and points over a plot, drawn by the GPU
from keyframes, and interactive by pointer and by touch.

A page declares what to draw each time it renders: a `Chart` with an x
and a y `Axis` (linear or logarithmic, ticks chosen or given), `Series`
(lines, dashed or solid, and areas from a line to a level or to another
line), horizontal `Rule`s with an optional label, and `ChartPoint`s that
can be dragged within ranges. It compares what it is given with what it
drew and writes only what changed, as keyframes:

- **Data.** A series whose values change gets an image holding where it
  is drawn now and its new values (`data.rs`), and its phase track moves
  from one to the other: eased (`DataMotion::Ease`), interpolated over a
  sample interval and so drawn one sample behind (`DataMotion::Interpolate`),
  or drawn current and carried on along its last rate until the next
  sample corrects it (`DataMotion::Extrapolate`), or, for a series that
  takes one new sample at its right and drops its oldest, slid left by a
  sample over the interval as the newest enters from the right edge
  (`DataMotion::Scroll`). Samples arriving at their own rate are drawn at
  the display's.
- **Axes.** A range change eases each axis's affine map; every mark
  reads it as tracks, so the series, the grid, the points and the labels
  move together with no frame drawn.
- **Points.** A point moved by its owner settles where it was put; one
  being dragged stands under the pointer.

Only layout runs on the CPU: the ticks, which labels fit (`scale::thin`)
and the images, made again when the data, the plot's size or the range
changes. A chart at rest presents nothing, and under Reduce Motion every
change lands at once. Where the renderer draws no shaders the chart
paints its resting state as paths.

### components::chart::legend

A chart's key: each series' stroke, short, beside its name, wrapping onto
as many lines as the width needs.

#### `Swatch`

*struct* · How a key draws its series: a short line in the series' colour, dashed
or solid, a hairline for a rule.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Swatch { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn line(color: Hsla) -> Self`

  A solid line in `color`.

- `pub fn dashed(self, dashed: bool) -> Self`

  Dashed rather than solid.

- `pub fn thin(self) -> Self`

  A hairline, as a rule is drawn.

#### `Legend`

*struct* · The keys, then anything else said about the chart (a level, a unit), in
one wrapping line of fine, muted text.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement, Default)]
pub struct Legend { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new() -> Self`

  A legend with no keys yet.

- `pub fn key(self, name: impl Into<SharedString>, swatch: Swatch) -> Self`

  A key: `name` beside its swatch.

- `pub fn child(self, child: impl IntoElement) -> Self`

  Something more after the keys, such as a control.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

### components::chart::scale

Where a value stands on a chart's axis, the ticks worth marking on it,
and which of their labels fit. Plain arithmetic, so the rules are tested
without a window.

#### `Scale`

*enum* · How an axis spaces its values.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Scale {
    Linear,
    /// By their logarithm: each decade the same width. Values at or under
    /// zero stand at the axis's low end.
    Log,
}
```

- `pub fn transform(self, v: f64) -> f64`

  The value as the axis spaces it: itself, or its base-10 logarithm.

- `pub fn invert(self, t: f64) -> f64`

  A transformed value back on the axis's own scale.

#### `Map`

*struct* · An axis's range as an affine map from a transformed value to its share
of the plot, 0 at the range's low end and 1 at its high: `a * t + b`.
Easing `a` and `b` together moves every value on the axis along a
straight line between where the two ranges put it, which is what lets
a rescale be drawn by the GPU from keyframes alone.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Map {
    pub a: f64,
    pub b: f64,
}
```

- `pub fn new(scale: Scale, lo: f64, hi: f64) -> Self`

  The map from values between `lo` and `hi` on `scale` to shares of the
  plot.

- `pub fn unit(&self, t: f64) -> f64`

  A transformed value as a share of the plot, 0 at `lo` and 1 at `hi`.

- `pub fn at(&self, unit: f64) -> f64`

  The transformed value at share `unit` of the plot.

- `pub fn lerp(&self, to: &Map, e: f64) -> Map`

  Partway from `self` to `to`, `e` of the way.

- `pub fn framed(&self, lo: f64, hi: f64) -> (f64, f64)`

  The map for a value stored as its share `s` of `[lo, hi]`: the
  coefficients a series' program reads, so the GPU never subtracts
  two large numbers.

#### `Tick`

*struct* · A place worth marking on an axis, and how much its label matters: rank 0
is kept first when labels are thinned.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Tick {
    pub value: f64,
    pub rank: u8,
}
```

## `koan_kit::components::chart::scale`

Where a value stands on a chart's axis, the ticks worth marking on it,
and which of their labels fit. Plain arithmetic, so the rules are tested
without a window.

#### `nice_step`

*function* · The step of about `target` round intervals across `span`: 1, 2 or 5
times a power of ten.

```rust
pub fn nice_step(span: f64, target: usize) -> f64
```

#### `every`

*function* · Every multiple of `step` from `lo` to `hi`; those that are multiples of
twice the step rank first, so thinning keeps every other one.

```rust
pub fn every(lo: f64, hi: f64, step: f64) -> Vec<Tick>
```

#### `linear`

*function* · About `target` round ticks across a linear range.

```rust
pub fn linear(lo: f64, hi: f64, target: usize) -> Vec<Tick>
```

#### `log`

*function* · The ticks of a logarithmic range: 1, 2 and 5 of each decade, the
decades ranked first, then the 2s, then the 5s.

```rust
pub fn log(lo: f64, hi: f64) -> Vec<Tick>
```

#### `thin`

*function* · Which labels to draw, given each label's extent along the axis and its
rank: taken by rank, then in order along the axis, each kept unless it
would come within `gap` of one already kept. Labels thin, never collide.

```rust
pub fn thin(spans: &[(f32, f32)], ranks: &[u8], gap: f32) -> Vec<bool>
```

#### `nearest`

*function* · The nearest of `points` to `at` within `reach`, by index.

```rust
pub fn nearest(points: &[(f32, f32)], at: (f32, f32), reach: f32) -> Option<usize>
```

## `koan_kit::components::choice`

A choice made from a kōan menu panel: a control showing what is chosen,
which opens the choices under it, with headings and rules between them
and destructive actions among them. The open state is kept by the window
under the control's id, so the view holding it needs no field. Escape, a
press outside or a choice closes it.

#### `Run`

*type alias* · What choosing an entry does.

```rust
pub type Run = Rc<dyn Fn(&mut Window, &mut App)>;
```

#### `Item`

*enum* · One entry of a menu.

```rust
pub enum Item {
    /// A choice: its label (data, which keeps its case), whether it is in
    /// force, and what choosing it does.
    Choice { label: SharedString, checked: Option<bool>, destructive: bool, run: Run },
    /// A heading over the choices after it.
    Heading(SharedString),
    Rule,
}
```

- `pub fn choice(label: impl Into<SharedString>, checked: bool, run: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  A choice, ticked while `checked`, that runs `run`.

- `pub fn action(label: impl Into<SharedString>, run: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  An action that runs `run`, with no tick.

- `pub fn destructive(label: impl Into<SharedString>, run: impl Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) + 'static) -> Self`

  An action in `bad` that destroys something.

#### `Trigger`

*enum* · How the control looks: a field showing the choice, or a quiet word.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Trigger {
    Field,
    Word,
}
```

#### `menu`

*function* · A control showing `shown` that opens `items` under it. The open state is
kept under `id` by the window, so the menu needs no field in its view.

```rust
pub fn menu(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    shown: impl Into<SharedString>,
    trigger: Trigger,
    items: Vec<Item>,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> AnyElement
```

## `koan_kit::components::field`

kōan's text fields: `TextField` for a line, `TextArea` for several,
both drawing a `FieldState` the page owns.

The editing is the platform's: an input method composes in place
(GPUI's `EntityInputHandler`), the selection takes clicks, drags, Shift
with the arrows and double and triple clicks, words and lines move as
the Mac's fields move them, the clipboard and undo work, a line too long
for the field scrolls to keep the caret in view, and a screen reader
hears a text field with its value and selection. The look is kōan's:
the type and ground of a `control`, no bezel and no focus ring (the
caret says where typing goes), the selection the accent at 30 %, the
placeholder in `muted`, and the caret a shader quad whose blink draws
nothing.

### components::field::actions

What a field's keys do, bound in the `Input` context (which koan's
window-wide shortcuts step aside for while a field has the keyboard).

#### `CONTEXT`

*constant* · The context every field's keys are bound in.

```rust
pub const CONTEXT: &str = "Input";
```

#### `bind`

*function* · Binds a field's keys, as the platform's text fields take them.

```rust
pub fn bind(cx: &mut App)
```

### components::field::state

What a field edits: its text, its selection and what an input method is
composing, the history its edits leave, and the caret's blink. The page
owns it (`cx.new(|cx| FieldState::new(window, cx))`) and listens to its
`InputEvent`s; `TextField` and `TextArea` draw it.

#### `InputEvent`

*enum* · What a field says to its owner.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum InputEvent {
    /// The text changed by the person's hand (typing, a paste, an undo, an
    /// input method). `set_value` changes it without saying so.
    Change,
    /// Return pressed: with Command (Control off the Mac) as `secondary`,
    /// with Shift as `shift`. A multi-line field takes a plain Return as a
    /// new line and says only the others.
    PressEnter { secondary: bool, shift: bool },
    /// The field took the keyboard.
    Focus,
    /// The field let the keyboard go.
    Blur,
}
```

#### `FieldState`

*struct* · A text field's state, kept by its owner: the text, the caret and
selection, the history, focus and how the field takes input.

```rust
pub struct FieldState { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>) -> Self`

  An empty field that takes the keyboard on Tab.

- `pub fn placeholder(self, placeholder: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  What it shows while empty, in `muted`.

- `pub fn default_value(self, value: impl Into<SharedString>) -> Self`

  The text it starts with.

- `pub fn number(self) -> Self`

  A number, which may be negative or fractional: a software keyboard
  shows figures and the minus sign.

- `pub fn content(self, content: gpui::TextContentType) -> Self`

  What it holds, for the platform to offer saved values: a username or
  a password.

- `pub fn set_content(&mut self, content: gpui::TextContentType)`

  Changes what it holds.

- `pub fn masked(self, masked: bool) -> Self`

  Its text drawn as bullets, never copied or read aloud: a password.
  A software keyboard treats it as secure.

- `pub fn read_only(self, read_only: bool) -> Self`

  Its text selectable and copyable, but not to be changed.

- `pub fn set_read_only(&mut self, read_only: bool, cx: &mut Context<Self>)`

  Its text can be selected and copied but not changed.

- `pub fn highlight(self, spans: impl Fn(&str) -> Vec<(Range<usize>, crate::theme::Tone)> + 'static) -> Self`

  Colours its text as `spans` says: byte ranges of the text with
  their tones, worked out again whenever the text changes, however it
  changes. A code field's syntax highlighting, which wraps the field
  rather than forking it.

- `pub fn rows(self, rows: usize) -> Self`

  `rows` lines tall, taking Return as a new line: a `TextArea`'s.

- `pub fn value(&self) -> SharedString`

  The text as it stands.

- `pub fn set_value(&mut self, value: impl Into<SharedString>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>)`

  Sets the text from the owner, the caret at its end. Says no
  `Change`, and starts the history afresh: the person's edits are what
  undo walks back.

- `pub fn clean(&mut self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>)`

  Empties the field as the person's edit (a clear mark, Escape in a
  filter): says `Change`, and can be undone.

- `pub fn clear(&mut self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>)`

  Empties the field as a clear mark does: what it held fades out
  leftward over `Motion::SETTLE` from where it stood, and the
  placeholder shows once it has gone, or as soon as the field is edited
  again. Several lines, and Reduce Motion, empty at once, as `clean`
  does; a read-only field keeps its text. For an owner's key that
  clears, as Escape does.

- `pub fn focus(&self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)`

  Gives the field the keyboard.

Implements `EntityInputHandler`, `EventEmitter`, `Focusable`.

## `koan_kit::components::focus_trap`

Tab and Shift-Tab kept inside a sheet, a question or a menu while it
holds the keyboard: the window's root (`Root`) and `sheet::step_focus`
move the focus through the window's order and, while a trap holds it,
past every stop outside the trap, wrapping inside it.

#### `FocusTrap`

*trait* · Keeping Tab and Shift-Tab inside an element, for a sheet or menu that
holds the keyboard.

```rust
pub trait FocusTrap: InteractiveElement + Sized {
    /// This element takes `focus` and keeps Tab and Shift-Tab among its
    /// own controls while the focus is inside it.
    fn focus_trap(self, focus: &FocusHandle, cx: &mut App) -> Self;
}
```

#### `active`

*function* · The innermost trap holding the focus, if any does.

```rust
pub fn active(window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Option<FocusHandle>
```

#### `step`

*function* · The next (or previous) control in the window's order, kept inside the
innermost trap holding the keyboard. GPUI walks the whole window's tab
order, so stops outside the trap are passed over, at most one cycle of
them; a trap with no stop of its own keeps the focus itself.

```rust
pub fn step(forward: bool, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

## `koan_kit::components::sheet`

A sheet: the square panel over the window, its head with a title and the
actions trailing, its scrolling body; and the one place a question is
asked before a write (`confirm`, `name`), drawn over the whole window by
`overlay`. A menu that asks is gone the moment it is used, so the
question lives here instead.

#### `TALLEST`

*constant* · The most of the window's height a sheet's panel takes on the desktop.

```rust
pub const TALLEST: f32 = 0.8;
```

#### `panel`

*function* · A sheet's panel: square, `bg`, a hairline `rule`, 560 wide. A dialog to
a screen reader, named `name` in the app's words: what its head says.
As tall as its content up to `TALLEST` of the window (on a phone, the
`room` it has), where its
`body` scrolls; a view drawn in it makes `content` its root, or its body
cannot shrink.

```rust
pub fn panel(name: impl AsRef<str>, look: &Look) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `trap_focus`

*function* · Keeps Tab and Shift-Tab among a sheet's own controls while it is up: a
sheet drawn by its owner rather than presented through `present`.

```rust
pub fn trap_focus(
    panel: Stateful<Div>,
    focus: &FocusHandle,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `step_focus`

*function* · Tab and Shift-Tab: the next control in the window's order, kept inside
the innermost sheet, question or menu holding the keyboard, as the
window's root keeps it. For an app that binds Tab in a context of its
own, which the root's binding never reaches.

```rust
pub fn step_focus(forward: bool, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `screen`

*function* · A sheet that is a screen on a phone (a list to choose from, a flow of
steps, an editor): the whole window, standing on the window's wash as Now
Playing does rather than on an opaque ground, its content inside the safe
area and clear of the keyboard, coming up from the foot. On the desktop,
`panel` over a `scrim` as any sheet. `content` is rooted in `content()`.

```rust
pub fn screen(
    id: impl Into<ElementId>,
    name: impl AsRef<str>,
    content: impl IntoElement,
    look: &Look,
    window: &Window,
    cx: &App,
) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `room`

*function* · The height a sheet's panel may take on a phone: the window less the
system's insets and the keyboard, a step off each edge.

```rust
pub fn room(look: &Look, window: &Window) -> Pixels
```

#### `content`

*function* · The root of a view drawn in a panel: a column that shrinks to the
panel's height, so its `body` scrolls, and grows to fill a `screen`.

```rust
pub fn content() -> Div
```

#### `wide_panel`

*function* · A wide sheet's panel, 880 wide and as tall as it may be: one whose body
ends in a table of paths that fills the rest, as organise's preview does.

```rust
pub fn wide_panel(name: impl AsRef<str>, look: &Look) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `tall_panel`

*function* · A sheet's panel as tall as it may be, whatever its content: one holding
a long list to choose from. The whole room on a phone (`room`),
`TALLEST` of the window on the desktop.

```rust
pub fn tall_panel(name: impl AsRef<str>, look: &Look, window: &Window) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `head`

*function* · A sheet's head: its title in the app's words, and its actions trailing.

```rust
pub fn head(
    title: impl AsRef<str>,
    actions: Vec<AnyElement>,
    look: &Look,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `head_data`

*function* · A sheet's head whose title is data, a profile's name, which keeps its
case.

```rust
pub fn head_data(
    title: impl Into<SharedString>,
    actions: Vec<AnyElement>,
    look: &Look,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `body`

*function* · A sheet's scrolling body.

```rust
pub fn body(id: impl Into<ElementId>, look: &Look) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `scrim`

*function* · What a sheet sits on: the whole window dimmed, taking every click, the
panel in its middle. The owner listens to it for a click outside the
panel, which the panel stops. The dim fades in as it comes up; the owner
draws its panel in a `Rise`.

On a phone the panel stands on the foot of the window instead, the
width less a margin, inside the safe area on every edge and clear of the
keyboard while it is up, as an iOS sheet does; its `Rise` brings it up
from there.

```rust
pub fn scrim(id: impl Into<ElementId>, look: &Look, window: &Window) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `scrim_top`

*function* · A scrim with the panel hung near the top on every platform: the search
box's, whose results run down toward the keyboard.

```rust
pub fn scrim_top(id: impl Into<ElementId>, look: &Look) -> Stateful<Div>
```

#### `band`

*function* · A band under the head or over the foot: a sheet's segmented choice.

```rust
pub fn band(content: impl IntoElement, look: &Look) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `foot`

*function* · A sheet's foot: what it says of its state leading, in `fine`, `muted`,
and its verbs trailing.

```rust
pub fn foot(
    leading: Vec<AnyElement>,
    actions: Vec<AnyElement>,
    look: &Look,
) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `note`

*function* · A line of `fine` text in a tone, for footers and notes.

```rust
pub fn note(text: impl Into<SharedString>, tone: Tone, look: &Look) -> Div
```

#### `Asking`

*struct* · The one place a question is asked, over the whole window: a menu that
asks is gone the moment it is used, so the question cannot live there.

```rust
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Asking { /* private fields */ }
```

#### `present`

*function* · Shows `view` as the window's sheet, over the shell and under the
question host, replacing any sheet showing. The view draws its own
`panel`; the host draws the scrim and the placement, and holds the
keyboard while it shows, as a question does.

```rust
pub fn present(view: impl Into<AnyView>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `hold`

*function* · While `held`, the sheet presented is busy with something it must see
through, such as files on the move: Escape leaves it showing, as its
disabled cancel does. Closing it lets go.

```rust
pub fn hold(held: bool, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `close`

*function* · Takes the sheet away. The keyboard goes back to what had it before, on
the window's next draw.

```rust
pub fn close(cx: &mut App)
```

#### `Layer`

*enum* · Where an overlay stands among the window's layers, lowest first. Every
layer is drawn after the window's content and the playing bars painted
over it, so nothing on a page shows through a panel or a sheet.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Layer {
    /// A panel over a page: a track's details.
    Panel = 1,
    /// A sheet over the window: organise, the picker, the shortcuts, the
    /// artwork viewer.
    Sheet = 2,
    /// A question asked over everything, sheets included.
    Question = 3,
}
```

#### `MODAL`

*constant* · The key context of everything drawn over the window: the app binds its
shortcuts outside it, so none acts behind a sheet or a question.

```rust
pub const MODAL: &str = "Modal";
```

#### `layered`

*function* · `element` drawn on `layer`, covering its parent: the parent lays it out,
and it is painted once the window's content is.

It holds the keyboard as a modal: its keys are `MODAL`'s, and a ⌘ or Ctrl
key nothing inside bound is taken here, so the macOS menu bar's key
equivalent behind it does not act either. Keys that type are left alone.

```rust
pub fn layered(layer: Layer, element: impl IntoElement) -> impl IntoElement
```

#### `presented`

*function* · The sheet presented, placed over the window, for the shell to draw under
`overlay`.

```rust
pub fn presented(window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> Option<AnyElement>
```

#### `asking`

*function* · The window's question host, made the first time it is wanted.

```rust
pub fn asking(cx: &mut App) -> Entity<Asking>
```

## `koan_kit::theme`

The kōan theme for GPUI: tokens, Geist Mono, the icon set, the record
accent and the wash. docs/design/koan-theme.md is the specification; there
is no other look, so nothing here has a "system" answer.

What every window shares (the text size, "show icons") is the global
`KoanTheme`; the record's accent is an entity, `AccentFeed`, since it
changes with every record. What a window looks like is
`Look::of(window, cx)`: its appearance, dark or light, and the tokens and
accent for it. A component reads the look as it renders. A change of
appearance or text size redraws every window past its caches, which
`follow_appearance` and the setters here do; a change of accent notifies
only the views that observe the feed (`observe_accent`), so a cached view
that draws the accent calls it when it is made.

Re-exported from `gpui`: `Hsla`.


### theme::accent

The record accent: the sleeve's hue, its lightness and chroma moved into a
band per appearance in OKLCH, so a dark or muddy sleeve gives a clean,
bright version of its hue and light mode never goes pastel. Mint when
there is no record or no usable hue.

`Color.dominant` (apps/ios/Sources/Koan/Support/Palette.swift) and
`KoanAccent` (Support/KoanTheme.swift), value for value, as the Android
app ports them, so a record gives the same accent on every device.

#### `Shade`

*struct* · One appearance's accent, as gamma-encoded sRGB in 0..=1. `reads_as_text`
is whether it reaches 4.5:1 on `bg` and `surface`; where it does not, it
is used for fills, indicators and rings only, and text stays `ink`.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Shade {
    pub rgb: [f64; 3],
    pub reads_as_text: bool,
    /// The colour slots it is drawn through, as fills and as text, while the
    /// accent moves on the GPU: what `hsla` and `text_hsla` name. `rgb` is
    /// where the slots are headed.
    pub slot: Option<[Hsla; 2]>,
}
```

- `pub fn rgba(self) -> Rgba`

  The shade as GPUI's colour.

- `pub fn hsla(self) -> Hsla`

  The colour to draw fills, rings and indicators in: the accent's slot
  when it has one, so a new record's accent settles with no frame drawn.

- `pub fn text_hsla(self, ink: Hsla) -> Hsla`

  The colour to draw text in: the accent where it reads as text, `ink`
  where it does not.

- `pub fn held(self, minimum: f64, dark: bool, tokens: Palette) -> Shade`

  With more contrast asked for: the same hue, its lightness moved away
  from the ground (lighter in dark mode, darker in light) until it
  reaches `minimum` on `bg` and `surface`. Worked out once per shade.

#### `Accent`

*struct* · The accent in each appearance.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Accent {
    pub dark: Shade,
    pub light: Shade,
}
```

- `pub fn shade(&self, dark: bool) -> Shade`

  The shade for the dark or the light appearance.

- `pub fn of(record: Option<[f64; 3]>) -> Accent`

  The accent for a record's colour, as `dominant` gives it; `None` is no
  record, or one with no colour. Worked out once per colour, so the same
  record always gives the same value.

- `pub fn of_sleeve(rgba: &[u8], width: u32, height: u32) -> Accent`

  The accent for a sleeve, as RGBA pixels.

### theme::icons

The kōan icon set, drawn through GPUI's SVG sprites.

`glyphs` is compiled by `just icons` from the SVGs the apps draw
(`apps/ios/Resources/Icons`). The stroke is not scaled with the drawing:
its width is set by the size of the type beside the glyph, so each glyph is
rasterised per stroke width. Every (glyph, width, layer) is a path served
by `Assets`, which writes the SVG on first use; GPUI keeps the rendered
sprite in its atlas by path and size, so a frame draws a cached sprite and
allocates nothing.

#### `KoanIcon`

*struct* · A glyph of the set, in the colour of the text beside it.

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct KoanIcon { /* private fields */ }
```

- `pub fn new(icon: Icon) -> Self`

  Sized for `body` type, in `ink`.

- `pub fn hover_color(self, group: impl Into<SharedString>, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  The colour it takes while the pointer is over the group named, as the
  label of a button does: a glyph is drawn in a colour of its own, and
  does not follow its parent's.

- `pub fn role(self, role: TypeRole) -> Self`

  Sized beside type of this role.

- `pub fn points(self, points: f32) -> Self`

  Sized beside type of this many points, as drawn.

- `pub fn tone(self, tone: Tone) -> Self`

  In `tone` rather than `ink`.

- `pub fn color(self, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  In `color`, for a glyph drawn on a ground of its own.

- `pub fn fill(self, color: Hsla) -> Self`

  Draws a fill layer in its own colour, with the strokes over it rather
  than cut out of it, as the album grid's tick is.

Implements `RenderOnce`.

#### `Tone`

*enum* · The colours text and glyphs take.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Tone {
    Ink,
    Strong,
    Muted,
    Accent,
    Bad,
}
```

#### `KoanTheme`

*struct* · The theme global: what the person chose (text size, icons, the wash,
touch), and the system's display settings as read.

```rust
pub struct KoanTheme {
    /// The text size, in percent (80 to 130 in steps of 5).
    pub text_size: u32,
    /// "Show icons": glyphs beside the labels that carry them.
    pub icons: bool,
    /// The wash and the accent take their colour from the record playing;
    /// off, there is no wash and the accent is mint.
    pub record_colours: bool,
    /// A wash painted from something other than the record (the rainbow's
    /// sheen), which runs whatever "colours from the record" says.
    pub painted_wash: bool,
    /// "Theme tint", in percent: how far the wash is held to its contrast
    /// limit.
    pub wash_tint: u32,
    /// How fast the wash drifts, in percent of its own pace: 0 holds it
    /// still.
    pub wash_drift: u32,
    /// How far the wash turns either way as it drifts, in degrees.
    pub wash_turn: u32,
    /// "Wash the whole window": the wash runs under the sidebar and the
    /// toolbar as well as the page, and they draw no ground of their own.
    pub wash_window: bool,
    /// Touch, not a pointer, is how the app is used: buttons, rows, menu
    /// rows, toggles and segmented controls are at least `HIT` tall, a
    /// finger's target, and a held touch opens context menus. On by default
    /// on phones and tablets; the storybook switches it to preview the look
    /// on a desktop.
    pub touch: bool,
    /// The hidden rainbow appearance, while it is drawn, and whether in
    /// brat's palette: what may carry the palette as a gradient (a progress
    /// fill, the chosen tab) does.
    pub prism: Option<bool>,
    /* private fields */
}
```

- `pub fn scale(&self) -> Scale`

  The text size chosen in the app, on top of the system's.

- `pub fn opaque(cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether the system asks for less transparency: the wash stays off
  and every ground is opaque.

- `pub fn motion_allowed(cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether the system lets things move for their own sake: Reduce
  Motion and Low Power Mode off, as the system last said.

- `pub fn set_accent(accent: Accent, ease: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  Moves the accent to a record's. `ease` is false for a colour already
  known when the record arrived, which lands with it; with Reduce Motion
  every change lands.

- `pub fn set_text_size(percent: u32, cx: &mut App)`

  The text size in percent, held to the sizes offered, every window
  drawn again.

- `pub fn set_prism(prism: Option<bool>, cx: &mut App)`

  The rainbow appearance drawn, in brat's palette or not, or put away.
  The one place it is kept: the windows' roots observe the theme for it.

- `pub fn prism(cx: &App) -> Option<bool>`

  Whether the rainbow is drawn, and in brat's palette.

- `pub fn prism_now() -> Option<bool>`

  The same, for elements built where the app is not to hand, such as
  the playing bars inside a row: they are drawn again whenever it
  changes, since it refreshes the windows.

- `pub fn prism_gradient(cx: &App) -> Option<bool>`

  The palette's gradient, where a component may draw one: the rainbow
  is drawn, shaders are, and motion is allowed.

- `pub fn set_touch(touch: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  Whether touch is how the app is used, every window drawn again.

- `pub fn set_icons(icons: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  Whether glyphs show beside the labels that carry them, every window
  drawn again.

- `pub fn set_record_colours(on: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  Whoever draws the wash observes the global for these two. The
  record's colours also decide whether the wash runs under the whole
  window (`washes_window`), whose layout every window then redraws.

- `pub fn set_painted_wash(on: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  A wash is painted, or no longer, from something other than the
  record: the rainbow's sheen runs with "colours from the record" off.

- `pub fn washes_window(cx: &App) -> bool`

  Whether the wash runs under the whole window: "wash the whole
  window" on, and a wash to run: the record's, which "colours from the
  record" off takes away, or one painted from elsewhere.

- `pub fn set_wash_tint(percent: u32, cx: &mut App)`

  How far the wash is held to its contrast limit, in percent.

- `pub fn set_wash_drift(percent: u32, degrees: u32, cx: &mut App)`

  The wash's drift: its pace in percent (0 still, up to 200) and how far
  it turns either way, in degrees (up to 10). The wash reads them when it
  is next drawn: whoever calls this draws the windows' washes again, as
  koan-gui's roots do when the pair changes.

- `pub fn set_wash_window(on: bool, cx: &mut App)`

  Moves the window's grounds, which sit in cached views: every window
  is drawn again.

Implements `Default`, `Global`.

#### `init`

*function* · Registers Geist Mono, sets the theme global, reads the system's motion,
contrast, transparency and text size settings (outside tests) and
dresses gpui-component's own theme (which the text field draws with) in
the tokens. Call after `gpui::init`.

```rust
pub fn init(cx: &mut App)
```

#### `follow_appearance`

*function* · Keeps a window drawn in its appearance: redrawn when the system's
changes. Hold the returned subscription as long as the window.

```rust
pub fn follow_appearance<V: 'static>(
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut Context<V>,
) -> Subscription
```

#### `pin_appearance`

*function* · Holds a window to one appearance whatever the system's, or lets it follow
the system again with `None`.

```rust
pub fn pin_appearance(dark: Option<bool>, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App)
```

#### `AccentFeed`

*struct* · The record's accent as it settles. An entity rather than part of the
global so that a change reaches the views that draw it, not every window.

```rust
pub struct AccentFeed { /* private fields */ }
```

#### `observe_accent`

*function* · Redraws a view when the accent changes in a way the colour slots cannot
carry: where no slots could be claimed, each record's accent. A cached
view that draws the accent holds the subscription for as long as it
lives; an uncached one redraws with its parent and needs none.

```rust
pub fn observe_accent<V: 'static>(cx: &mut Context<V>) -> Subscription
```

#### `observe_accent_when`

*function* · `observe_accent`, drawing the view again only while `draws` says it
shows the accent: a tile, which draws it only while its record plays.

```rust
pub fn observe_accent_when<V: 'static>(
    cx: &mut Context<V>,
    draws: impl Fn(&V) -> bool + 'static,
) -> Subscription
```

#### `observe_accent_then`

*function* · `observe_accent`, running `then` as the accent moves: for a view whose
cached children draw it too and are not views of their own making.

```rust
pub fn observe_accent_then<V: 'static>(
    cx: &mut Context<V>,
    then: impl Fn(&mut V, &mut Context<V>) + 'static,
) -> Subscription
```

#### `Look`

*struct* · A window's look: its appearance and everything drawn from it.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Look {
    pub dark: bool,
    /// More contrast asked for: the palette is the contrast variant, the
    /// accent is held to 7:1, and a picked row is outlined as well as filled.
    pub contrast: bool,
    pub palette: Palette,
    pub accent: Shade,
    pub scale: Scale,
    pub icons: bool,
    /// A rule's width: `HAIRLINE`, twice that with more contrast.
    pub hairline: Pixels,
    /// Less transparency asked for: veils over the page are near opaque.
    pub opaque: bool,
    /// Used by touch (`KoanTheme::touch`).
    pub touch: bool,
    /// Under touch, a window narrower than `COMPACT_WIDTH`: a phone, or a
    /// tablet's window in Split View or Stage Manager with a phone's room.
    /// Layout follows this; what touch asks of a control follows `touch`.
    pub compact: bool,
}
```

- `pub fn of(window: &Window, cx: &App) -> Look`

  The look for `window`: its appearance, the text size, the accent and
  the system's settings.

- `pub fn target(&self) -> Pixels`

  The least height of anything pressed: `HIT` under touch, nothing
  asked of a pointer.

- `pub fn color(&self, tone: Tone) -> Hsla`

  The colour `tone` takes in this look.

- `pub fn text_selection(&self) -> Hsla`

  Selected text's ground: the accent at 30 %, at 45 % with more
  contrast asked for.

- `pub fn text_color(&self, tone: Tone) -> Hsla`

  A tone for text: the accent stays off text it cannot reach 4.5:1 as,
  which reads in `ink` instead.

- `pub fn veil(&self, alpha: f32) -> Hsla`

  The ground drawn over the page behind a sheet or the artwork viewer:
  `alpha` of `bg`, all but opaque with less transparency asked for.

- `pub fn space(&self, space: Space) -> Pixels`

  A step of the spacing scale, at the text size.

- `pub fn spaced(&self, points: f32) -> Pixels`

  A length on spacing's scale for a padding the spec gives in points
  that are not steps of the scale (a prominent button's 12 × 20).

- `pub fn sized(&self, points: f32) -> Pixels`

  A width that holds text, in points at 100 %, grown with the text
  size rather than the spacing.

- `pub fn measure(&self, measure: Measure) -> Pixels`

  The widest a column of content runs (`Measure`).

- `pub fn font_size(&self, role: TypeRole) -> Pixels`

  A type role's size, at the text size.

- `pub fn line_height(&self, role: TypeRole) -> Pixels`

  A type role's line height, at the text size.

- `pub fn points(&self, role: TypeRole) -> f32`

  A role's size in points as drawn, which the icon set is sized beside.

#### `KoanStyled`

*trait* · Type roles on any element.

```rust
pub trait KoanStyled: Styled + Sized {
    fn text_role(self, role: TypeRole, look: &Look) -> Self;
}
```

#### `label`

*function* · An app word as the theme writes it: lowercase. Text from the library and
from people keeps its own case and does not come through here.

```rust
pub fn label(text: impl AsRef<str>) -> SharedString
```

### theme::tokens

The kōan theme's tokens: colour, type, spacing and motion, as
docs/design/koan-theme.md sets them out.

#### `Palette`

*struct* · The colour tokens of one appearance. The accent is not here: it follows
the record playing (`theme::accent`).

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Palette {
    pub bg: Hex,
    pub surface: Hex,
    pub rule: Veil,
    pub hover: Hex,
    pub ink: Hex,
    pub strong: Hex,
    pub muted: Hex,
    pub bad: Hex,
    /// A picked list row's ground: the Mac's tables' selection, as the
    /// person's graphite accent draws it, letting the wash through.
    pub selection: Veil,
    /// Text and marks on `selection`.
    pub on_selection: Hex,
}
```

- `pub fn of(dark: bool) -> Palette`

  The palette for the dark or the light appearance.

- `pub fn for_look(dark: bool, contrast: bool) -> Palette`

  The tokens for an appearance, in their contrast variant when the
  system asks for more.

#### `ROW_RULE_OPACITY`

*constant* · The opacity of `ink` in the faint hairline that may mark where one region
of a list runs into the next (`koanRowRule`).

```rust
pub const ROW_RULE_OPACITY: f32 = 0.12;
```

#### `ROW_HOVER_OPACITY`

*constant* · The opacity of `hover` behind a list row under the pointer.

```rust
pub const ROW_HOVER_OPACITY: f32 = 0.3;
```

#### `DISABLED_OPACITY`

*constant* · The opacity of a disabled control's label and outline.

```rust
pub const DISABLED_OPACITY: f32 = 0.4;
```

#### `HAIRLINE`

*constant* · One rule's width: a point, not a device pixel, which at `rule`'s contrast
is too faint on a 2× display. Twice that with more contrast asked for
(`Look::hairline`).

```rust
pub const HAIRLINE: Pixels = px(1.0);
```

#### `MARK`

*constant* · The width of the accent rule on a selected navigation row's leading edge,
and of the focus ring.

```rust
pub const MARK: Pixels = px(2.0);
```

#### `HIT`

*constant* · The smallest a control's hit area is in either direction.

```rust
pub const HIT: Pixels = px(44.0);
```

#### `COMPACT_WIDTH`

*constant* · Under touch, the width below which a window is laid out as a phone is
(`Look::compact`): a phone in either hand, and a tablet's window in Split
View or Stage Manager once a sidebar would leave its page too little.

```rust
pub const COMPACT_WIDTH: Pixels = px(660.0);
```

#### `Measure`

*enum* · The widest a column of content runs, however wide the window: past it a
line is too long to read and a page's parts drift apart. `Look::measure`
gives it on the window's scale.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Measure {
    /// Prose: a biography, a long note. 680 points on the spacing scale.
    Prose,
    /// A page of controls and their notes, the EQ page's: 720 points on the
    /// size scale, so it grows with the text it holds.
    Page,
}
```

#### `TOOLBAR_BUTTON`

*constant* · A toolbar button's cell, the size of the platform's own.

```rust
pub const TOOLBAR_BUTTON: Pixels = px(36.0);
```

#### `Scale`

*struct* · The scales the text size gives: type at `MAC_FIT` times the size chosen,
spacing at half the type's change, rows at the size alone.

```rust
pub struct Scale {
    pub text: f32,
    pub space: f32,
    pub size: f32,
}
```

- `pub fn of(text_size: u32) -> Scale`

  The scales for a text size in percent, with the system's at 1.

- `pub fn at(text_size: u32, system: f32) -> Scale`

  The app's text size on top of the system's (GNOME's text scaling
  factor; 1 where the system has none): 100 % is the system's size.

#### `Space`

```rust
    Xxl,

```

## `koan_kit::theme::accent`

The record accent: the sleeve's hue, its lightness and chroma moved into a
band per appearance in OKLCH, so a dark or muddy sleeve gives a clean,
bright version of its hue and light mode never goes pastel. Mint when
there is no record or no usable hue.

`Color.dominant` (apps/ios/Sources/Koan/Support/Palette.swift) and
`KoanAccent` (Support/KoanTheme.swift), value for value, as the Android
app ports them, so a record gives the same accent on every device.

#### `dominant`

*function* · The colour a record reads as. Not the average of the sleeve, which comes
out the same brown-grey for every busy cover as opposite hues cancel: a
circular mean of hue weighted by each sample's chroma, so the one strong
colour on a mostly black cover wins. Samples are gated on chroma rather
than brightness, since thin strokes on black come out of the downscale
dark but still coloured.

The sleeve is first reduced to 32 pixels square, as the Apple apps'
thumbnail is, with its colour premultiplied by its alpha: Swift draws the
thumbnail into a premultiplied context, so a transparent part of a sleeve
reads as black and falls under the black floor.

```rust
pub fn dominant(rgba: &[u8], width: u32, height: u32) -> Option<[f64; 3]>
```

## `koan_kit::theme::accent::oklch`

OKLab in polar form: what the accent is tone-mapped in, because its
lightness is perceptual and its hue holds still while lightness moves.

#### `from_srgb`

*function* · Lightness, chroma and hue in degrees, from gamma-encoded sRGB in 0..=1.

```rust
pub fn from_srgb([r, g, b]: [f64; 3]) -> (f64, f64, f64)
```

#### `lab`

*function* · OKLab from linear sRGB.

```rust
pub fn lab([r, g, b]: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3]
```

#### `linear_from_lab`

*function* · Linear sRGB from OKLab.

```rust
pub fn linear_from_lab([l, a, b]: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3]
```

#### `to_srgb`

*function* · sRGB, with chroma given up until the colour is inside the gamut.

```rust
pub fn to_srgb(l: f64, c: f64, h: f64) -> [f64; 3]
```

#### `luminance`

*function* · Relative luminance, as WCAG's contrast ratio takes it, from
gamma-encoded sRGB in 0..=1.

```rust
pub fn luminance([r, g, b]: [f64; 3]) -> f64
```

#### `linear`

*function* · One sRGB channel, gamma-encoded, as linear light.

```rust
pub fn linear(v: f64) -> f64
```

#### `gamma`

*function* · One channel of linear light, gamma-encoded as sRGB, held to 0..=1.

```rust
pub fn gamma(v: f64) -> f64
```

## `koan_kit::theme::icons`

The kōan icon set, drawn through GPUI's SVG sprites.

`glyphs` is compiled by `just icons` from the SVGs the apps draw
(`apps/ios/Resources/Icons`). The stroke is not scaled with the drawing:
its width is set by the size of the type beside the glyph, so each glyph is
rasterised per stroke width. Every (glyph, width, layer) is a path served
by `Assets`, which writes the SVG on first use; GPUI keeps the rendered
sprite in its atlas by path and size, so a frame draws a cached sprite and
allocates nothing.

#### `Glyph`

*struct* · One glyph: its stroked outline, and the outline of its fill layer (the
heart of a favourite, the box of a selection) where it has one, as
absolute M/L/C/Z commands on a 24-unit grid.

```rust
pub struct Glyph {
    pub name: &'static str,
    pub strokes: &'static str,
    pub fills: &'static str,
}
```

#### `side`

*function* · The square a glyph is drawn in beside type of `points`: the optical size
of an SF Symbol at that size.

```rust
pub fn side(points: f32) -> f32
```

#### `stroke_width`

*function* · The stroke, in points, beside type of `points`: 1.2 at 15, 1.4 at 20,
never under a point.

```rust
pub fn stroke_width(points: f32) -> f32
```

#### `Assets`

*struct* · The app's assets: the icon set's sprites and the ensō, drawn from code.

```rust
pub struct Assets;
```

Implements `AssetSource`.

17 points, and 12.9 reads as fine print.

```rust
pub const MAC_FIT: f32 = if cfg!(any(target_os = "ios", target_os = "android")) {
    1.0
} else {
    0.86
};
```
